An asymptotic formula of spectral average of central L L -values on 𝐆𝐒𝐩 ⁡ ( 2 ) {\bf GSp}(2) for square free levels
Seiji Kuga and Masao Tsuzuki

Abstract
. We develop a new kind of relative trace formulas on 𝐏𝐆𝐒𝐩 2 {\bf PGSp}_{2} involving the Bessel periods and the Rankin-Selberg type integral a la Piatetski-Shapiro for Siegel cusp forms on its spectral side. As an application, a version of weighted equidistribution theorems for the Satake parameters of Siegel cusp forms of square-free level and of scalar weights is proved.



1.
Introduction

The periods of automorphic forms is of pivotal impotance in number theory. For elliptic modular forms, the toric periods and the Fourier coefficients are two of such; they have a deep connection with the special values of automorphic
L
-functions (
[
44
]
,
[
45
]
). The Gan-Gross-Prasad conjecture (the GGP conjecture for short), originally posed by
[
13
]
and its refined form by Ichino-Ikeda (
[
15
]
) and by Liu (
[
24
]
), is a far reaching generalization of Waldspurger’s results. Recently, Furusawa-Morimoto completely solved an important case of the refined GGP conjecture for the Bessel periods on
{\bf PGSp}_{2}
(
[
6
]
,
[
7
]
,
[
8
]
), giving an affirmative answer to Böcherer’s conjecture on the average of the Fourier coefficients of Siegel cusp forms of degree
2
. In this paper, we are concerned ourselves with the statistical nature of the family of spinor
L
-functions for Siegel modular forms. For this family, Kowalski-Saha-Tsimerman (
[
19
]
) introduced an analogue of the harmonic average for the elliptic cusp forms in terms of the Bessel periods. They studied an asymptotic formula of the “harmonic average” of spinor
L
-values on the convergent range of the Euler products in the weight aspect. Later, asymptotic formulas are proved for the second moment of the central
L
-values by Blomer (
[
2
]
) in the weight aspect, and by Waibel (
[
43
]
) in the level aspect for square free levels. In these works, the basic tool is Kitaoka’s formula, which is a Sielgel modular forms counterpart of Petersson’s trace formula for elliptic modular forms. In this paper, continuing our study
[
20
]
, we develop a new kind of relative trace formula involving the Bessel periods and the Rankin-Selberg integrals of Andrianov type on the spectral side, and prove a weighted density theorem of Satake parameters for the family of Siegel cusp forms of varying weights and square-free levels (Theorems
1.2
and
7.2
). Note that, for Siegel modular forms with principal congruence levels, an automorphic density theorem for the natural average over a family is proved by
[
21
]
. As an application, we prove a new version of simultaneous non-vanishing results of central critical values of
3
twisted spinor
L
-functions in a family of Siegel cusp forms of general type; this is the first such result with a good control of Satake parameters (Corollary
1.3
). In§
7
, we deduce the existence of an infinite family of regular algebraic self-dual cuspidal automorphic representations of
{\bf GL}_{4}
with large Hecke fields (Corollary
1.4
), invoking Arthurs’s classification of discrete spectrum of
{\bf PGSp}_{2}
and Furusawa-Morimoto’s resolution of refined GGP conjecture on Bessel periods.

1.1.
Main results

Set
\mathsf{G}={\bf GSp}_{2}:=\{g\in{\bf GL}_{4}\mid{}^{t}g\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0_{2}&1_{2}\\
-1_{2}&0_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]g=\nu(g)\,\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0_{2}&1_{2}\\
-1_{2}&0_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\}
with
\nu:\mathsf{G}\longrightarrow{\bf GL}_{1}
being the similitude character;
{\bf Sp}_{2}
is defined to be the kernel of
\nu
. Let
{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}:=\{Z=X+iY\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})\mid{}^{t}Z=Z,\,Y\gg 0\}
be the Siegel upper-half space. For a square-free positive integer
N
, the space
S_{l}(N)
of all the holomorphic Siegel cusp forms on
\Gamma_{0}^{(2)}(N):=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})\mid C\equiv 0\pmod{N}\}
of weight
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
is a finite dimensional
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector space. We endow
S_{l}(N)
with the hermitian inner product associated with the norm

\langle\Phi\mid\Phi\rangle_{L^{2}}=\frac{1}{[{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}):\Gamma_{0}^{(2)}(N)]}\int_{\Gamma_{0}^{(2)}(N)\backslash{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}}|\Phi(Z)|^{2}\,(\det{\operatorname{Im}}(Z))^{l}{\rm{d}}\mu_{{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}}(Z),\quad\Phi\in S_{l}(N),

where
{\rm{d}}\mu_{{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}}(Z)=\det(Y)^{-3}\,{{\rm{d}}X\,{\rm{d}}Y}
is the invariant volume element on
{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
. Let
\Phi(Z)
be a non-zero element of
S_{l}(N)
and

\Phi(Z)=\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}}a_{\Phi}(T)\,\exp(2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(TZ)),\quad Z\in{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}

its Fourier expansion, where
\{a_{\Phi}(T)\mid T\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}\}
is the set of Fourier coefficients of
\Phi
, which is indexed by
{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}
, the set of positive definite half-integral symmetric matrices of degree
2
. By the modularity of
\Phi(Z)
, we have
a_{\Phi}(\delta T{}^{t}\delta)=a_{\Phi}(T)
for all
\delta\in{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
and
T\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}
. Let
E={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{D})
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
D<0
. Then, the set

{\mathcal{Q}}_{\rm prim}^{+}(D):=\Bigl\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&\frac{b}{2}\\
\frac{b}{2}&c\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}\Bigm|\,b^{2}-4ac=D,\,(a,b,c)=1\,\Bigr\}

is
{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-invariant, and the quotient
{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})\backslash{\mathcal{Q}}_{\rm prim}^{+}(D)
is in a natural bijective correspondence with the ideal class group
{\rm Cl}(E)
of
E
; the ideal class corresponding to the
{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-orbit of
T\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\rm prim}^{+}(D)
is denoted by
[T]
. Following
[
19
]
, for a character
\Lambda
of
{\rm Cl}(E)
, define

R(\Phi,D,\Lambda):=\sum_{T\in{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})\backslash{\mathcal{Q}}_{\rm prim}^{+}(D)}a_{\Phi}(T)\,\Lambda([T])^{-1},

and

(1.1)

\displaystyle\omega^{\Phi}_{l,D,\Lambda}:=\tfrac{\sqrt{\pi}}{4}(4\pi)^{3-2l}\Gamma\left(l-\tfrac{3}{2}\right)\Gamma(l-2)\times\left(\tfrac{|D|}{4}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}-l}\frac{4(2-\delta_{\Lambda^{2},{\bf 1}})}{w_{D}\,h_{D}}\frac{|R(\Phi,D,\Lambda)|^{2}}{\langle\Phi\mid\Phi\rangle_{L^{2}}}

with
h_{D}:=\#{\rm Cl}(E)
and
w_{D}
being the number of the roots of unity in
E
.

To describe our results, we use the notion of automorphic representations of
\mathsf{G}
, which we refer to
[
5
, §3]
. Let
{\mathbb{A}}
denote the ring of adeles of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
the subring of finite adeles. Since
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})=\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}})\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}{\bf K}_{0}(N)
with

{\bf K}_{0}(N):=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{G}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})\mid C\in N\,{\bf M}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})\},

from
\Phi(Z)
, we can define a function
\tilde{\Phi}(g)
on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
in such a way that

(1.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}(\gamma g_{\infty}g_{\rm{\bf{f}}})

\displaystyle=\nu(g_{\infty})^{l}\det(Ci+D)^{-l}\Phi((Ai+D)(Ci+D)^{-1}),

\displaystyle\quad\gamma\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}),\,g_{\infty}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0},\,g_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\in{\bf K}_{0}(N).

The image of the linear injective map
\Phi\mapsto\widetilde{\Phi}
, say
\tilde{S}_{l}(N)
, is contained in the space of cusp forms on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
that are
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}}){\bf K}_{0}(N)
-invariant. Let
\Pi_{\rm cusp}
be a maximal set of mutually orthogonal irreducible subrepresentations
(\pi,V_{\pi})
of the automorphic forms in
L_{\rm cusp}^{2}({\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}})\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}))
. Let
\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)
be the set of
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}
such that
V_{\pi}(l,N):=V_{\pi}\cap\widetilde{S}_{l}(N)
is non-zero, so that
\widetilde{S}_{l}(N)
is a direct sum of
V_{\pi}(l,N)
for
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)
. Let
N_{\pi}
for
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)
be the least positive divisor of
N
such that
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N_{\pi})
. Any
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)
is decomposed to a restricted tensor product of irreducible smooth representations
\pi_{p}
of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
for
p<\infty
and a holomorphic discrete series representation
D_{l}
of weight
l
of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
and of trivial central character. As in
[
20
]
, throughout this paper, we suppose that

(1.3)

all the prime divisors of
N
are inert in
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Let
\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)
be a set of
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)
such that
R(D,\Phi,\Lambda^{-1})\not=0
for some
\widetilde{\Phi}\in V_{\pi}(l,N)
. Recall that, under assumption (
1.3
), the label of the local representation
\pi_{p}
for
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)
is either I or IIb if
p\mid\frac{N}{N_{\pi}}
and is either IIIa or VIb if
p\mid N_{\pi}
(
[
5
, §3]
). Given a character
\mu=\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}\mu_{p}
of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, define
{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geqslant 0}
to be the product
\prod_{p\mid N}{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi_{p},\mu_{p})
with
{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi_{p},\mu_{p})
being defined as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1\quad(\text{$p\mid N_{\pi}$ and $\pi_{p}$ is of type VIb}),\\
2\quad(\text{$p\mid N_{\pi}$ and $\pi_{p}$ is of type IIIa}),\\
2(p-1)p^{-5}L(1,\pi_{p},{\rm Std})\,\{1+\mu_{p}^{2}(p)-\tfrac{\mu_{p}(p)}{p+1}{\operatorname{tr}}(p^{-1}T_{p}+\eta_{p}|\pi_{p}^{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})})\}\quad(\text{$p\mid\frac{N}{N_{\pi}}$}),\end{cases}

where
T_{p}
is the Hecke operator defined by the double coset
{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p1_{2}&{}\\
{}&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
on
{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
-fixed vectors in an admissible
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-module,
\eta_{p}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is the Atkin-Lehner element at
p
(see §
6.2
(ii)), and
L(s,\pi_{p},{\rm Std})
is the local standard
L
-factor of
\pi_{p}
. Note that
{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)=1
if
N=1
, and
{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)>0
if
\mu^{2}={\bf 1}
and
N_{\pi}=N
. As in
[
20
, §4.4]
, for each
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)
, we, once and for all, fix an element
\Phi_{\pi}^{0}\in S_{l}(N_{\pi})-\{0\}
, which is a newform in the sense of
[
32
, §3.3.5]
, in such a way that
\widetilde{\Phi_{\pi}^{0}}\in V_{\pi}
corresponds to a pure tensor
(\otimes_{p<\infty}\phi_{\pi_{p}}^{0})\otimes\phi_{\infty}
with
\phi_{\pi_{p}}^{0}
being a
{\bf K}_{0}(N_{\pi}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
-invariant vector of
\pi_{p}
which is an eigenvector of the operator
T_{p}
if
\pi_{p}
is of type IIIa, and
\phi_{\infty}
being the fixed lowest weight vector of
D_{l}
.
Let
{\bf B}
be the Borel subgroup of
\mathsf{G}
, which consists of all the matrices of the form
\mathsf{m}(A,c)\mathsf{n}(B)
(for this notation we refer to §
2.1
) with
A
being an upper-triangular matrix of degree
2
. The set of elements
\mathsf{m}(A,1)\mathsf{n}(B)
with
A
being upper-triangular unipotent (resp.
\mathsf{m}(A,c)
with
A
being diagonal) is denoted by
{\bf U}
(resp. by
{\bf T}
). Then
{\bf U}
is the unipotent radical of
{\bf B}
and
{\bf B}={\bf T}{\bf U}
is a Levi decomposition. The involutions on the complex torus
({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2}
,
(a,b)\mapsto(b,a)
and
(a,b)\mapsto(a^{-1},b)
, generate a subgroup
W\subset{\rm Aut}(({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2})
isomorphic to the
C_{2}
-Weyl group. For
y=(a,b)\in({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2}/W
, define a quasi-character
\chi_{\alpha,\beta}:{\bf B}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
by

\chi_{\alpha,\beta}({\operatorname{diag}}(t_{1},t_{2},\lambda t_{1}^{-1},\lambda t_{2}^{-1})n)=|t_{1}|_{p}^{-\alpha-\beta}|t_{2}|_{p}^{-\alpha+\beta}|\lambda|_{p}^{\alpha},\quad(t_{1},t_{2},\lambda)\in({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times})^{3},\,u\in{\bf U}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),

where
\alpha:={\rm ord}_{p}(a)
and
\beta:={\rm ord}_{p}(b)
.
Let
\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y)
be the smallest subspace of smooth functions on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
that is invariant by the right translations by
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and contains the spherical function
\omega_{p}(y):{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
(a la Harish-Chandra and Satake) defined by

(1.4)

\displaystyle\omega_{p}(y;g)=\int_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\chi_{\alpha-3/2,\beta+1/2}(t(kg)){\rm{d}}k,\quad g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),

where
t(g)\in{\bf T}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\bf T}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is uniquely defined by
g\in t(g){\bf U}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. The map
y\mapsto\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y)
yields a bijection from
({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2}/W
onto the set of all the equivalence classes of smooth irreducible unramified representations of
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Let
Y_{p}
denote the set of
y=(a,b)\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
such that
\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y)
is unitarizable. Set
[Y_{p}]:=Y_{p}/W
. For a prime
p\nmid N
, let
y_{p}(\pi):=(a_{p},b_{p})\in[Y_{p}]
be the Satake parameter of
\pi
at
p
, so that
\pi_{p}\cong\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(a_{p},b_{p})
. The local spinor
L
-factor of
\pi_{p}
is defined as

L(s,\pi_{p}):=(1-a_{p}p^{-s})^{-1}(1-b_{p}p^{-s})^{-1}(1-a_{p}^{-1}p^{-s})^{-1}(1-b_{p}^{-1}p^{-s})^{-1}.

The representation
\pi_{q}
with
q\mid N
is one of the Iwahori spherical representations listed in
[
32
, Table 2]
; we use the local
L
-factor
L(s,\pi_{q})
as proposed there. The twisted local
L
-factor
L(s,\pi_{p},\mu_{p})
by a character
\mu_{p}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
is defined as
L(s,\pi_{p},\mu_{p}):=L(s+c_{p},\pi_{p})
if
\mu_{p}
is unramified with
c_{p}\in{\mathbb{C}}
being defined by
\mu_{p}(p)=p^{-c_{p}}
, and
L(s,\pi_{p},\mu_{p}):=1
if
\mu_{p}
is ramified and
\pi_{p}
is
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
-spherical. Let
\mu:{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
be a character of conductor
M
prime to
N_{\pi}
. The spinor
L
-function of
\pi
twisted by
\mu
is initially defined by the Euler product
L(s,\pi,\mu):=\prod_{p<\infty}L(s,\pi_{p},\mu_{p})
absolutely convergent on
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>5/2
. The completed
L
-function for
L(s,\pi,\mu)
is defined as

(1.5)

\displaystyle\widehat{L}(s,\pi,\mu):=\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}\left(s+\tfrac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}\left(s+l-\tfrac{3}{2}\right)\times L(s,\pi,\mu).

In
[
20
, §4.6]
, for
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)
with
N
being as in (
1.3
) and for
\mu
as above with the conductor being a product of odd primes inert in
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
, we have the explicit functional equation as expected (
[
32
, §3.1.4]
) by identifying the local
L
-factor and the local
\varepsilon
-factor defined in
[
29
]
(in terms of the Bessel model from
E
and
\Lambda
) with the ones proposed in
[
32
, Table 2,3]
. In particular, the functional equation of
\widehat{L}(s,\pi,\mu)
is self-dual with the sign being
(-1)^{l}
for all
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)
if
\mu^{2}={\bf 1}
.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
E={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{D})
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
D<0
, and
\Lambda
a character of
{\rm Cl}(E)
. Let
\mu:{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
be a character whose conductor
M
is a product of odd primes inert in
E
; as such,
\mu
induces a primitive Dirichlet character
\tilde{\mu}
mod
M
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
such

\displaystyle[{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}):\Gamma_{0}^{(2)}(N)]^{-1}

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)}\omega_{l,D,\Lambda^{-1}}^{\Phi_{\pi}^{0}}\,\,L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi,\mu\right)\,{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)

\displaystyle=\prod_{q\in S(N)}(1+q^{-2})\times 2P(l,N,\Lambda,\mu)+O(2^{-l+\#S(N)}N^{-\frac{l-6}{4}})

uniformly for
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 8}
and all square free positive integer
N
relatively prime to
M
such that the set
S(N)
of prime divisors of
N
consists of inert primes for
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
, where

\displaystyle P(l,N,D,\Lambda,\mu):=\begin{cases}L(1,\kappa_{D})\,(\log N+\psi(l-1)-\log(4\pi^{2}))+L^{\prime}(1,\kappa_{D})\quad&(\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1}),\\
\tilde{\mu}(DN)^{-2}L(1,\Lambda\mu_{E})+\tilde{\mu}(4)^{-1}\left(\tfrac{G(\tilde{\mu}^{-1})}{\sqrt{M}}\right)^{4}\,L(1,\Lambda^{-1}\mu_{E}^{-1})\quad&(\Lambda\mu_{E}\not={\bf 1})\end{cases}

with
\psi(s):=\Gamma^{\prime}(s)/\Gamma(s)
the di-gamma function,
\kappa_{D}
the Kronecker character,
L(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})
the Hecke
L
-function of
\Lambda\mu_{E}
with
\mu_{E}:=\mu\circ{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}
, and
G(\tilde{\mu}):=\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/M{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\tilde{\mu}(a)e^{2\pi ia/M}.

Note that
\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1}
is equivalent to both
\Lambda
and
\mu
being trivial. We remark that this result is consistent with
[
2
, Theorem 1]
, which deals with the case
\mu={\bf 1}
,
N=1
and
D=-4
, so that
\Lambda={\bf 1}
and
M=1
. Due to the factor
{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)
, our average is a bit different from the one in
[
2
, Theorem 6]
, which corresponds to
\mu={\bf 1}
,
N\equiv 3\pmod{4}
being a large prime and
D=-4
in our setting. Discarding the contribution from the old forms and the Saito-Kurokawa forms, the two formulas should be consistent with each other. It should be noted that
P(l,N,D,\Lambda,\mu)/\log(lN)\sim L(1,\kappa_{D})>0
if
\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1}
, and
P(l,N,\Lambda,\mu)=L(1,\Lambda\mu_{E})>0
if
\Lambda\mu_{E}\not={\bf 1}
and
\mu
is quadratic. To describe our second theorem, which is a refinement of Theorem
1.1
, we need additional notation and definitions. Let
{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
denote the Hecke algebra for
(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))
. The spherical Fourier transform of
f\in{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
(or rather its
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-average
f^{Z}(g):=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}f(zg)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}z
) is defined by
\widehat{f}(y):=\int_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}f(g)\omega_{p}(a^{-1},b^{-1};g)\,{\rm{d}}g
for
y=(a,b)\in[Y_{p}]
with
{\rm{d}}g
being the Haar measure on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that
{\operatorname{vol}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))=1
. The Fourier inversion formula is known to be described as

(1.6)

\displaystyle f^{Z}(g)=\int_{Y_{p}/W}{\widehat{f}}(y)\,\omega_{p}(y;g)\,{\rm{d}}\mu_{p}^{\rm Pl}(y),\quad f\in{\mathscr{H}}_{p},\,g\in{\mathsf{G}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),

where
{\rm{d}}\mu_{p}^{\rm Pl}(y)
is the spherical Plancherel measure of
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, which is a Radon measure on
[Y_{p}]
supported on the tempered locus
[Y_{p}^{0}]:={\bf U}(1)^{2}/W
. For
y=(a,b)\in({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2}/W
and an irreducible smooth unramified representation
\sigma_{p}
of
{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of Satake parameter
B_{p}={\operatorname{diag}}(c,c^{-1})\in{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
, set
A_{p}:={\operatorname{diag}}(a,b,a^{-1},b^{-1})\in{\bf Sp}_{4}({\mathbb{C}})\,(={}^{L}{\bf PGSp}_{4}))
and

\displaystyle L(s,\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y)\times\sigma_{p})

\displaystyle:=\det(1_{8}-(A_{p}\otimes B_{p})\,p^{-s})^{-1},

\displaystyle L(s,\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y);{{\operatorname{Ad}}})

\displaystyle:=\det(1-{\operatorname{Ad}}(A_{p})p^{-s})^{-1},

where
{\operatorname{Ad}}
is the adjoint representation of
{\bf Sp}_{4}({\mathbb{C}})
on its Lie algebra.
For a class group character
\Lambda\in\widehat{{\rm Cl}(E)}
, viewing it as a character of the idele class group of
E^{\times}
, we form its automorphic induction
{\mathcal{AI}}(\Lambda)=\bigotimes_{p\leqslant\infty}{\mathcal{AI}}_{p}(\Lambda)
to
{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
.
Let
\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{(E,\Lambda)}(l,N){}^{\rm G,new}
be the set of
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{(E,\Lambda)}(l,N)
whose Arthur parameter is of the form
\pi^{{\rm GL}}\boxtimes{\bf 1}_{{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})}
with
\pi^{{\rm GL}}
an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
{\bf GL}_{4}({\mathbb{A}})
such that
L(s,\pi^{{\rm GL}},\wedge^{2})
has a pole at
s=1
(
[
1
]
and
[
9
]
, see also
[
33
]
).

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
D<0
be fundamental discriminant. Let
\mu:{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}
be a character whose conductor
M
is a product of odd primes inert in
E:={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{D})
. Let
N
be either
1
or a prime number inert in
E
and
(N,M)=1
. Let
S
be a finite set of prime numbers prime to
NMD
. Let
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 7}
. Then, as
N\rightarrow\infty
, or
N=1
and
l\rightarrow\infty
,

(1.7)

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{q\in S(N)}(1+q^{-2})^{-1}}{(\log Nl)^{\delta(\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1})}[{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}):\Gamma_{0}^{(2)}(N)]}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{(E,\Lambda)}(l,N){}^{\rm G,new}}{\alpha}(y_{S}(\pi))\,{L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi,\mu\right)}\,\omega^{\Phi_{\pi}^{0}}_{l,D,\Lambda^{-1}}{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)

\displaystyle\quad\longrightarrow 2{{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\Lambda}_{S}(\alpha)\begin{cases}L(1,\kappa_{D}),\quad&(\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1}),\\
L(1,{\mathcal{AI}}(\Lambda)\times\mu),\quad&(\Lambda\mu_{E}\not={\bf 1}),\end{cases},\qquad{\alpha}\in C([Y_{S}^{0}]),

where
y_{S}(\pi)=\{y_{p}(\pi)\}_{p\in S}\in[Y_{S}^{0}]
is the set of Satake parameter of
\pi
over
S
, and
{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{S}^{\Lambda,\mu}
is a Radon measure on
[Y^{0}_{S}]
such that
{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{S}^{\Lambda,\mu}(\alpha)
for
\alpha=\otimes_{p\in S}\alpha_{p}\,(\alpha_{p}\in C([Y_{p}^{0}])
equals
\prod_{p\in S}\int_{[Y_{p}^{0}]}{\mathfrak{D}}_{p}(y_{p})\alpha_{p}(y_{p})\,{\rm{d}}\mu_{p}^{\rm Pl}(y_{p})
for
y=(y_{p})_{p\in S}
with

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{D}}_{p}(y_{p}):=\frac{\zeta_{p}(1)^{-1}\zeta_{p}(2)\zeta_{p}(4)}{L(1,{\mathcal{AI}}(\Lambda)_{p}\times\mu_{p})}\,\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y)\times{\mathcal{AI}}(\Lambda)_{p}\right)L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y),\mu_{p}\right)}{L(1,\pi_{p}^{\rm ur}(y),{\rm Ad})}.

Combining Theorem
1.2
with a version of the refined GGP conjecture for the Bessel periods proved in
[
7
]
, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 1.3
.

Let
D<0
be a fundamental discriminant, and
N
either
1
or a prime number inert in
E={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{D})
. Let
\mu:{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
be a character whose conductor
M>0
is an odd fundamental discriminant such that
(DN,M)=1
. Let
S
be a finite set of prime numbers prime to
NMD
. Let
\Lambda
be a character of
{\rm Cl}(E)
. Let
U
be a measurable subset of
[Y_{S}^{0}]
such that
\mu_{S}^{\rm Pl}(U)>0
and
\mu_{S}^{\rm Pl}(\partial U)=0
. Define
{{\mathfrak{P}}}(l,N,\Lambda,\mu,U)
to be the set of
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)^{\rm G,new}
such that

\displaystyle L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi,\mu\right)\,L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi\times{\mathcal{A}I}(\Lambda)\right)>0,\qquad\quad y_{S}(\pi)\in U.

(i)

Let
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 7}
be fixed. Then, there exists
q_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
such that
{{\mathfrak{P}}}(l,q,\Lambda,\mu,U)\not=\varnothing
for any prime number
q>q_{0}
such that
q\nmid DM
and inert in
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{D})
.

(ii)

Let
N=1
. Then, there exists
l_{0}\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 7}
such that
{{\mathfrak{P}}}(l,1,\Lambda,\mu,U)\not=\varnothing
for any even integer
l>l_{0}
.

Theorem
1.2
is deduced from Theorem
6.11
with an error term whose dependence on
f_{S}\in\otimes_{p\in S}{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
is explicit: this version is more important in potential applications. As one of such applications, in §
7
, we prove the following.

Corollary 1.4
.

Let
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 7}
. Let
M
and
D<0
be fundamental discriminants prime to each other. Let
p
be a prime such that
p\nmid MD
. Then, there exists constants
N_{p}>p|DM|
and
C_{p}>0
with the following properties: For any prime number
N>N_{p}
, there exists an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi
of
{\bf GL}_{4}({\mathbb{A}})
such that

(i)

The JPSS-conductor of
\Pi
is
N^{2}
,

(ii)

\Pi
is regular algebraic of infinite type
(l,2,1,3-l)

(
[
3
, Definition 3.6]
)
, so that its field of rationality
{\mathbb{Q}}(\Pi)
is a number field,

(iii)

\Pi
is of symplectic type, i.e.,
L(s,\Pi,\wedge^{2})
has a pole at
s=1
,

(iv)

L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\Pi\right)\,L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\Pi\times\kappa_{M}\right)\,L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\Pi\times\kappa_{D}\right)\not=0
,

(v)

\dim_{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{Q}}(\Pi)\geqslant C_{p}\sqrt{\log\log N}
.

Note that the quadratic twists
L(s,\Pi\times\kappa)
as in the corollary are expected to be “motivic” and
s=1/2
is the (unique) critical point in the sense of Deligne.

1.2.
Structure of paper

In §2, after introducing basic notation, the notion of the Bessel periods of automorphic forms on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
is recalled and the construction of the Eisenstein series on the subgroup
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})
is reviewed. The Shintani function on the
p
-adic orthogonal groups plays a pivotal role in the formation of the refined GGP conjecture (
[
15
]
,
[
24
]
). An archimedean counterpart was first introduced in
[
40
]
as a kernel of a Poincaré series; the latter was closely studied in
[
41
]
to prove a weighted equidistribution theorem of the Satake parameters in the weight aspect for full level cusp forms on
{\rm SO}(2+m)
. Actually, in
[
42
]
the second author already deduce a part of Theorem
1.2
from a general result in
[
41
]
by the exceptional isomorphism
{\bf PGSp}_{2}\cong{\rm SO}(5)
. In this paper, we write some of the archimedean computations in
[
41
]
directly on
{\bf GSp}_{2}
. In §
3.1
, the Shintani function on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
is introduced; then the construction of a Poincarè series given in
[
40
]
is modified so that the Poincaré series depend on two test functions
\phi
and
f
; its spectral expansion is given in §
3.5
. In §
4
, we describe the double coset space
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}})/\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{Q}})
explicitly, which allows us to write the Bessel period of the Poincaré series as a sum of 5 terms
{\mathbb{J}}_{1_{4}}
,
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm s})}
,
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm r})}
,
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{1}}
and
{\mathbb{J}}_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}
in (
5.7
) according to the double cosets. In §
6
, we specify the test functions
\phi
and
f
and state our results on explicit formula of the main term
{\mathbb{J}}_{1_{4}}+{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm s})}
(Proposition
6.1
) and the estimate of other terms (Proposition
6.4
), postponing their proof to later sections. The proof of Theorem
1.1
and
1.2
are completed in §
6.5
. In §
7
, we describe a proof of Corollary
1.4
. The remaining sections are devoted to addressing highly technical issues.
In §
8
, we prove Proposition
6.1
by using the explicit determination of local orbital integrals given in §
11
. Upper estimates of
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm r})}
,
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{1}}
and
{\mathbb{J}}_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}
are obtained by constructing gauge functions.

2.
Preliminaries

For sets
X\subset Y
, let
\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{X}
denote the characteristic function of
X
on
Y
; if
Y
is a topological space,
C(Y)
denotes the space of all
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued continuous functions on
Y
. The identity matrix of degree
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>1}
is denoted by
1_{n}
. For
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
and
\varepsilon>0
, set
{\mathcal{T}}_{I,\varepsilon}:=\{s\in{\mathbb{C}}\mid{\operatorname{Re}}(s)\in I,\,|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|\geqslant\varepsilon\}
. A meromorphic function
f(s)
, holomorphic on
{\mathbb{C}}-{\mathbb{R}}
, is said to be vertically of polynomial growth (resp. vertically bounded) if for any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
there exist
\varepsilon>0
and
N\geqslant 0
such that
f(s)\ll(1+|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|)^{N}
(resp.
f(s)\ll 1
) for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I,\varepsilon}
.

For any totally disconnected unimodular group
L
with a Haar measure, let
{\mathscr{H}}(L)
denote the Hecke algebra of
L
, i.e., the
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra of all those compactly supported locally constant
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued functions with the product being defined by the convolution of two such functions.
For a number field
F
, the adele ring of
F
,
{\mathbb{A}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}}F
, is denoted by
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}
; the subring of finite adele is denoted by
{\mathbb{A}}_{F,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}
. The subscript
{\rm{\bf{f}}}
is used to indicate that the object is related to the finite adeles. For
a=(a_{p})_{p<\infty}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}
, set
|a|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}=\prod_{p<\infty}|a_{p}|_{p}
. The Kronecker character of fundamental discriminant
D
is denoted by
\kappa_{D}
.

2.1.

Let
{\mathsf{V}}
denotes the space of symmetric matrices of degree
2
; we consider
{\mathsf{V}}
as a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-scheme by defining
{\mathsf{V}}(R)=\left\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
b&c\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mid a,b,c\in R\right\}
for any commutative ring
R
. The group
{\bf GL}_{2}(R)
acts on this space from the right as

(2.1)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}(R)\times{\bf GL}_{2}(R)\ni(T,h)\quad\longmapsto\quad T{\mathsf{s}}(h):=\tfrac{1}{\det h}{}^{t}hTh\in{\mathsf{V}}(R).

For a
2\times 2
-matrix
A=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right]
, set
A^{\dagger}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}d&-b\\
-c&a\end{smallmatrix}\right]
, so that
AA^{\dagger}=(\det A)\,1_{2}
. We define a symmetric
R
-bilinear form on
{\mathsf{V}}(R)
as

(2.2)

\displaystyle\langle X,Y\rangle:={\operatorname{tr}}(XY^{\dagger}),\quad X,Y\in{\mathsf{V}}(R).

As in §
1.1
, let
\mathsf{G}
denote the symplectic similitude group and
{\mathsf{Z}}
its center.
Let
\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{M}\mathsf{N}
be the Siegel parabolic subgroup of
\mathsf{G}
, where

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathsf{m}(A,c):=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&O\\
O&{}^{t}A^{-1}c\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mid A\in{\bf GL}_{2}\,,c\in{\bf GL}_{1}\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathsf{n}(B):=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&B\\
O&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mid B\in{\mathsf{V}}\}.

Note that
\nu(\mathsf{m}(A,c))=c
and
\nu(\mathsf{n}(B))=1
.

2.2.
Distinguished subgroups

Let
E={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{D})\,(\subset{\mathbb{C}})
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
D<0
and
\mathfrak{o}_{E}
the ring of integers in
E
. Set
\theta:=\frac{{\mathtt{t}}-\sqrt{D}}{2}
with
{\mathtt{t}}\in\{0,1\}
having the same parity as
D
. Then,
\{1,\theta\}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis of
\mathfrak{o}_{E}
such that
\bar{\theta}-\theta=\sqrt{D}
and
{\operatorname{tr}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\theta)={\mathtt{t}}
. Set
{{\mathtt{N}}}:={\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\theta)
, so that
{\mathtt{t}}^{2}-4{\mathtt{N}}=D
.
Throughout this article,
E
and
\theta
are fixed. Let
{\mathcal{Q}}:=\{T\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})\mid{\operatorname{tr}}(TX)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,(\forall X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}))\}
, which is also equal to the dual lattice of
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}})
with respect to (
2.2
); explicitly,
{\mathcal{Q}}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\varepsilon_{j}{\mathbb{Z}}
with

(2.3)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\quad\varepsilon_{2}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1/2\\
1/2&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\quad\varepsilon_{3}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right].

We have
{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(a+b\theta)=[a,b]T_{\theta}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right]
for
a,b\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with

T_{\theta}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&2^{-1}{\mathtt{t}}\\
2^{-1}{\mathtt{t}}&{\mathtt{N}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\mathcal{Q}}.

We record the following easily confirmed formulas for later use:

(2.4)

\displaystyle{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger}T_{\theta})

\displaystyle={-D}/{2},\qquad\det(T_{\theta})={-D}/{4}.

For
\tau\in E
, let
I_{\theta}(\tau)\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
be the matrix representing the regular representation of
E
, i.e.,
[\tau,\tau\theta]=[1,\theta]I_{\theta}(\tau)
, or explicitly

I_{\theta}(a+b\theta)=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&-b{\mathtt{N}}\\
b&a+{\mathtt{t}}b\end{smallmatrix}\right]
for
a,b\in{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Set

(2.5)

\displaystyle\sigma:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&{\mathtt{t}}\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}\right].

Then,
\sigma^{2}=1_{2}
and

(2.6)

\displaystyle{}^{t}I_{\theta}(\tau)T_{\theta}I_{\theta}(\tau)={\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\tau)\,T_{\theta},\quad\sigma I_{\theta}(\tau)\sigma=I_{\theta}(\bar{\tau})=I_{\theta}(\tau)^{\dagger}.

Let
{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}
be the closed
{\mathbb{Q}}
-subgroup of
{\bf GL}_{2}
defined by the relation
{}^{t}hT_{\theta}h=\pm\det(h)\,T_{\theta}
. The Zariski identity connected component
{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}^{\circ}=\{h\in{\bf GL}_{2}\mid{}^{t}hT_{\theta}h=\det(h)T_{\theta}\}
of
{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}
is isomorphic to
{\rm Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}{\bf GL}_{1}
by the map
I_{\theta}
. Since
\det(\sigma)=-1
and
{}^{t}\sigma T_{\theta}\sigma=T_{\theta}
, we have

(2.7)

\displaystyle{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}})=I_{\theta}(E^{\times}),\quad{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{Q}})=I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\cup\sigma I_{\theta}(E^{\times}).

Let
\mathsf{G}^{\#}
be a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-group scheme, whose
R
-points for any commutative ring
R
is given as

\mathsf{G}^{\#}(R)=\{h\in{\bf GL}_{2}(\mathfrak{o}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R)\mid\det(h)\in R^{\times}\},

and define an embedding
\iota_{\theta}:\mathsf{G}^{\#}\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}
of group schemes as in
[
20
, §2.1]
by the symplectic form
\langle x,y\rangle_{D}:={\operatorname{tr}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\frac{-1}{\sqrt{D}}\det\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}&y_{1}\\
x_{2}&y_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right])
on the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-lattice
W:=\mathfrak{o}_{E}^{\,2}
with the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis

(2.8)

\displaystyle v_{1}^{+}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\quad v_{2}^{+}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\theta\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\quad v_{1}^{-}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
-\bar{\theta}\end{smallmatrix}\right],\quad v_{2}^{-}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right],

so that
\nu(\iota_{\theta}(h))=\det h\,(h\in\mathsf{G}^{\#})
. Note that
\langle v_{i}^{+},v_{j}^{-}\rangle_{D}=\delta_{ij}
and
\langle v_{i}^{+},v_{j}^{+}\rangle_{D}=\langle v_{i}^{-},v_{j}^{-}\rangle_{D}=0
for all
i,j
. Set
{\mathsf{Z}}^{\#}:=\{a1_{2}\mid a\in{\bf GL}_{1}\}
, which is a central subgroup of
\mathsf{G}^{\#}
. Let
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}*&*\\
0&*\end{smallmatrix}\right]\}\cap\mathsf{G}^{\#}
denote the Borel subgroup of
\mathsf{G}^{\#}
formed by the upper-triangular matrices in
\mathsf{G}^{\#}
. We have the following formulas, which, combined with the Bruhat decomposition
\mathsf{G}^{\#}={\mathsf{B}}^{\#}\cup{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}
, yields a practically useful description of the map
\iota_{\theta}
:

(2.9)

\displaystyle\iota_{\theta}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&\tau^{-1}a\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(I_{\theta}(\tau),a),\quad\tau\in E^{\times},\,a\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times},

(2.10)

\displaystyle\iota_{\theta}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{n}(X_{\beta}),\quad\beta\in E,

(2.11)

\displaystyle\iota_{\theta}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&0&-{\mathtt{t}}&1\\
0&0&1&0\\
0&1&0&0\\
1&{\mathtt{t}}&0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],

where

(2.12)

\displaystyle X_{\beta}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}b_{1}&b_{2}\\
b_{2}&b_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})\quad\text{for $\beta=b_{2}+b_{3}\,\theta\,(b_{2},b_{3}\in{\mathbb{Q}})$ with $b_{1}:=-b_{2}{\mathtt{t}}-b_{3}{\mathtt{N}}$}.

Note that
\beta\mapsto X_{\beta}
is a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear isomorphism from
E
onto the space

{\mathsf{V}}^{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{Q}}):=\{X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})\mid{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X)=0\},

which is the orthogonal space of
T_{\theta}^{\dagger}
with respect to (
2.2
). We also have the relation

-{\operatorname{tr}}(X_{\alpha}X_{\beta}^{\dagger})={\operatorname{tr}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\alpha\bar{\beta})
for
\alpha,\beta\in E
.

2.3.
Exceptional isomorphism

For use in §
10.2
and §
10.3
, let us recall the exceptional isomorphism between
{\mathsf{Z}}\backslash\mathsf{G}\,(={\bf PGSp}_{2})
and a special orthogonal group of degree
5
. Recall
W=\mathfrak{o}_{E}^{2}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
. Since
W\ni x\mapsto\langle x,\bullet\rangle_{D}\in W^{\vee}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear isomorphism, we obtain a symplectic form on
W^{\vee}
by transport of structure, which yields an element
\omega
of
\wedge^{2}W
.
Set
\Omega=2^{-1}\omega\wedge\omega=v_{1}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{+}\wedge v_{1}^{-}\wedge v_{2}^{-}
. Since
\wedge^{4}W={\mathbb{Z}}\Omega
, a symmetric
{\mathbb{Z}}
-bilinear form
B:\wedge^{2}W\times\wedge^{2}W\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
is defined by the relation
\xi\wedge\xi^{\prime}=B(\xi,\xi^{\prime})\,\Omega
(
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\wedge^{2}W
). Note that
\omega=v_{1}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{+}+v_{2}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{-}
and
\Omega=v_{1}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{+}\wedge v_{1}^{-}\wedge v_{2}^{-}
. If we set

\displaystyle\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=v_{1}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{+},\quad\xi_{2}=v_{1}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{-},\quad\xi_{3}=v_{1}^{+}\wedge v_{1}^{-}-v_{2}^{+}\wedge v_{2}^{-},

\displaystyle\xi_{4}

\displaystyle=v_{2}^{+}\wedge v_{1}^{-},\quad\xi_{5}=v_{1}^{-}\wedge v_{2}^{-},

then
\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{5},\omega\}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis of
\wedge^{2}W
such that

B(\xi_{i},\xi_{j})=\delta_{i,6-j}\,(i\not=3),\quad B(\xi_{3},\xi_{3})=2,\quad B(\omega,\omega)=-2,

and
B(\xi_{i},\omega)=0\,(\forall i)
, which means that
{\rm SO}({\mathsf{X}})
is identified with the matrix group

{\bf SO}(Q):=\{g\in{\bf SL}_{4}\mid{}^{t}gQg=Q\}\quad\text{with}\,Q=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}{}&{}&{}&{}&1\\
{}&{}&{}&{1}&{}\\
{}&{}&{2}&{}&{}\\
{}&{1}&{}&{}&{}\\
{1}&{}&{}&{}&{}\end{smallmatrix}\right].

For any
g\in\mathsf{G}(R)
, define
\mathsf{r}(g)\in{\operatorname{End}}(\wedge^{2}W\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R)
by
\mathsf{r}(g)=\nu(g)^{-1}\,(\wedge^{2}g)
.
Then, the map
\mathsf{r}
yields an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Q}}
-groups from
{\mathsf{Z}}\backslash\mathsf{G}
to
{\rm SO}({\mathsf{X}})
. Define a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear injection
{\mathsf{V}}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{X}}
by

(2.13)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}\ni T=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x&y\\
y&z\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mapsto[T]:=x\xi_{2}-y\xi_{3}-z\xi_{4}\,\in{\mathsf{X}}.

A direct computation shows
B([S],[T])=-{\operatorname{tr}}(ST^{\dagger})
for
S,T\in{\mathsf{V}}(R)
, and the formulas

(2.14)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{m}(A,\lambda))\,\xi_{1}&=\lambda^{-1}\det(A)\,\xi_{1},\\
\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{n}(S))\,\xi_{1}&=\xi_{1},\end{cases}\qquad\begin{cases}\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{m}(A,\lambda))\,[T]&=[T{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}A)],\\
\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{n}(S))\,[T]&={\operatorname{tr}}(ST^{\dagger})\,\xi_{1}+[T]\end{cases}

for
\mathsf{m}(A,\lambda)\in\mathsf{M}(R)
,
S,T\in{\mathsf{V}}(R)
, and that
h_{0}=\mathsf{r}(\iota_{\theta}(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]))
satisfies

(2.15)

\displaystyle h_{0}\xi_{i}=\xi_{6-i}\,(i=1,5),\quad[h_{0}\xi_{2},h_{0}\xi_{3},h_{0}\xi_{4}]=[\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&2{\mathtt{t}}&-{\mathtt{t}}^{2}\\
0&1&-{\mathtt{t}}\\
0&0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right].

Lemma 2.1
.

For any field extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}
,

\iota_{\theta}(\mathsf{G}^{\#}(K))=\{g\in\mathsf{G}(K)\mid\mathsf{r}(g)\,[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]=[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]\}.

Proof.

Let
H
denote the stabilizer of
[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]
in
{\rm SO}(\mathsf{X})
. The second set of formulas in (
2.14
) combined with (
2.9
) and (
2.10
) shows that
\mathsf{r}(\iota_{\theta}({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}))\subset H
. By (
2.15
), we get
h_{0}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]=[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]
. By
\mathsf{G}^{\#}={\mathsf{B}}^{\#}\cup{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}
, we obtain the inclusion
\mathsf{r}(\iota(\mathsf{G}^{\#}))\subset H
. By the Bruhat decomposition of
H\cong{\rm SO}([T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]^{\bot})
, the converse inclusion also holds. ∎

2.3.1.
Height functions

For
p<\infty
, let
\|\cdot\|_{p}
be the height function on
\mathsf{X}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, defined by
\|\sum_{j=1}^{5}x_{j}\xi_{j}\|_{p}=\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant 5}(|x_{j}|_{p})
. Let
{\mathsf{X}}^{\star}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
denote the finite adelization of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-scheme
{\mathsf{X}}^{\star}:={\mathsf{X}}-\{0\}
. Then,
\|\xi_{p}\|_{p}=1
for almost all
p
for any
\xi=(\xi_{p})_{p<\infty}\in{\mathsf{X}}^{\star}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
, which allows us to set
\|\xi\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}:=\prod_{p<\infty}\|\xi_{p}\|_{p}
. For
T\in{\mathsf{V}}^{\star}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
with
{\mathsf{V}}^{\star}:={\mathsf{V}}-\{0\}
, set
\|T\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}:=\|[T]\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
with
[T]\in{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
being as in (
2.13
).

2.4.
maximal compact subgroups

The identity connected component of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
is
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}=\{g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})\mid\nu(g)>0\}
. Set
{\bf K}_{\infty}:=\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}\cap{\bf O}(4)
, which is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
given as

\displaystyle{\bf K}_{\infty}=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
-B&A\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mid A,B\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}),\,A+iB\in{\bf U}(2)\}.

Note that
{\bf K}_{\infty}\subset{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
. The action of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
on the Siegel upper-half space
{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
, denoted by
(g,Z)\mapsto g\langle Z\rangle
, and the canonical automorphy factor
J(g,Z)
are defined by

g\langle Z\rangle:=(AZ+B)(CZ+D)^{-1},\quad J(g,Z)=(\nu(g))^{-1}\det(CZ+D)

for
g=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
and
Z\in{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
. The stabilizer of
i1_{2}\in{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
in
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
coincides with
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{R}}){\bf K}_{\infty}
. Define
h_{0}:{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
as
h_{0}(z):=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a1_{2}&b1_{2}\\
-b1_{2}&a1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]
for
z=a+ib\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
; for
(p,q)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, let
{\mathfrak{g}}^{p,q}
denote the
z^{p}\bar{z}^{q}
-eigenspace of the representation
{\rm Ad}(h_{0}(z))
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}:={\rm Lie}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{C}}
. Then,
{\mathfrak{g}}^{0,0}={\rm Lie}({\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{R}}){\bf K}_{\infty})
and
{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{g}}^{0,0}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}^{1,0}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}^{0,1}
. Set
{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}:={\mathfrak{g}}^{1,0}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}:={\mathfrak{g}}^{0,1}
. Then,
{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}
correpsonds to the holomorphic tangent space of
{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
by the map
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}/{\mathbb{Z}}({\mathbb{R}}){\bf K}_{\infty}\cong{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
. By
[
20
, Lemma 2]
, the image of
{\bf SU}(2)
, which is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
, by the map
\iota_{\theta}
is described as

(2.16)

\displaystyle\iota_{\theta}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&-\bar{b}\\
b&\bar{a}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)=b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
-B&A\end{smallmatrix}\right](b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})^{-1}\quad\text{with $A:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}{\operatorname{Re}}(a)&-{\operatorname{Im}}(a)\\
{\operatorname{Im}}(a)&{\operatorname{Re}}(a)\end{smallmatrix}\right],\,B:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}{\operatorname{Im}}(b)&{\operatorname{Re}}(b)\\
{\operatorname{Re}}(b)&-{\operatorname{Im}}(b)\end{smallmatrix}\right]$},

where

(2.17)

\displaystyle b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{m}(A_{\theta}^{-1},2/\sqrt{|D|}),\quad A_{\theta}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&{\mathtt{t}}/2\\
0&-\sqrt{|D|}/2\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}).

Note that
{}^{t}A_{\theta}A_{\theta}=T_{\theta}
and
\nu(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})>0
. For
p<\infty
, set
{\bf K}_{p}:=\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

2.5.
Haar measures

For any locally compact unimodular group
G
with a Haar measure
\eta_{G}
and its unimodular closed subgroup
H
with a Haar measure
\eta_{H}
, the quotient
H\backslash G
(or
G/H
) is always endowed with the quotient measure of
\eta_{G}
by
\eta_{H}
. As in
[
20
, §2.4]
, we fix
\eta_{G}
as follows for several
G
relevant to us.

Let
\eta_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\eta_{{\mathbb{C}}}
be the Lebesgue measures on
{\mathbb{R}}
and on
{\mathbb{C}}\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, respectively. We define
{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}}(x)=|x|_{\mathbb{R}}^{-1}{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{R}}}(x)
and
{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}(\tau)=|\tau|_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{-1}{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\tau)
. For a place
p\leqslant\infty
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
, set
E_{p}:=E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, and
\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}:=\mathfrak{o}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
if
p<\infty
. For each
p<\infty
,
\eta_{E_{p}^{\times}}
(resp.
\eta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}
) is fixed by demanding that
\eta_{E_{p}^{\times}}(\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}^{\times})=1
(resp.
\eta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times})=1
). Note that our measures
\eta_{E_{\infty}}
and
\eta_{E_{\infty}^{\times}}
are half of the ones in
[
46
]
. For idele groups, we define
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}=\prod_{p\leqslant\infty}\eta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}
, and
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}}=\prod_{p\leqslant\infty}\eta_{E_{p}^{\times}}
viewing
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}
as the restricted product of
E_{p}^{\times}
. We have that
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
is a compact group, whose volume is computed by
[
46
, Chap. V §4 Proposition 9]
; indeed, we have
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\underline{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}})=\pi h_{D}/w_{D}
with
{\underline{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}}=\{t^{1/2}\in E_{\infty}^{\times}\mid t>0\}
,
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})=1
and

(2.18)

\displaystyle{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times})=\pi h_{D}/2w_{D},

where
h_{D}
is the class number of
E
, which is the order of the finite abelian group

{\rm Cl}(E):={\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}^{\times},

and
w_{D}
the number of roots of unity in
E
. By
{\mathsf{Z}}\cong{\bf GL}_{1}
, the measures
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}
and
\eta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}
are transferred to groups
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}})
and
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\,(p\leqslant\infty)
, respectively. Similar remark is applied to
{\mathsf{Z}}^{\#}\cong{\bf GL}_{1}
. On adele spaces
{\mathbb{A}}
,
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}
and
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})
, we fix
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}}
,
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}
and
\eta_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})}
to be the self-dual Haar measure with respect to the bi-characters
(x,y)\mapsto\psi(xy)
,
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto\psi({\operatorname{tr}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\alpha\bar{\beta}))
and
(X,Y)\mapsto\psi({\operatorname{tr}}(XY^{\dagger}))
, respectively. According to
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})={\mathbb{A}}T_{\theta}^{\dagger}\oplus{\mathsf{V}}^{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{A}})
, we have
{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})}(X)={\rm{d}}\eta_{\mathbb{A}}(a)\otimes{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}(\beta)
for
X=aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}\,(a\in{\mathbb{A}},\,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E})
. Through the identification
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})\ni X\mapsto\mathsf{n}(X)\in\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})
and
\beta\ni{\mathbb{A}}_{E}\mapsto\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{N}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})
, the groups
\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})
and
\mathsf{N}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})
acquire Haar measures. Thus,

(2.19)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})}f(n)\,{\rm{d}}n=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}}\int_{\mathsf{N}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})}f(\mathsf{n}(xT_{\theta})\,\iota_{\theta}(n^{\#}))\,{\rm{d}}x\,{\rm{d}}n^{\#}

for
f\in L^{1}(\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}))
, and
{\operatorname{vol}}(\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}))={\operatorname{vol}}(\mathsf{N}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{N}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}))=1
. Note that
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}
is
(\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2})^{-1}\times\prod_{p\leqslant\infty}\eta_{E_{p}}
due to the formulas
(\prod_{p}\eta_{E_{p}})({\mathbb{A}}_{E}/E)=|D|^{1/2}/2
(
[
46
, Chap V §4 Proposition 7]
) and
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}/E)=1
.

2.5.1.

Let
p\leqslant\infty
. Any element
g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is written as

(2.20)

\displaystyle g^{\#}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&\bar{\tau}a\end{smallmatrix}\right]\,k^{\#}

with
a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
,
\tau\in E_{p}^{\times}
,
\beta\in E_{p}
and
k^{\#}\in{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
. Then, our Haar measure on
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is symbolically defined as

(2.21)

\displaystyle{\rm{d}}\eta_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(g_{p}^{\#})=|a|_{p}^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}}(\beta)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}(a)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}^{\times}}(\tau)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}}(k^{\#}),

where
{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}}(k^{\#})
is the Haar measure on
{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
with volume
1
. For
p<\infty
, the Haar measure
\eta_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}
is the one with
{\operatorname{vol}}({\bf K}_{p}^{\#})=1
. On the adele group
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})
, we use the product measure of
\eta_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}
(
p\leqslant\infty
). Then,

(2.22)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})}f(g^{\#})\,{\rm{d}}g^{\#}=\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\int_{\mathsf{N}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})\times{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}\times{\bf K}^{\#}}f

\displaystyle\left(n^{\#}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&a\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\,k^{\#}\right)|a|_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}\,{\rm{d}}n^{\#}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}(a)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}}(\tau)\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#},

where
{\rm{d}}k^{\#}
is the normalized Haar measure on
{\bf K}^{\#}:=\prod_{p\leqslant\infty}{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
.

2.5.2.

We normalize
\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}}
so that
{\operatorname{vol}}({\bf K}_{\infty})=1
. Then, we normalize
\eta_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}))}
in such a way that the quotient
\eta_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}/\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}}
corresponds to the measure
(\det Z)^{-3}{\rm{d}}X{\rm{d}}Y
on the Siegel upper-half space
{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}\cong{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}
. By
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}={\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}\,{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})\cong{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
,
\eta_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})}
is defined so that
\eta_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})}|\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
is
\eta_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}}:=\eta_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}\otimes\eta_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}
. For
p<\infty
, we fix
\eta_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}
by
{\operatorname{vol}}({\bf K}_{p})=1
. Then,
\eta_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})}
is defined to be the restricted product of
\eta_{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}\,(p\leqslant\infty)
. By this Haar measure, we construct the
L^{2}
-space of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}){\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
, which contains the space
\tilde{S}_{l}({\bf K}_{0}(N))
in such a way that for a classical
\Phi\in S_{l}(N)
, the
L^{2}
-norm
\langle\tilde{\Phi}\mid\tilde{\Phi}\rangle_{L^{2}}
of its adelic lift
\tilde{\Phi}\in\tilde{S}_{l}({\bf K}_{0}(N))
(see (
1.2
)) is the same as the Petersson norm
\langle\Phi\mid\Phi\rangle
.

For
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, set

\displaystyle a_{\infty}^{(t)}:=b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}({\cosh\,}t)1_{2}&({\sinh\,}t)\,1_{2}\\
({\sinh\,}t)\,1_{2}&({\cosh\,}t)\,1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right](b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta})^{-1},

which is an element of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
representing the
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear symplectic automorphism of
W_{\mathbb{R}}:=W\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{R}}
(see §
2.2
) given by

\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\longmapsto\left[\begin{smallmatrix}({\cosh\,}t)x_{1}+(i{\sinh\,}t)\bar{x}_{2}\\
(i{\sinh\,}t)\bar{x}_{1}+({\cosh\,}t)x_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]

with respect to the basis (
2.8
). Set
A:=\{a_{\infty}^{(t)}\mid t\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
and
A^{+}=\{a_{\infty}^{(t)}\mid t\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geqslant 0}\}
.

Lemma 2.2
.

We have

\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})=\iota_{\theta}(\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}}))A^{+}b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}{\bf K}_{\infty}
. The Haar measure on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
is decomposed as

c_{\mathsf{G}}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})}(h)\,({\sinh\,}2t)^{2}{\cosh\,}2t\,{\rm{d}}t\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}}(k)

with
c_{\mathsf{G}}=2^{5}\pi^{1/2}\Gamma(3/2)^{-1}
, where
\eta_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}}
and
\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}}
are Haar measures on
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})
and on
{\bf K}_{\infty}
fixed in §
2.5.1
, and
{\rm{d}}t
is the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
J
and
C
be the
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear automorhism of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
defined as

J\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}ix_{1}\\
ix_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right],\quad C\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-i\bar{x}_{2}\\
ix_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right].

Then,
J,C\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
,
J^{2}=C^{2}=-{\rm Id}_{W_{\mathbb{R}}}
and
JC=-CJ
. Thus,
{\rm Int}(J):g\longmapsto JgJ^{-1}
and
{\rm Int}(C)g\longmapsto CgC^{-1}
are mutually commuting involutions of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
. The group
\iota_{\theta}(\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}}))
coincides with the fixed point set of
{\rm Int}(J)
, and
{\rm Int}(C)
is the Cartan involution corresponding to
b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}{\bf K}_{\infty}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})^{-1}
. Indeed,
\iota_{\theta}(\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}}))\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
is a reductive symmetric space of split rank
1
. Since
{\rm Int}(C)(a_{\infty}^{(t)})={\rm Int}(J)(a_{\infty}^{(t)})=a_{\infty}^{(-t)}
for
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, a general result (see
[
14
, Theorem 2.4]
) can be applied to get
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})=\iota_{\theta}(\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}}))Ab_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta}{\bf K}_{\infty}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})^{-1}
. The assertion on Haar measures follows from
[
14
, Theorem 2.5]
; to determine
c_{\mathsf{G}}
explicitly, an argument is in order. We use the
{\bf K}_{\infty}
-invariant inner product
2^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(X\,{}^{t}Y)
on
{\mathfrak{s}}:={\mathfrak{sp}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
. For any connected closed subgroup
L\subset\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
, we identify
{\mathfrak{l}}:={\rm Lie}(L)
with its dual
{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}
by this inner-product. We choose an invariant volume form
\Omega_{L}
on
L
so that the value
\Omega_{L}(1_{4})\in\wedge^{\dim{\mathfrak{l}}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}\cong\wedge^{\dim(\mathfrak{l})}{\mathfrak{l}}
is
\pm X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\dim(\mathfrak{l})}
for any orthonormal basis
\{X_{i}\}_{i}
of
{\mathfrak{l}}
. Let
|\Omega_{L}|
denote the Haar measure on
L
defined by
\Omega_{L}
. By using an orthonormal basis from the Iwasawa decomposition, we have that the measure
|\Omega_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}|/\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}}
on
{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}\cong{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
coincides with
(\det Z)^{-3}{\rm{d}}X{\rm{d}}Y
. Set
G_{1}^{\#}:=\{g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\mid\det g^{\#}=1\}
, which coincides with all the elements (
2.20
) with
a>0
,
k^{\#}\in({\bf K}^{\#}_{\infty})^{0}
and
\tau=a^{-1/2}
. Then,
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}={\mathsf{Z}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}\times G_{1}^{\#}
, and
({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
is a maximal compact subgroup of
G_{1}^{\#}
. Set
H:={\rm Int}((b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})^{-1})\circ\iota_{\theta}(G_{1}^{\#})
, and let
M
be the centralizer of
A
in
K_{H}:=H\cap{\bf K}_{\infty}
; it turns out that
M={\rm Int}((b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta})^{-1})\circ\iota_{\theta}(T)
with
T:=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right])\mid\tau\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1}\}
. A computation reveals the equality

(2.23)

\displaystyle|\Omega_{H}|/|\Omega_{K_{H}}|=\eta_{G_{1}^{\#}}/\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#}}

between quotient measures on
H/K_{H}\cong G_{1}^{\#}/({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
, where

{\rm{d}}\eta_{G_{1}^{\#}}(g^{\#}):=2\pi\,|a|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\beta){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}(a){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#}}(k^{\#})

for
g^{\#}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a^{-1/2}&0\\
0&a^{1/2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]k^{\#}
. Note that the factor
2\pi
arises as
{\operatorname{vol}}(T)=\int_{T}|\Omega_{T}|
(
T
is identified with its image by
{\rm Int}((b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})^{-1})\circ\iota_{\theta}
). By
({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{\circ}/T\cong{\bf S}^{\#}\subset W
(see §
2.9
), we compare
\Omega_{K_{H}}
and the Euclidean volume form on
{\bf S}^{\#}
to obtain the relation

(2.24)

\displaystyle|\Omega_{K_{H}}|/|\Omega_{T}|=2\pi^{3/2}\Gamma(3/2)^{-1}\times 2\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#}}/\eta_{T},

where
\eta_{T}
is the normalized Haar measure on
T
.
Note that
{\operatorname{vol}}({\bf S}^{\#})=2\pi^{3/2}\Gamma(3/2)^{-1}
, whereas
\int_{({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}/T}\eta_{{\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#}}/\eta_{T}=1/2
. Let
f:G_{1}^{\#}/T\times{\mathbb{R}}\to{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}
be the map
f(g^{\#},t)=(b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta})^{-1}\iota_{\theta}(g^{\#})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}a(t){\bf K}_{\infty}
with
a(t):=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}({\cosh\,}t)1_{2}&({\sinh\,}t)\,1_{2}\\
({\sinh\,}t)\,1_{2}&({\cosh\,}t)\,1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]
, and
L_{t}:g\mapsto a(t)^{-1}g
on
{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}
. Since
L_{t}\circ f(1,t,1)=1_{4}{\bf K}_{\infty}
, the tangent map
(dL_{t})_{a(t)}\circ(df)_{(1,t)}
is viewed as a linear map from
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\#}_{1}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathfrak{k}}
to
{\mathfrak{sp}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\mathfrak{k}}
. By using the root space decomposition by
{\rm Ad}(a(t))
, we see that, when
t\not=0
, the tangent map is an isomorphism and

(2.25)

\displaystyle(L_{t}\circ f)^{*}(\Omega_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}}(e))=2^{4}({\sinh\,}2t)^{2}{\cosh\,}2t\,(\Omega_{G_{1}^{\#}/T}(e)\oplus dt,

where
\Omega_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}}
is the volume form on
{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}
such that
\Omega_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}
equals the wedge product of the pull-back of
\Omega_{{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})/{\bf K}_{\infty}}
and
\Omega_{{\bf K}_{\infty}}
. We are done by (
2.23
), (
2.24
) and (
2.25
), noting
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}={\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}\times{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\iota_{\theta}({\mathsf{Z}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})^{0})={\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
. We also note
{\operatorname{vol}}(T)=2\pi
so that
c_{\mathsf{G}}=4\pi^{3/2}\Gamma(3/2)^{-1}\times 2^{4}\times{\operatorname{vol}}(T)^{-1}=2^{5}\pi^{1/2}\Gamma(3/2)^{-1}
.
∎

2.6.
Hecke algebra

Let
p
be a prime number. Let
{\mathscr{H}}_{p}:={\mathscr{H}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}{\bf K}_{p})
the subspace of bi-
{\bf K}_{p}
-invariant functions in
{\mathscr{H}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}))
.
Set
D:=\{{{\underline{d}}}=(d_{1},d_{2};d_{0})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}\mid d_{1}\geqslant d_{2}\geqslant d_{0}/2\}
. For
{\underline{d}}\in D
, set
\kappa({\underline{d}}):=\max(d_{1}-d_{2},2d_{2}-d_{0})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}
and
\varpi({\underline{d}}):={\operatorname{diag}}(p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},p^{d_{0}-d_{1}},p^{d_{0}-d_{2}})\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Due to the Cartan decomposition
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\sqcup_{{\underline{d}}\in D}{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}}){\bf K}_{p}
, the characteristic function of
{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}}){\bf K}_{p}\,({\underline{d}}\in D)
form a
{\mathbb{C}}
-basis of
{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
.
Let
\mathsf{r}:{\mathsf{Z}}\backslash\mathsf{G}\rightarrow{\bf SO}(Q)
be as in §
2.3
. The following lemma is used in §
10.2
and §
10.3
.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
{\underline{d}}\in D
. Then, for
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\bf K}_{p}
,

\mathsf{r}(k_{1}\varpi({\underline{d}})k_{2})^{-1}{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\subset p^{-(d_{1}+d_{2}-d_{0})}{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\subset p^{-2\kappa({\underline{d}})}{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).

Proof.

This follows from the fact that

\mathsf{r}(\varpi({\underline{d}}))={\operatorname{diag}}(p^{d_{1}+d_{2}-d_{0}},p^{d_{1}-d_{2}},1,p^{d_{2}-d_{1}},p^{-d_{1}-d_{2}+d_{0}})\in{\bf SO}(Q)

and that
\mathsf{r}(k)\,(k\in{\bf K}_{p})
preserves the lattice
{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.
∎

Let
S
be a finite set of prime numbers. Set
{\mathscr{H}}_{S}:=\otimes_{p\in S}{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
identified with a subspace of
{\mathscr{H}}(G_{S})
, where
G_{S}:=\prod_{p\in S}\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. For
f_{S}=\otimes_{p\in S}f_{p}
with
f_{p}\in{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
, we define

(2.26)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})

\displaystyle:=\prod_{p\in S}p^{2\kappa_{p}}

with
\kappa_{p}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
being the smallest integer such that
{\rm supp}(f_{p})\subset\cup_{\kappa({\underline{d}})\leqslant\kappa}{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}}){\bf K}_{p}
. We use the
L^{1}
-norm
\|f_{S}\|_{1}:=\int_{G_{S}}|f_{S}(h_{S})|{\rm{d}}h_{S}
on
{\mathscr{H}}_{S}
. The double coset
{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}}){\bf K}_{p}
is written
as a disjoint union of right
{\bf K}_{p}
-cosets as

(2.27)

\displaystyle{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}}){\bf K}_{p}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n}{\bf K}_{p}\mathsf{m}(\gamma_{i};\lambda_{i}\det\gamma_{i})\mathsf{n}(X_{i}),\quad n:=\#({\bf K}_{p}\backslash{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}}){\bf K}_{p})

with
\gamma_{i}\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
,
\lambda_{i}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
and
X_{i}\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Set
\rho({\underline{d}}):=2d_{1}+d_{2}-\tfrac{3}{2}d_{0}
for
{\underline{d}}\in D
. Then,

(2.28)

\displaystyle p^{2\rho({\underline{d}})-13}\leqslant n=\#({\bf K}_{p}\cap\varpi({\underline{d}})^{-1}{\bf K}_{p}\varpi({\underline{d}})\backslash{\bf K}_{p})\leqslant p^{2\rho({\underline{d}})+13},

(2.29)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\mathsf{s}}(\gamma_{i})\subset p^{-2\kappa({\underline{d}})}{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

(2.30)

\displaystyle p^{-2\kappa({\underline{d}})}\leqslant|\lambda_{i}|_{p}^{-1}\leqslant p^{2\kappa({\underline{d}})}.

(For the second inequality in (
2.28
), we refer to
[
34
, Lemma 2.13]
. We include a proof of the first inequality. Let
I
denote the Iwahori subgroup of
{\bf K}_{p}
. By the Iwahori factorization,
\varpi({\underline{d}})^{-1}I\varpi({\underline{d}})\cap{\bf K}_{p}=\varpi({\underline{d}})^{-1}I\varpi({\underline{d}})\cap I
and
\#(I\cap\varpi({\underline{d}})^{-1}I\varpi({\underline{d}})\backslash I)=p^{2\rho({\underline{d}})}
. Hence
p^{2\rho({\underline{d}})}=\#(\varpi({\underline{d}})^{-1}I\varpi({\underline{d}})\cap{\bf K}_{p}\backslash I_{p})\leqslant\#(\varpi({\underline{d}})^{-1}I\varpi({\underline{d}})\cap{\bf K}_{p}\backslash{\bf K}_{p})\leqslant[{\bf K}_{p}:I]\,n\leqslant p^{13}n
. As for (
2.30
), first by looking at similitude norms,
|\lambda_{i}\det\gamma_{i}|_{p}=p^{-d_{0}}
. By considering the
2\times 2
minors, we have
|\det\gamma_{i}|_{p}\leqslant p^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2d_{0}}
and
|\lambda_{i}^{2}\det\gamma_{i}|_{p}\leqslant p^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2d_{0}}
. From these, we get (
2.30
) easily. ) Due to the inequality
3\kappa({\underline{d}})\leqslant 2\rho({\underline{d}})
for
{\underline{d}}\in D
and (
2.28
), if
f_{S}
is the characteristic function of a single
{\bf K}_{S}
-double coset in
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{S})
, we get

(2.31)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{3/2}\prod_{p\in S}p^{-13}\leqslant\|f_{S}\|_{1}

2.7.
Bessel periods of automorphic forms

Let
\psi=\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}\psi_{p}
be a unique character of
{\mathbb{A}}
such that
\psi|{\mathbb{Q}}
is trivial and
\psi_{\infty}(x)=e^{2\pi ix}
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
. For
T\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})
, define a character
\psi_{T}:\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
by

(2.32)

\displaystyle\psi_{T}(\mathsf{n}(X)=\psi({\operatorname{tr}}(TX)),\qquad X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}).

The group
\mathsf{M}
acts rationally on
\mathsf{N}
by conjugation, hence on
{\rm{Hom}}(\mathsf{N},{\mathbb{G}}_{a})
by transport of structure. Let
\mathsf{M}^{T_{\theta}}
denotes the stabilizer of the map
\mathsf{n}(X)\mapsto{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X)
. Then,
(\mathsf{M}^{T_{\theta}})^{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}})=\{\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mid\tau\in E^{\times}\}
, where we set

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{m}(I_{\theta}(\tau),{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\tau)),\quad\tau\in E^{\times}.

The group
(\mathsf{M}^{T_{\theta}})^{\circ}\mathsf{N}
is called the Bessel subgroup for
T_{\theta}
. Any idele class character
\Lambda:{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
gives rise to an automorphic character of the Bessel subgroup as

(\mathsf{M}^{T_{\theta}})^{\circ}({\mathbb{A}})\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})\ni\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X)\longmapsto\Lambda(\tau)\psi_{T_{\theta}}(\mathsf{n}(X))\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1}.

Note that
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}})\subset(\mathsf{M}^{T_{\theta}})^{\circ}({\mathbb{A}})
. Let
F
be an automorphic form on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
with trivial central character, i.e.,
F(zg)=F(g)
for all
(z,g)\in{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}})\times\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
. Suppose that
\Lambda|{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
is trivial. Then, the global
(T_{\theta},\Lambda)
-Bessel period of
F
is defined by

(2.33)

\displaystyle B^{T_{\theta},\Lambda}(F:g):=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau)^{-1}\biggl\{\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})}F(n\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)g)\,\psi_{T_{\theta}}(n)^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}n\biggr\}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau,\quad g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}).

Note that the quotients
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E^{\times}
and
\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})
are compact.

2.8.
Godement section

Let
p\leqslant\infty
. Given characters
\Lambda_{p}:E_{p}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
and
\mu_{p}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
, let
{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})
be the smooth representation of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
induced from the character

\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&\beta\\
0&a\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\longmapsto\Lambda_{p}^{-1}(\tau)\mu_{p}^{-1}(a)|a|_{p}^{-(s+1)}
of
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Let
{\mathcal{S}}(E_{p}^{2})
denote the Schwartz-Bruhat space on
E_{p}^{2}
. For
\phi\in{\mathcal{S}}(E_{p}^{2})
, set

(2.34)

\displaystyle f_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(g^{\#}):=\mu_{p}(\det g^{\#})|\det g^{\#}|_{p}^{s+1}\int_{E_{p}^{\times}}\phi\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right]g^{\#}\right)(\Lambda\mu_{E})_{p}(\tau)|\tau\bar{\tau}|_{p}^{s+1}{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}^{\times}}(\tau)

for
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, which is absolutely convergent on the region
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>0
. When
p=\infty
, we suppose
\phi
is
{\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#}
-finite. By the local Tate’s theory, the function
s\mapsto f_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(g^{\#})
is continued meromorphically to
{\mathbb{C}}
in such a way that

(2.35)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(g^{\#}):=L_{p}(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})^{-1}\,f_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(g^{\#})

is holomorphic on
{\mathbb{C}}
. As a function of
g^{\#}
, it belongs to
{\mathcal{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})
for all regular points
s
. When both
\Lambda_{p}
and
\mu_{p}
are unramified and
\phi=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}\oplus\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}}
, then
\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}
yields a
{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
-invariant vector in
{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})
whose restriction to
{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
is the constant
1
.

Set
w_{0}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. The standard intertwining operator

M(s):{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(-s,\Lambda_{p}^{-1},\mu_{p}^{-1})

is defined on
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>0
as the absolutely convergent integral for
f\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})
:

(2.36)

\displaystyle M(s)f(g^{\#})=\int_{E_{p}}f\left(w_{0}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]g^{\#}\right)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}}(\beta),\quad g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).

The effect of
M(s)
on the section
f_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}
is described by the Fourier transform

(2.37)

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}(x,y):=\int_{E_{p}^{2}}\phi(u,v)\,\psi_{E_{p}}\left(\tfrac{xv-yu}{\sqrt{D}}\right)^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}}(u)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}}(v),\quad(x,y)\in E_{p}^{2}

as

(2.38)

\displaystyle f_{\widehat{\phi}}^{(-s,\Lambda_{p}^{-1},\mu_{p}^{-1})}(g^{\#})=|D|_{p}^{-s+1/2}\,c_{p}\,\varepsilon_{p}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E},\psi_{E_{p}})\frac{L_{p}(1-s,\Lambda^{-1}\mu_{E}^{-1})}{L_{p}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}\times M(s)f_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(g^{\#}).

with
c_{p}=1
if
p<\infty
and
c_{\infty}=\frac{|D|}{2}
if
p=\infty
, where
\varepsilon_{p}(s,\cdot,\psi_{E_{p}})
is Tate’s local
\varepsilon
-factor by the additive character
\psi_{E_{p}}:=\psi_{p}\circ{\rm tr}_{E_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
and the associated self-dual measure on
E_{p}
as usual.

2.9.
Section for the archimedean place

Recall that
\Lambda_{\infty}
is trivial. Set
{\bf S}^{\#}:=({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0})\backslash({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
; since
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}t&0\\
0&t^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mid t\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1}\}
and
({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}={\bf SU}(2)
, the manifold
{\bf S}^{\#}
is diffeomorphic to the unit sphere in
W:=\{X\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})\mid{}^{t}\bar{X}=X,\,{\operatorname{tr}}(X)=0\}\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
with the inner product
(X|Y):=2^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(XY)
, on which
({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
acts by
(X,k_{0})\mapsto{}^{t}\bar{k}_{0}Xk_{0}
. Due to
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})={\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})({\bf K}^{\#})^{0}
, by the restriction to
({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
, we have
{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})\cong C^{\infty}({\bf S}^{\#})_{0}
, the space of
({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
-finite
C^{\infty}
-functions on
{\bf S}^{\#}
; for
f_{\infty}\in C^{\infty}({\bf S}^{\#})_{0}
, let
f_{\infty}^{(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})}\in{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})
denote the inverse image of
f_{\infty}
by this map.
For each
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}
, let
(\varrho_{d},V_{d})
be the representation of
{\bf SU}(2)=({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
on the space
V_{d}
of homogeneous polynomials of degree
d
in
{\mathbb{C}}[X,Y]
(
[
16
, p.101]
), which carries a non-degenerate paring
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
given by
[
16
, (18.3.4)]
. For
d\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}
, set
\xi_{d}:=(XY)^{d/2}
, and define
\mathsf{f}_{d}(k^{\#}):=(d+1)^{1/2}{\langle\varrho_{d}(k^{\#})\xi_{d},\xi_{d}\rangle}/{\langle\xi_{d},\xi_{d}\rangle}
for
k^{\#}\in({\bf K}^{\#}_{\infty})^{0}
. Since
\xi_{d}\in V_{d}
is fixed by
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap({\bf K}^{\#}_{\infty})^{0}

, the function
\mathsf{f}_{d}
descend to a function on
{\bf S}^{\#}
. The
{\mathbb{C}}
-span of
\mathsf{f}_{d}\,(d\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0})
coincides with the
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
-fixed vectors of
C^{\infty}({\bf S}^{\#})_{0}
(
cf
.
[
40
, §4.4]
).
Define
\phi_{\infty}^{(d)}\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})
by

(2.39)

\displaystyle\phi_{\infty}^{(d)}(x,y):=\sum_{j=0}^{d/2}\tfrac{(x\bar{x})^{j}}{(j!)^{2}}\tfrac{(-y\bar{y})^{d/2-j}}{((d/2-j)!)^{2}}\,\tfrac{2}{\pi}\exp\left(-\tfrac{2\pi}{\sqrt{|D|}}(x\bar{x}+y\bar{y})\right),\quad(x,y)\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}.

By (
2.34
) and (
2.35
),

(2.40)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{\phi_{\infty}^{(d)}}^{(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})}=|D|^{\frac{s+1}{2}}(-1)^{d/2}\tfrac{\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+d/2+1)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+1)}\,\mathsf{f}_{d}^{(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})}.

In particular,
|D|^{-\frac{s+1}{2}}\mathsf{f}_{\phi_{\infty}^{(0)}}^{(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})}
corresponds to the constant
1\in C^{\infty}({\bf S}^{\#})_{0}
.

2.10.
Eisenstein series and Rankin-Selberg integrals

Let
\Lambda=\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}\Lambda_{p}\in\widehat{{\rm Cl}(E)}
and
\mu=\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}\mu_{p}
a character of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
of finite order. For a section
f^{(s)}
of the adelic principal series
\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})
such that
f^{(s)}|{\bf K}^{\#}
is
{\bf K}^{\#}
-finite and meromorphic, define the Eisenstein series

{\rm Eis}(f^{(s)};g^{\#}):=\widehat{L}(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})\times\sum_{\delta\in{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}})}f^{(s)}(\delta g^{\#}),\quad g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}),

which is absolutely convergent on
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1
and has a meromorphic continuation to
{\mathbb{C}}
. Let
\phi=\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}\phi_{p}
be a decomposable element of
{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2})
such that
\phi_{p}=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}\oplus\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}}
for almost all
p<\infty
. Define

(2.41)

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(g^{\#}):=\prod_{p\leqslant\infty}\mathsf{f}_{\phi_{p}}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(g_{p}^{\#}),\quad g^{\#}=(g_{p}^{\#})_{p}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}),

which is a
{\bf K}^{\#}
-finite holomorphic section of the adelic principal series
\otimes_{p\leqslant\infty}{\mathscr{V}}^{\#}(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})
. Let

E(\phi,s,\Lambda,\mu;g^{\#}):={\rm Eis}(\mathsf{f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}_{\phi};g^{\#}),\quad g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})

be the associated Eisenstein series, which is known to be holomorphic except for possible simple poles at
s=\pm 1
and is vertically bounded; these poles occur if and only if
\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1}
. For
\phi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2})
, let
\widehat{\phi}
be the Fourier transform of
\phi
with respect to the self-dual Haar measure
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2}}:=\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}\otimes\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}
on
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2}
:

(2.42)

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}(x,y):=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2}}\phi(u,v)\psi\left({\operatorname{tr}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\tfrac{yu-xv}{\sqrt{D}}\right)\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2}}(u,v),\quad(x,y)\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{2}.

Thus, when
\phi=\otimes_{p}\phi_{p}
, its adelic Fourier transform (
2.42
) is related to the product of the local Fourier transforms (
2.37
) as
\widehat{\phi}=\frac{4}{|D|}\otimes_{p}\widehat{\phi}_{p}
due to the relation between
\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}}
and
\otimes_{p}\eta_{E_{p}}
mentioned in §
2.5.1
. We have the functional equation

(2.43)

\displaystyle E(\widehat{\phi},-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})=E(\phi,s,\Lambda,\mu).

Let
\varphi
be a cusp form on
{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{A}})\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
. For
f_{\infty}\in C^{\infty}({\bf S}^{\#})_{0}
,
\phi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{2})
, and for
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
, define

(2.44)

\displaystyle Z_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(f_{\infty}\mid\varphi,g)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathsf{Z}}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})}{\rm Eis}(f_{\infty}^{(s,\Lambda_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})}\otimes\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda_{\rm{\bf{f}}},\mu_{\rm{\bf{f}}})};h^{\#})\varphi(\iota_{\theta}(h^{\#}g))\,{\rm{d}}h^{\#}.

Due to the cuspidality of
\varphi
, this converges absolutely to define a holomorphic function on any domain where the Eisenstein series is regular.

3.
Smoothed Rankin-Selberg integral: the spectral side

3.1.
Shintani functions

The complex power function
z^{\alpha}\,(z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},\alpha\in{\mathbb{C}})
is defined as

z^{\alpha}:=|z|^{\alpha}\exp(i\alpha{\rm Arg}(z)),\quad{\rm Arg}(z)\in(-\pi,\pi].

Then,
(z,\alpha)\mapsto z^{\alpha}
is holomorphic on
({\mathbb{C}}-{\mathbb{R}}_{\leqslant 0})\times{\mathbb{C}}
and

(3.1)

\displaystyle(z^{-1})^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=z^{-\alpha},\quad({\operatorname{Im}}(z)\not=0),

(3.2)

\displaystyle(zw)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=z^{\alpha}w^{\alpha},\quad\text{if ${\rm Arg}(z)+{\rm Arg}(w)\in(-\pi,\pi]$,}

(3.3)

\displaystyle|z^{\alpha}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\exp\left(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|\right)\,|z|^{{\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha)}\quad({\operatorname{Re}}(z)>0).

For
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
, a character
\mu_{\infty}:{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}
and
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
, set

(3.4)

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g):=J(g,i1_{2})^{-l}\mu_{\infty}(\nu(g))\,\left({\rm sgn}(\nu(g))\frac{{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}\,g\langle i1_{2}\rangle)}{i\sqrt{|D|}}\right)^{s-l+1},\quad g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}}).

Lemma 3.1
.

The function
\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}:\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
is
C^{\infty}
and

(3.5)

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(gk)

\displaystyle=J(k,i1_{2})^{-l}\,\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g),\quad(k\in{\bf K}_{\infty},\,g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})),

(3.6)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{X}}\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g)

\displaystyle=0,\qquad X\in{\mathfrak{p}}^{+},\,g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}}),

(3.7)

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})

\displaystyle=1.

For each
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
, the function
s\mapsto\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g)
is holomorphic and vertically bounded on
{\mathbb{C}}
. Moreover, it has the following equivariance with respect to the left
\iota({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}}))
action:

(3.8)

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}\left(\iota\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&\beta\\
0&a\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\,g\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{\infty}(a)|a|^{-(s+1)}\,\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g),\quad(\tau\in{\mathbb{C}},a\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times},\beta\in{\mathbb{C}},g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})).

Proof.

Since
{\operatorname{Im}}(Z)
for
Z\in{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
is positive definite, by
{}^{t}A_{\theta}A_{\theta}=T_{\theta}
,
{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}Z))={\operatorname{tr}}({}^{t}A_{\theta}{\operatorname{Im}}(Z)A_{\theta})>0
, which implies
{\operatorname{Re}}\left({{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}\,Z)}/{(i\sqrt{|D|})}\right)>0
for
Z\in{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
. Thus,
(s,Z)\longmapsto\left({{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}\,Z)}/({i\sqrt{|D|}})\right)^{s-l+1}
is holomorphic on
{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
. Since
{\rm sgn}(\nu(g))\,g\langle i1_{2}\rangle\in{\mathfrak{h}}_{2}
, we obtain (
3.5
) and (
3.7
) as well as the holomorphicity of
\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g)
in
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
. The remaining formulas are confirmed by a direct computation.
∎

We consider (
2.44
) for
g=a_{\infty}^{(t)}b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}
. The group
b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}\iota_{\theta}({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap{\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})^{-1}
is contained in
\mathsf{M}({\mathbb{R}})
, so that it is in the centralizer of
A
. Then, by
[
20
, Lemma 2.2]
, we have
Z_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(R(k^{\#})f_{\infty}\mid\varphi,a_{\infty}^{(t)}b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})=Z^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}_{\phi}(f_{\infty}\mid\varphi,a_{\infty}^{(t)}b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
for all
k^{\#}\in{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
. We have
Z^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}_{\phi}(f_{\infty}\mid\varphi,a_{\infty}^{(t)}b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})=0
unless
f_{\infty}
is right
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{R}})\cap({\bf K}_{\infty}^{\#})^{0}
-invariant, i.e.,
f_{\infty}
is a zonal spherical function on
{\bf S}^{\#}
. As such,
f_{\infty}
is in the linear span of
\mathsf{f}_{d}\,(d\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0})
(see §
2.9
). Define

(3.9)
