{\bf K}_{p}\varpi(1,1;2){\bf K}_{p}
, respectively. Note that
\varpi(1,1;2)=p1_{4}\in{\mathsf{Z}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. We identify the dual
(\widehat{\mathsf{G}}({\mathbb{C}}),\widehat{\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{C}}),\widehat{\mathsf{T}}({\mathbb{C}}))
with
(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathsf{T}}({\mathbb{C}}))
as usual. Let a general point of
{\bf T}({\mathbb{C}})
as
\check{t}={\operatorname{diag}}(p^{-s_{1}},p^{-s_{2}},p^{-s_{0}+s_{1}},p^{-s_{0}+s_{2}})
with
(s_{0},s_{1},s_{2})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{3}
, and define a character
\chi_{\check{t}}:{\bf B}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
as
\chi_{\check{t}}({\operatorname{diag}}(t_{1},t_{2},\lambda t_{1}^{-1},\lambda t_{2}^{-1})n)=|t_{1}|_{p}^{-s_{1}-s_{2}+s_{0}}|t_{2}|_{p}^{-s_{1}+s_{2}}|\lambda|_{p}^{s_{1}}
. Let
\pi_{\check{t}}
denote the unique irreducible
{\bf K}_{p}
-spherical subquotient of
{\rm Ind}_{{\bf B}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}^{\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(\chi_{\check{t}})
;
\check{t}\pmod{W}
is the Satake parameter of
\pi_{\check{t}}
. Note that the central character of
\pi_{\check{t}}
is trivial if and only of
\check{t}\in{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
; such
\check{t}
is dentified with an element of
({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2}/W
under the map

({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{2}/W\ni(a,b)\mapsto{\operatorname{diag}}(a,b,a^{-1},b^{-1})\in{\bf T}({\mathbb{C}})\cap{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})/W
(see §
1.1
). The spherical Fourier transform
\widehat{f_{p}}
of
f_{p}\in{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
is defined to be the eigenvalue of
\pi_{\check{t}}(f)
on
(\pi_{\check{t}})^{{\bf K}_{p}}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
. Then, by
[
31
]
, the map
f_{p}\mapsto\widehat{f_{p}}
(
cf
. §
1.1
) yields a
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra isomorphism from
{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
onto
{\mathbb{C}}[p^{-s_{0}},p^{-s_{1}},p^{-s_{2}}]^{W}
; moreover,
\{X_{j}:=\widehat{c_{j}}\mid j=0,1,2\}
yields an algebraically independent generator of the
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra
{\mathbb{C}}[p^{-s_{0}},p^{-s_{1}},p^{-s_{2}}]^{W}
.

Definition 1
.

For
{\bf k}=(k_{0},k_{1},k_{2})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{3}
, let
f_{p,{\bf k}}\in{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
be the inverse image of the monomial
X_{0}^{k_{0}}X_{1}^{k_{1}}X_{2}^{k_{2}}\in{\mathbb{C}}[X_{0},X_{1},X_{2}]
by the Fourier transform.

Lemma 7.1
.

\|f_{p,{\bf k}}\|_{1}\leqslant p^{17(k_{1}+k_{2})}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{p,{\bf k}})\leqslant p^{\frac{34}{3}(k_{1}+k_{2})+\frac{26}{3}}

for
{\bf k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{3}
.

Proof.

We have
\|f*f_{1}\|_{1}\leqslant\|f\|_{1}\|f_{1}\|_{1}
for any
f,f_{1}\in{\mathscr{H}}_{p}
. Applying this inequality successively to
f_{p,{\bf k}}=c_{0}^{*k_{0}}*c_{1}^{*k_{1}}*c_{2}^{*k_{2}}
, and using (
2.28
), we get

\|f_{p,{\bf k}}\|_{1}\leqslant\|c_{0}\|_{1}^{k_{0}}\|c_{1}\|_{1}^{k_{1}}\|c_{2}\|_{1}^{k_{2}}\leqslant 1^{k_{0}}\times(p^{16})^{k_{1}}\times(p^{17})^{k_{2}}\leqslant p^{17(k_{1}+k_{2})}.

For the second claim, use (
2.31
).
∎

Let
D<0
,
\Lambda
,
\mu
and
M
be as in Theorem
1.2
, so that
\mu^{2}={\bf 1}
. Let
N
be a prime number such that
N\nmid DM
and
p\nmid DMN
. Let
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 7}
be fixed.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
{\bf k}=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{0})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{3}
.Then, as
N\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\frac{(1+N^{-2})^{-1}}{(\log Nl)^{\delta(\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1})}[{\bf Sp}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}):\Gamma_{0}^{(2)}(N)]}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{(E,\Lambda)}(l,N){}^{\rm G,new}}\widehat{f_{p,{\bf k}}}\,(y_{p}(\pi))\,{L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi,\mu\right)}\,\omega^{\Phi_{\pi}^{0}}_{l,D,\Lambda^{-1}}{\mathfrak{t}}(\pi,\mu)

\displaystyle=2{{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\Lambda}_{p}(\widehat{f_{p,{\bf k}}})\begin{cases}L(1,\kappa_{D}),\quad&(\Lambda\mu_{E}={\bf 1}),\\
L(1,{\mathcal{A}I}(\Lambda)\times\mu),\quad&(\Lambda\mu_{E}\not={\bf 1}),\end{cases}

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\varepsilon}(N^{-2}p^{a_{1}(k_{1}+k_{2})+b_{1}}+N^{-2l+10+\varepsilon}p^{a_{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})+b_{2}}+N^{-l+12+\varepsilon}p^{a_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2})+b_{3}})

\displaystyle\qquad+O(N^{\frac{-3}{2}}p^{17(k_{1}+k_{2})}).

where
a_{i},b_{i}(i=1,2,3)
are positive constants depending only on
l
.

Proof.

From Theorem
6.11
combined with
[
20
, Theorem 5.4]
, we obtain the formula with the error term (
6.15
) (
\sigma=2+\varepsilon
) and
O(N^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f_{p,{\bf k}}\|_{1})
which comes from the Saito-Kurokawa representations and the Yoshida type representations. By Lemma
7.1
, the error term amounts to a sum of terms of the form
p^{A}N^{-B}\,(A\in{\mathbb{R}},B>0)
as in (
7.1
). Note that
l>12
is necessary for the
O
-terms to decay to
0
.
∎

Like analogous theorems in
[
19
]
and
[
4
]
, our Theorem
7.2
can be viewed as a weighted version of the quantitative automorphic density theorem for
{\bf PGSp}_{2}
(
[
34
]
,
[
21
]
).
In this article, we do not make any attempt to improve the error term. Instead, we shall content ourselves with one particular but interesting arithmetic application of Theorem
7.2
; for this purpose, the error term of the form in (
7.1
) is good enough. For the notion of field of rationality of automorphic representations, we refer to
[
3
]
and
[
35
]
.

Theorem 7.3
.

There exist constants
N_{p}>DM
and
C_{p}>0
with the following properties: For any prime number
N>N_{p}
, there exists

\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)^{{\rm G},{\rm new}}
such that

•

L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi\times\mu\right)\not=0
and
L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi\right)L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi,\kappa_{D}\right)\not=0
,

•

[{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi):{\mathbb{Q}}]\geqslant C_{p}\sqrt{\log\log N}
, where
{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi)
is the field of rationality of
\pi
.

Proof.

We follow the argument described in
[
39
, §3.2]
.
We consider the set

{\mathcal{Y}}(p,N):=\{y_{p}(\pi)\in[Y_{p}^{0}]\mid\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,{\bf 1}}(l,N)^{{\rm G},{\rm new}},\,L(1/2,\pi,\mu)\not=0\}

and estimate its cardinality
\#{\mathcal{Y}}(p,N)
from above and from below. We have that
\#{\mathcal{Y}}(p,N)
is bounded by
O((8p^{A})^{3d^{2}})
with
A>0
being a constant (depending on
l
) and

d:=\max\{[{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi):{\mathbb{Q}}]\mid\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,{\bf 1}}(l,N)^{{\rm G},{\rm new}},\,L(1/2,\pi,\mu)\not=0\}.

At this step, it is critical to use the integrality of the complex numbers
p^{2l-3}{\widehat{c}_{1}}(y_{p}(\pi),1)
and
p^{2(2l-3)}{\widehat{c}_{2}}(y_{p}(\pi),1)
, which follows from the integrality of arithmetically normalized Hecke operators
T(p)
and
T_{1,1}(p)
(
[
39
, §2.3]
) on
S_{l}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
(
[
39
, Theorem 1.2]
). We also use that
the map
[Y_{p}^{0}]\ni(a,b)\mapsto(p^{2l-3}{\widehat{c}_{1}}(a,b,1)
and
p^{2(2l-3)}{\widehat{c}_{2}}(a,b,1))\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
is injective. To have a lower bound, we need a version of
[
35
, Lemma 6.16]
for
{\bf PGSp}_{2}
with the Plancherel measure
\mu_{p}^{\rm Pl}
therein being replaced with our
{\mathfrak{m}}_{p}^{\Lambda,\mu}
; the same proof works since the Lebesgue measure on
(i{\mathbb{R}})^{2}/W\,(\cong[Y_{p}^{0}])
is absolutely continuous with respect to
{\mathfrak{m}}_{p}^{\Lambda,\mu}
. Then, we argue in the same way as
[
39
, §3.2]
, using Theorem
7.2
instead of
[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
, to have the lower bound
\#{\mathcal{Y}}(p,N)\gg_{p}(\log N)^{2}
. ∎

We need the notion of regular-algebraicity of irreducible cuspidal representations of
{\bf GL}_{n}
in the sense of
[
3
, Definition 3.12]
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)^{{\rm G},{\rm new}}
and
\sigma\in{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{C}})
. Let
\psi=\pi^{{\rm GL}}\boxtimes 1_{{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})}
be the global Arthur parameter of
\pi
, where
\pi^{{\rm GL}}
is an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
{\bf GL}_{4}({\mathbb{A}})
of symplectic type.

(i)

We have
{}^{\sigma}\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)^{{\rm G},{\rm new}}
. When
\Lambda=\chi\circ{\rm N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}
for some finite order character
\chi
of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
, we have
{}^{\sigma}\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\sigma\circ\Lambda}(l,N)^{{\rm G},{\rm new}}
.

(ii)

We have that
\pi^{{\rm GL}}
is regular algebraic, and
{}^{\sigma}\psi:={}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}\boxtimes 1_{{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})}
is the global Arhtur parameter of
{}^{\sigma}\pi
.

(iii)

We have
{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi)={\mathbb{Q}}(\pi^{\rm GL})
, and that the JPSS conductor of
\pi^{{\rm GL}}
is
N^{2}
.

(iv)

For any finite order character
\eta
of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
,
L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\pi,\eta\right)\not=0
if and only if
L\left(\tfrac{1}{2},{}^{\sigma}\pi,\sigma\circ\eta\right)\not=0.

Proof.

By definition,
{}^{\sigma}\pi=\pi_{\infty}\otimes{}^{\sigma}\pi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
, so that
\pi_{\infty}={}^{\sigma}\pi_{\infty}
is a HDS of weight
l
. Due to Shimura, the space of
\Phi\in S_{l}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
with rational Fourier coefficients is a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-structure of the
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector space
S_{l}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
. Thus,
S_{l}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
has a
\sigma
-conjugate linear action;
{}^{\sigma}\pi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
occurs in the
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
-module generated by the
\sigma
-conjugate of the adelic lift to
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
of
\Phi_{\pi}^{0}\in S_{l}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
. This shows
{}^{\sigma}\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)
. For any open compact subgroup
K\subset\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
, it is obvious that
\pi_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{K}\not=0
if and only if
({}^{\sigma}\pi_{\rm{\bf{f}}})^{K}\not=0
. From this,
{}^{\sigma}\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}(l,N)^{\rm new}
.
Due to the identification of the archimedean parameter (
cf
.
[
28
, Formula (243)]
),
\pi^{{\rm GL}}
is seen to be regular algebraic of infinite type
p_{\infty}(\pi^{\rm GL})=(l,2,1,3-l)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}/{\mathfrak{S}}_{4}
(
[
3
, §3.3, Definition 3.6]
). For
\sigma\in{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{C}})
,
{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}:=\pi^{{\rm GL}}_{\infty}\otimes{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
is also cuspidal automorphic representation (
[
3
, Théorème 3.13]
); moreover, by Jacquet-Shalika’s criterion for a cuspidal automorphic representation of
{\bf GL}_{2n}
to be of symplectic type (
[
18
, Theorem 1]
) and by the theorem in the appendix of
[
11
]
,
{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}
is also of symplectic type. Thus, we have a global
A
-parameter
{}^{\sigma}\psi:={}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}\boxtimes{\bf 1}_{{\bf SL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})}
of
\mathsf{G}
. We claim that
{}^{\sigma}\pi
is of general type belonging to the
A
-packet
\Pi_{{}^{\sigma}\psi}
. Indeed, for large enough
S
, the coincidence of unramified local
p
-factors
L(s,\pi_{p})=L(s,\pi_{p}^{{\rm GL}})
for
p\not\in S
implies the equality
L(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p})=L(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p}^{\rm{GL}})
(
cf
,
[
28
, Lemma 5.3.1]
). Thus,
L^{S}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi)=L^{S}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}})
. On the other hand, since
{}^{\sigma}\pi
is a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}})
, it belongs to some global
A
-packet
\Pi_{\psi^{\prime}}
with a global
A
-parameter
\psi^{\prime}
. For sufficiently larger
S
, the partial
L
-function
L^{S}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi)
coincides with the standard partial
L
-function
L^{S}(s,\xi)
of an isobaric automorphic representation
\xi
of
{\bf GL}_{4}({\mathbb{A}})
determined by
\psi^{\prime}
. Thus,
L^{S}(s,{}^{\sigma}\Pi)=L^{S}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi)=L^{S}(s,\xi)
. By
[
17
, Theorem (4.4)]
, we conclude that
{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}=\xi
, i.e.,
{}^{\sigma}\psi=\psi^{\prime}
, which in particular means that
{}^{\sigma}\pi
is also of general type.

It remains to show that
{}^{\sigma}\pi
has a global
(E,\sigma\circ\Lambda)
-Bessel model. At this point, we use the assumption that
\Lambda
is of the form
\chi\circ{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}
, so that
\widehat{L}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi\times{\mathcal{A}I}(\sigma\circ\Lambda))=\widehat{L}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi^{\rm GL}\times\sigma\circ\chi)\widehat{L}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\sigma\circ\chi\kappa_{D})
for all
\sigma\in{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{C}})
. By
[
8
, Corollary 1.1]
, we have
L(1/2,\pi\times{\mathcal{A}I}(\Lambda))\not=0
and
\pi_{v}
has local
(E_{v},\Lambda_{v})
-Bessel model for all
v
. Thus,
\widehat{L}(1/2,\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\chi)\widehat{L}(1/2,\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\chi\kappa_{D})\not=0
. By
[
11
, Theorem 7.1.2]
, there exists a system of elements
\Omega({}^{\sigma}\pi)\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi^{{\rm GL}})^{\times}
such that, for any finite order
\eta\in\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}
,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\sigma\left(\frac{\widehat{L}(1/2,\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\eta)}{G(\tilde{\eta})^{2}\,\Omega(\pi)}\right)=\frac{\widehat{L}(1/2,{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\sigma\circ\eta)}{G(\sigma\circ\tilde{\eta})^{2}\,\Omega({}^{\sigma}\pi)},\quad\sigma\in{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{C}})

By this, we have
\widehat{L}(1/2,{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\sigma\circ\chi)\widehat{L}(1/2,{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}\times\sigma\circ\chi\kappa_{D})=\widehat{L}(1/2,{}^{\sigma}\pi\times{\mathcal{A}I}(\sigma\circ\Lambda))\not=0
. For
p<\infty
, there is a natural
\sigma
-linear
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-intertwining bijection
B_{p}\mapsto{}^{\sigma}B_{p}
from the space of
(E_{p},\Lambda_{p})
-Bessel functions on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
onto the space of
(E_{p},\sigma\circ\Lambda_{p})
-Bessel functions (
cf
.
[
12
, §3.3.1]
). If
V(\pi_{p})
is the
(E_{p},\Lambda_{p})
-local Bessel model of
\pi_{p}
, then its
\sigma
-conjugate image
{}^{\sigma}(V(\pi_{p}))
is the
(E_{p},\sigma\circ\Lambda_{p})
-Bessel model of
{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p}
. Thus, by
[
8
, Corollary 1.1]
once again,
{}^{\sigma}\pi
admits a global
(E,\sigma\circ\Lambda)
-Bessel model.
This complete the proof of (i) and (ii). For the rest of the proof, we address (iii) and (iv). Due to (ii), for
\sigma\in{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{C}})
,
{}^{\sigma}\pi\cong\pi
implies
{}^{\sigma}\psi=\psi
, or equivalently
{}^{\sigma}(\pi^{\rm{GL}})=\pi^{{\rm GL}}
. Let us show the converse implication. Suppose that
{}^{\sigma}\pi^{{\rm GL}}=\pi^{{\rm GL}}
. Since
\pi\in\Pi_{\rm cusp}^{E,\Lambda}(l,N)^{\rm new}
, we know that the completed
L
-function
\widehat{L}(s,\pi)
defined by Piatetski-Schapiro coincides with the principal
L
-function
\widehat{L}(s,\pi^{\rm GL})
(
[
20
, §6]
). Thus (iv) is immediate from (
7.2
). By (i) and (ii), we also have
\widehat{L}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi)=\widehat{L}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi)
and
\pi_{p}\cong{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p}
for almost all
p
. Thus,
\widehat{L}(s,\pi_{p})=\widehat{L}(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p})
for all
p<\infty
by
[
32
, Lemma 3.1.1]
. As we recalled in
[
20
, §4.3]
,
\pi_{p}
and
{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p}
are representations of type IIIa, which have degree
2
local
L
-factors, or representations of type IVb, which have degree
1
local
L
-factors. Noting this, we
get
\pi_{p}\cong{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p}
from
L(s,\pi_{p})=L(s,{}^{\sigma}\pi_{p})
as in the proof of
[
32
, Lemma 3.1.2]
. Therefore,
\pi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\cong{}^{\sigma}\pi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
as desired. Thus,
{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi)={\mathbb{Q}}(\pi^{{\rm GL}})
. In
[
20
, §4.6]
, we established the functional equation
\widehat{L}(s,\pi)=N^{1-2s}\widehat{L}(1-s,\pi)
. Since
\widehat{L}(s,\pi)=\widehat{L}(s,\pi^{{\rm GL}})
, by comparing this with
\widehat{L}(s,\pi^{{\rm GL}})=\varepsilon N(\pi^{{\rm GL}})^{1/2-s}\widehat{L}(1-s,\pi^{{\rm GL}})
, we get
N^{2}=N(\pi^{{\rm GL}})
, where
N(\pi^{{\rm GL}})
is the JPSS-conductor of
\pi^{{\rm GL}}
. ∎

We deal with
\Lambda\in{\widehat{{\rm Cl}(E)}}
in this work; however, this proof works on a general
\Lambda
having ramifications. Corollary
1.4
follows from Theorem
7.3
applied to
(\Lambda,\mu)=({\bf 1},\kappa_{M})
and Lemma
7.4
.

8.
Deduction of the main terms

In this section, we prove Propositions
6.1
and
6.7
. Let
\phi=\otimes_{p<\infty}\phi_{p}\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{2})
and
f=\otimes_{p<\infty}\in{\mathscr{H}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}))
; set
\Phi=\widehat{\bf\Phi_{\phi,f,l}}^{(\beta,\Lambda,\mu)}
.

8.1.
The term
{\mathbb{J}}_{1_{4}}(\Phi)

We start from (
5.3
). Substituting (
3.13
) and formally changing the order of integrals, we find the following formula, whose proof will be given at the end of this section.

(8.1)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{J}}_{1_{4}}(\Phi)=\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}\beta(s)s(s^{2}-1)\widehat{L}(1+s,\Lambda\mu_{E})\{{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(1_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}({\sigma})\}\,{\rm{d}}s

with
c>1
, where, for
\delta\in\{1_{4},\sigma\}
with
\sigma
being as in (
2.5
), we set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta):=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau){\bf\Phi}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\,\psi(aD/2)\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau.

Since
\Lambda
is trivial on
E_{\infty}^{\times}
and since
{\operatorname{vol}}(E_{\infty}^{\times}/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times})=\pi
, the integral
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)
is factored to the product of

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau){\Phi}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}))\,\psi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}(aD/2)\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle:=\pi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\Phi}_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\,\exp(\pi iaD)\,{\rm{d}}a.

Lemma 8.1
.

For any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

(8.2)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}|\Phi_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}))|\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\ll 1,\quad s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I},

so that the integral
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)
defines an entire function on
{\mathbb{C}}
, which is vertically bounded. Moreover, for
(\phi,f)
as in §
6.2
(i) and (ii),

(8.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)=

\displaystyle C_{\delta}(\Lambda)\,\frac{|D|h_{D}}{8w_{D}}\,\tilde{\mu}(\det\delta)\tilde{\mu}(N)\,N^{s-1}\,M^{s-6}

\displaystyle\times\quad\zeta_{M}(1)\zeta_{M}(4)G(\tilde{\mu})\tilde{\mu}^{-1}(2)\tilde{\mu}(D)\prod_{p\in S}\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda_{p}^{\delta})}}(f_{p};s),

where
C_{\delta}(\Lambda)
is
\delta_{\Lambda^{2},{\bf 1}}
if
\delta=1_{2}
and is
1
if
\delta=\sigma
.

Proof.

For
\delta\in\{1_{4},\sigma\}
and
\tau\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E}
, set
\tau^{\delta}:=\tau
if
\delta=1_{4}
and
\tau^{\delta}:=\bar{\tau}
if
\delta=\sigma
; then, by (
2.6
),
\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)^{-1}=\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau^{\delta})
for
\tau\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}
. Using this on the way, by (
3.10
) and by the Iwasawa decomposition on
\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(see §
2.5.1
), we have that
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)
equals

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\int_{(a,\beta)\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}\int_{\tau\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau)\,\psi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}(-X_{\beta}+aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})))

\displaystyle\times\Bigl\{\int_{(\lambda,\tau_{1},k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}\times{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}}\Lambda^{-1}(\tau_{1})\mu^{-1}(\lambda)|\lambda|_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{-s+1}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})

\displaystyle\quad\times f(\iota_{\theta}(k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})^{-1}\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau_{1}^{-1}\tau^{\delta})\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(-X_{\beta}+aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}))\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau_{1}\,{\rm{d}}k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}\lambda\Bigr\}\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}\beta{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau,

where
{\rm{d}}\beta
on
{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}
is the product of self-dual Haar measures on
E_{p}
for
p<\infty
with respect to the bi-character
\psi_{p}({\operatorname{tr}}_{E_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\alpha\bar{\beta}))
. Since
{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}
is compact, we see that the integral in (
8.2
) is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\int_{(a,\beta,\lambda,\tau_{1},k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})}|\lambda|_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}|f(\iota_{\theta}(k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau_{1})\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(X_{\beta}+aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}))|\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau_{1}\,{\rm{d}}k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}\beta.

Since
{\rm supp}(f)\subset\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
is compact, by
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})={\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\mathsf{P}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
, the domain of integral is restricted to a compact set of
(a,\beta,\lambda,\tau_{1},k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})
. From this remark, the estimation (
8.2
) is evident. By (
2.4
), we see that
{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}(X):=|D/2|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{1/2}{\rm{d}}\beta\,{\rm{d}}a
for
X=aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}\,(a\in{\mathbb{A}}_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}},\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})
is the self-dual Haar measure on
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
with respect to the bi-character
\psi_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}(XY^{\dagger})
. The group
\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})\cong{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
is endowed with the Haar measure
{\rm{d}}X
such that
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathsf{V}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}))=1
, which coincides with
\prod_{p<\infty}[{\mathcal{Q}}_{p}:{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]^{1/2}\times{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}(X)
. Note that
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{p}:{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]=2^{\delta_{p,2}}
. Hence
{\rm{d}}\beta\,{\rm{d}}a=2^{-1/2}|D/2|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-1/2}\,{\rm{d}}X
. By making a change of variables
\tau_{1}\mapsto\tau_{1}(\tau^{\delta})^{-1}
on the way,
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)
equals

\displaystyle\frac{|D|}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau)\Lambda^{-1}((\tau^{\delta})^{-1})\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\int_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}\times{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}}\Lambda^{-1}(\tau_{1})\mu^{-1}(\lambda)|\lambda|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-s+1}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})}f(\iota_{\theta}(k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#})^{-1}\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau_{1}^{-1})\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(X))\,\psi_{\rm{\bf{f}}}(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))\,{\rm{d}}X\biggr\}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau_{1}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda\,{\rm{d}}k_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}.

We have that
E_{\infty}^{\times}/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\cong{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
is a closed subgroup of
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
; these two groups are naturally regarded as measure spaces. The measure on
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}\cong({\mathbb{A}}_{E}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times})/(E_{\infty}^{\times}/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times})
is the quotient measure. Then, by
{\operatorname{vol}}(E_{\infty}^{\times}/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times})=\pi
and (
2.18
), we have
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times})=\frac{\pi h_{D}}{2w_{D}}\times\frac{1}{\pi}=\frac{h_{D}}{2w_{D}}
. Hence, the
\tau
-integral of
\Lambda(\tau)\Lambda^{-1}((\tau^{\delta})^{-1})
is equal to
C_{\delta}(\Lambda)\,\frac{h_{D}}{2w_{D}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle=C_{\delta}(\Lambda)\frac{|D|h_{D}}{8w_{D}}\prod_{p<\infty}{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi_{p},f_{p}}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(\delta),

where
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi_{p},f_{p}}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(\delta)
is the local orbital integral defined by (
11.1
). For
p\not\in S
such that
p\nmid NM
, we easily have
{\mathscr{W}}_{p}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda_{p}^{-1})}(f_{p};\mathsf{m}(1_{2},\lambda))=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}(\lambda)
, which yields
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi_{p},f_{p}}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(\delta)=\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda_{p})}}(f_{p};s,\mu_{p})=1
. For
p\in S
, we have
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi_{p},f_{p}}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(\delta)=\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda_{p}^{\delta})}}(f_{p};s,\mu_{p})
. We apply formula
11.2
for
p\mid M
and Proposition
11.2
for
p\mid N
to have that
\prod_{p\mid NM}{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi_{p},f_{p}}^{(s,\Lambda_{p},\mu_{p})}(\delta)
equals

\displaystyle\prod_{p\mid N}\mu_{p}(p)p^{s-1}\,\prod_{p\mid M}|M|_{p}^{-(s-11/2)}(1+p^{-2})^{-1}\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(1)\zeta_{E_{p}}(1)\mu_{p}(2^{-1}D\det\delta)W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu_{p})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mu}(\det\delta)\tilde{\mu}(N)N^{s-1}\,M^{s-11/2}\zeta_{M}(1)\zeta_{M}(4)\tilde{\mu}(2)^{-1}\tilde{\mu}(D)\,M^{-1/2}G(\tilde{\mu}),

where the second expression is obtained by
\zeta_{E_{p}}(1)=(1-p^{-2})^{-1}
for
p\mid M
and by
\prod_{p\mid M}W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu)=M^{-1/2}G(\tilde{\mu})
and
\prod_{p\mid L}\mu_{p}(L)=\tilde{\mu}(L)
for any
L\in{\mathbb{Z}}
relatively prime to
M
. ∎

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
0<c<l-1
. Then, for
\delta\in\{1_{2},\sigma\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})|\,{\rm{d}}a\ll_{c}1,\quad{\operatorname{Re}}(s)=c.

For
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)<l-2
, we have

(8.4)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{s,\mu}(\delta)=\tilde{\mu}(\det\delta)\times\frac{2\pi}{{|D|}}\,\frac{(2\pi\sqrt{|D|})^{-s+l-1}}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)}\times(-1)^{l}e^{-2\pi\sqrt{|D|}}.

Proof.

By (
3.4
),

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(g)=\mu_{\infty}(\det\delta)(-1)^{l}\left(1-\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}ai\right)^{s-l+1}\quad\text{for $g=\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}$}.

Using this and by noting that
\mu_{\infty}(\det\delta)=\tilde{\mu}(\det\delta)
, we obtain

(8.5)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle=\pi\cdot\tilde{\mu}(\det\delta)(-1)^{l}\frac{2}{\sqrt{|D|}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}(1+ix)^{s-l+1}\exp(2\pi i\sqrt{|D|}x)\,{\rm{d}}x.

for
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)<l-2
. By
[
10
, 3.382.6]
, we are done.
∎

By Lemmas
8.1
and
8.2
, we can apply Fubini’s theorem to have formula (
8.1
) for
0<c<l-2
. The function
{\mathbb{J}}_{l}^{1_{4}}(\phi,f\mid s,\Lambda,\mu)
defined as the sum over
\delta\in\{1_{2},\sigma\}
of the product of (
8.3
) and (
8.4
) has the desired property in Proposition
6.1
. This completes the proof of formula (
6.1
). Proposition
6.7
for
\mathsf{u}=1_{4}
follows from (
8.3
) and (
8.4
) since
\tilde{\mu}(\det\delta)^{2}=\tilde{\mu}(1)=1
.

8.2.
The term
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm s})}(\Phi)

Starting from (
5.5
) by substituting (
3.13
), we formally get the following formula, whose proof will be given at the end of this section.

(8.6)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm s})}(\Phi)=\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}\beta(s)s(s^{2}-1)\widehat{L}(1+s,\Lambda\mu_{E})\{{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(1_{2})+{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\sigma)\}\,{\rm{d}}s

with
c>1
, where for
\delta\in\{1_{4},\sigma\}
, we set

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta):={\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}(\delta)\,{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)

with

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau)\biggl\{\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}{\Phi}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)n\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,\psi_{T_{\theta}}(n)^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}n\biggr\}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle:=\pi\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})}{\Phi}_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(X)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\,\exp(-2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))\,{\rm{d}}X.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\delta\in\{1_{2},\sigma\}
and
I\subset(1,\infty)
any compact interval. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}|{\Phi}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)n\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})|\,{\rm{d}}n\biggr\}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\ll 1,\quad s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}.

For
(\phi,f)
as in §
6.2
(i) and (ii),

(8.7)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle=2^{1/2}|D|_{\infty}^{-s-1/2}\varepsilon(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})^{-1}\frac{L(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}{L(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})}M^{-2s-1}G(\tilde{\mu}^{-1})^{2}\,{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta).

Proof.

By (
3.10
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}{\Phi}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)n\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,\psi_{T_{\theta}}(n)^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}n

(8.8)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}\biggl\{\int_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(h)f(\iota(h)^{-1}w_{2}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,{\rm{d}}h\biggr\}\,\psi(\det\delta{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}X

By writing
X=aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}

(a\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}},\,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})
and using (
2.10
) and by making the change of variables
h\rightarrow\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&-\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]h
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(h)\,f(\iota(h)^{-1}w_{2}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,{\rm{d}}h

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&-\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]h)\,f(\iota(h)^{-1}\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,{\rm{d}}h.

Since the self-dual measure
{\rm{d}}X
of
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
is decomposed as
{\rm{d}}X=|D/2|^{1/2}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}{\rm{d}}\beta\,{\rm{d}}a
, the formula in (
8.8
) becomes

\displaystyle|D/2|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{1/2}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\int_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}[M(s)\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}](h)\,f(\iota(h)^{-1}\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,{\rm{d}}h\,\psi(aD(\det\delta)/2)\,{\rm{d}}a,

where

[M(s)\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}](h):=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&-\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]h)\,{\rm{d}}\beta.

By the relation
{\rm{d}}\beta=|D|^{-1/2}\times\prod_{p<\infty}{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}}(\beta_{p})
(see §
2.5.1
) and by (
2.38
) and (
2.35
),
[M(s)\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}](h)
equals

|D|^{-1/2}\prod_{p<\infty}|D|_{p}^{s-1/2}\varepsilon_{p}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E},\psi_{E_{p}})^{-1}\frac{L_{p}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}{L_{p}(1+s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}\mathsf{f}_{\widehat{\phi}_{p}}^{(-s,\Lambda_{p}^{-1},\mu_{p}^{-1})}(h)

Note that
\varepsilon_{\infty}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E},\psi_{E,\infty})=1
. Moreover,
\widehat{\phi}=\otimes_{p<\infty}\widehat{\phi_{p}}
.
Then, due to (
3.10
), we obtain the equality:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}{\Phi}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)n\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))\,\psi_{T_{\theta}}(n)^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}n

(8.9)

\displaystyle=2^{1/2}|D|^{-s-1/2}\varepsilon(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})^{-1}\frac{L(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}{L(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})}\,\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\Phi_{\widehat{\phi},f}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1}.\mu^{-1})}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}))\,\psi(aD/2)\,{\rm{d}}a

using the identity
\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)=\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(a(\det\delta)T_{\theta}^{\dagger})
. Multiplying
\Lambda(\tau)
and taking the integral in
\tau
, we get

(8.10)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)=2^{1/2}|D|^{-1/2-s}\varepsilon(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})^{-1}\frac{L(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}{L(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})}{\mathcal{I}}_{\widehat{\phi},f}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta).

The same argument yields the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\biggl\{\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}|{\Phi}_{f}^{(\Lambda,s)}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)n\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau))|\,{\rm{d}}n\biggr\}{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{1/2}|D|^{-1/2-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}\tfrac{L({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1})}{L({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1,{\bf 1})}\,\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}E_{\infty}^{\times}}\biggl\{\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\Phi_{|\widehat{\phi}|,|f|}^{(-{\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}(\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}))\,{\rm{d}}a\biggr\}{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau

for
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1
. The majorant is convergent by Lemma
8.1
. The formula (
8.7
) follows from (
8.10
) by (
11.3
) and Proposition
11.2
together with (
8.3
) easily.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

For
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)=c>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})}|{\Phi}_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(w_{2}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)\mathsf{n}(X)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})|\,{\rm{d}}X\ll_{c}1,

(8.11)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}(\delta)=|D|^{1/2}2^{-1/2}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+1)}\,{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(-s,\mu)}(\delta).

Proof.

Set
X=aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}

(a\in{\mathbb{R}},\beta\in{\mathbb{C}})
. By (
3.4
),
\pi^{-1}\times{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}({\delta})
equals

\displaystyle\left|\tfrac{D}{2}\right|_{\infty}^{1/2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(w_{2}\iota(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right])\,\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\,\mathsf{m}(\delta,\det\delta)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\,\exp(-\pi ia(\det\delta)D)\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}\beta

\displaystyle=|\tfrac{D}{2}|^{1/2}\mu_{\infty}(\det\delta)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\left(1-ia\det\delta\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\right)^{s-l+1}\biggl\{\int_{{\mathbb{C}}}\{\left(1-ia\det\delta\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\right)^{2}+\beta\bar{\beta}\}^{-s-1}{\rm{d}}\beta\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp(-\pi ia(\det\delta)|D|)\,{\rm{d}}a.

Then, using

\int_{{\mathbb{C}}}(A^{2}+\beta\bar{\beta})^{-(s+1)}\,{\rm{d}}\beta=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+1)}A^{-2s},\quad{\operatorname{Re}}(A)>0,\,{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>0,

and by (
8.5
), we immediately get (
8.11
).
∎

By the first parts of Lemmas
8.3
and
8.4
, we can apply Fubini’s theorem to have formula (
8.1
) for
1<c<l-2
. This completes the proof of formula (
6.1
). By (
8.10
) and (
8.11
), we have

{\mathbb{J}}_{l}^{w_{2}({\rm s})}(\phi,f\mid s,\Lambda,\mu):=|D|_{\infty}^{-s}\varepsilon(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})^{-1}\frac{\widehat{L}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})}{\widehat{L}(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})}\sum_{\delta\in\{1_{2},\sigma\}}{\mathcal{I}}_{\widehat{\phi},f}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta)\times\,{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(-s,\mu)}(\delta)

which, combined with the functional equation of
\widehat{L}(s,\Lambda\mu_{E})
, shows the formula (
6.2
).

9.
Preliminaries for error term estimates

To handle the terms
{\mathbb{J}}_{\mathsf{u}}(\Phi)
with
\Phi={\widehat{\bf\Phi}_{\phi,f,l}}^{(\beta,\Lambda,\mu)}
for
\mathsf{u}\in\{w_{2}({\rm r}),w_{1},\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}\}
, additional notation is in order. Let us extend (
4.5
) by setting
{\mathfrak{X}}(w_{2}({\rm r})):=\{1\}\times(I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash({\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})-I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\{1_{2},\sigma\}))
. We start from (
5.4
); by changing the order of
n
-integral and the summation, and then by
n\rightarrow\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda\det\delta)^{-1}n\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda\det\delta)
after that, we obtain that
{\mathbb{J}}_{\mathsf{u}}(\Phi)
equals

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau)\Bigl\{\sum_{(\lambda,\delta)\in{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathsf{u})}\int_{\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{A}})}\psi(\lambda{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\delta^{-1})\,X))^{-1}\,\Phi(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda\det\delta)\,\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta})\,{\rm{d}}X\Bigr\}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau,

where the symbol
w_{2}({\rm r})
appearing in the integral is simply
w_{2}
. Substituting (
3.13
) to this, and formally changing the order of integrals and summation, we get

(9.1)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{J}}_{\mathsf{u}}(\Phi)=\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}\beta(s)s(s^{2}-1)\widehat{L}(s+1,\Lambda\mu_{E})\,{\mathbb{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{u}}(\phi,f\mid s,\Lambda,\mu)\,{\rm{d}}s\times e^{-2\pi\sqrt{|D|}},

where

(9.2)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{u}}(\phi,f\mid s,\Lambda,\mu):=\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}\Lambda(\tau)\,\bigl\{\sum_{(\lambda,\delta)\in{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathsf{u})}{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,\delta I_{\theta}(\tau))\bigr\}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau

with

(9.3)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h)

\displaystyle:=e^{2\pi\sqrt{|D|}}\,\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})}\Phi_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\,\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)\,b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\,\psi(\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})\,X))^{-1}\,{\rm{d}}X

for
(\lambda,h)\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\times{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
. Define

(9.4)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}})

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}\psi(\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}((T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-1}))\,X))^{-1}\,{\Phi}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}},\lambda^{-1}\det h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}))\,{\rm{d}}X,

(9.5)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h_{\infty})

\displaystyle:=e^{2\pi\sqrt{|D|}}\,\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})}\psi(\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}((T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h_{\infty}^{-1}))\,X))^{-1}\,{\Phi}_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h_{\infty},\lambda^{-1}\det h_{\infty})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\,{\rm{d}}X,

so that
{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h)
for
h=h_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}h_{\infty}\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
equals
{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{\mathsf{n},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}})\times{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h_{\infty})
. Since
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
is compact, we can fix a compact set
{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}\subset E_{\infty}^{\times}
such that
{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
is mapped surjectively onto
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
. To make the argument so far rigorous by Fubini’s theorem, it is sufficient to establish the estimates,

(9.6)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}})}|{\bf\Phi}_{\phi,f,l}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})|\,{\rm{d}}X\ll_{h}1,\quad s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I},

(9.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{(\lambda,\delta)\in{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathsf{u})}|{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,\delta I_{\theta}(\tau))|\ll\exp(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|),\quad s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I},\,\tau\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\,{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}

for a compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
, and choose the
c
in (
9.1
) from the interval
I
. To get (
9.6
) with
h=h_{\rm{\bf{f}}}h_{\infty}\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
, we shall prove estimations of its factors

(9.8)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}|{\Phi}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h_{\rm{\bf{f}}},\lambda^{-1}\det h_{\rm{\bf{f}}}))|\,{\rm{d}}X,

(9.9)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})}|{\Phi}_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h_{\infty},\lambda^{-1}\det h_{\infty})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})|\,{\rm{d}}X

for
\mathsf{u}=w_{2}({\rm r}),w_{1},\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
in §
10.1
, §
10.2
and §
10.3
, respectively. The integrals in (
9.5
) are exactly evaluated (see Lemmas
10.2
,
10.8
and
10.11
) by the method to be explained in §
9.2
, whereas the integrals in (
9.4
) are rather crudely bounded. Using these, (
9.7
) will be established.

9.1.
Convergence of the archimedean integrals

In this section, we shall prove

Lemma 9.1
.

The integral (
9.9
) converges on
{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-3)}
normally for
(\lambda,h_{\infty})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\times{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
.

Proof.

In the proof, a general element of
{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
will be written as
h
. From (
3.4
), by a direct computation, for
X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}}),\,h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}),\,\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
, we get

(9.10)

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta})=\mu_{\infty}(\lambda^{-1}\det h)\lambda^{-l}A^{-l}\left(\tfrac{{\rm sgn}(\lambda\det h)}{i\sqrt{|D|}}\tfrac{B}{A}\right)^{s-l+1}

with

(9.11)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\lambda^{-1}\langle\lambda^{-1}X^{\dagger}+\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1}),\varepsilon_{3}\rangle,\quad&(\mathsf{u}=w_{1}),\\
\frac{1}{2}\langle\lambda^{-1}X^{\dagger}+\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})\rangle^{2},\quad&(\mathsf{u}=w_{2},\,\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}),\end{cases}

(9.12)

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\langle\lambda^{-1}(X^{\dagger}-t\varepsilon_{2})+\frac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})\rangle^{2}-\frac{|D|}{4\lambda^{2}},\quad&(\mathsf{u}=w_{1}),\\
-\lambda^{-1}\langle\lambda^{-1}X^{\dagger}+\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1}),T_{\theta}\rangle,\quad&(\mathsf{u}=w_{2}),\\
\frac{|D|}{4}\langle\lambda^{-1}(X^{\dagger}+\tfrac{2}{|D|}T_{\theta})+\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})\rangle^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2\lambda^{2}},\quad&(\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}),\end{cases}

where
w_{2}
is as in (
2.11
),
w_{1}
is in (
4.1
) and
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}
are as in (
2.3
) and
\langle Z\rangle^{2}:=\langle Z,Z\rangle
. Thus,

|\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta})|\ll_{l,s}|A|^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}|B|^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+1}.

We consider the case
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
. By Lemma
9.2
(1), we obtain the estimate for
A,B
, viewed as functions in
X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
:

|A|\gg(x-\lambda^{-1}c)^{2}+1,\quad|B|^{2}\gg(x^{2}+1)\left\{1+(x^{2}+1)^{-1}\|Y\|^{2}\right\}^{2}

if we write
\lambda^{-1}(X^{\dagger}+\frac{2}{|D|}T_{\theta})=x\tfrac{2}{\sqrt{|D|}}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})+Y
with
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
Y\in(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1}))^{\bot}
and set
a=\frac{2}{\lambda^{2}|D|}
,
c=2|D|^{-3/2}\langle T_{\theta},T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})\rangle
, where
\|\cdot\|
is a norm on
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
. From this, when
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>-1
and
\lambda
is large, we can observe that (
9.9
) with
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
is majorized, locally uniformly in
(\lambda,h)
, by

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\{x^{2}+1+(x^{2}+1)^{-1}\|Y\|^{4}\}^{(-l+{\operatorname{Re}}s+1)/2}\{(x-\lambda^{-1}c)^{2}+1\}^{-({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1)}\,{\rm{d}}x\,{\rm{d}}Y,

which, by the change of variables
Y=(x^{2}+1)^{1/2}Y_{1}
, equals

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\{1+\|Y_{1}\|^{4}\}^{(-l+{\operatorname{Re}}s+1)/2}\{(x-\lambda^{-1}c)^{2}+1\}^{-({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1)}(x^{2}+1)^{(-l+{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1)/2+1}\,{\rm{d}}x\,{\rm{d}}Y_{1}.

This is convergent for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(-1,l-2)}
. This completes the proof for
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
. For
\mathsf{u}=w_{2}({\rm r})
, we estimate
|B|\gg 1
by Lemma
9.2
(2) and
|A|
by Lemma
9.2
(1), and argue as above to get the convergence for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-1)}
. For
\mathsf{u}=w_{1}
, we estimate
|A|\geqslant 1
by Lemma (
9.2
) (2) and
|B|
by Lemma (
9.2
) (1) to get the convergence for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(-1,l-3)}
.
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

(1)

Let
Z\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
be a vector with
\det(Z)>0
. Let
a\geqslant 0
. Then, there exists a norm
\|\cdot\|
on
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
such that

\displaystyle|\langle X+iZ\rangle^{2}-a|^{2}\geqslant(2\det Z)^{2}(x^{2}+1)\left\{1+\frac{\|X_{Z}\|^{2}+a}{2(\det Z)(x^{2}+1)}\right\}^{2}

for any
X=xZ+X_{Z}
with
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
X_{Z}\in Z^{\bot}
.

(2)

Let
Z,Z^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
be vectors such that
\det(Z)>0
and
\det(Z^{\prime})>0
. Then,
\langle Z,Z^{\prime}\rangle\not=0
,
\langle Z,\varepsilon_{3}\rangle\not=0
, and

\displaystyle|\langle X+iZ,Z^{\prime}\rangle|\geqslant|\langle Z,Z^{\prime}\rangle|,\quad|\langle X+iZ,\varepsilon_{3}\rangle|\geqslant|\langle Z,\varepsilon_{3}\rangle|,\quad X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}}).

Proof.

(1) Since
Z^{\bot}
is a toatally negative subspace of
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, we may define a norm
\|X\|
on
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
by
\|X\|:=(x^{2}-\langle X_{Z}\rangle^{2})^{1/2}
for
X=xZ+X_{Z}\,(x\in{\mathbb{R}},\,X_{Z}\in Z^{\bot})
. Set
C:=\langle Z\rangle^{2}\,(=2\det Z)>0
and
y:=C^{-1}(x^{2}+1)^{-1}(\|X_{Z}\|^{2}+a)
. Then a computation shows

\displaystyle|\langle X+iZ\rangle^{2}-a|^{2}

\displaystyle=C^{2}\{x^{2}+1-C^{-1}(\|X_{Z}\|^{2}+a)\}^{2}+4C(\|X_{Z}\|^{2}+a)

\displaystyle=C^{2}(x^{2}+1)^{2}(1-y)^{2}+4C^{2}(x^{2}+1)y

\displaystyle\geqslant(x^{2}+1)\{C^{2}(1-y)^{2}+4C^{2}y\}=(x^{2}+1)C^{2}(y+1)^{2}.

(2) If
\langle Z,Z^{\prime}\rangle
were
0
, then
{\mathbb{R}}Z+{\mathbb{R}}Z^{\prime}
would be a totally positive plane in
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
, which does not exist because the signature of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is
(1+,2-)
. Hence,
\langle Z,Z^{\prime}\rangle\not=0
. Since
(\varepsilon_{3})^{\bot}={\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon_{1}+{\mathbb{R}}\varepsilon_{2}=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&b\\
b&c\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mid,b,c\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
and
\det\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&b\\
b&c\end{smallmatrix}\right]=-b^{2}\leqslant 0
, the space
(\varepsilon_{3})^{\bot}
does not contain a vector
Z
with
\det Z>0
. Hence,
\langle Z,\varepsilon_{3}\rangle\not=0
. The inequalities are obvious.
∎

9.2.
Computation of archimedean integrals

For
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
on the convergence region of (
9.9
), we consider the integral

(9.13)

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{u}}^{Z}(g):=\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})}\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{u}\mathsf{n}(X)g)\,\exp(-2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(ZX))\,{\rm{d}}X,\quad g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})

for
Z\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
with
\det Z>0
and
u\in\{w_{2},w_{1},\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}\}
. At least on
{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-3)}
, by Lemma
9.1
, we get the absolute convergence of the integral
W_{\mathsf{u}}^{Z}(g)
for any
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
due to
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})=\mathsf{P}({\mathbb{R}}){\bf K}_{\infty}
. Then, by Lemma
3.1
, the function
W:=W_{u}^{Z}|\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
satisfies

(9.14)

\displaystyle W(\mathsf{n}(X)g)

\displaystyle=\exp(2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(ZX))\,W(g),\quad X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}}),

(9.15)

\displaystyle W(gk)

\displaystyle=J(k,i1_{2})^{-l}\,W(g),\quad k\in{\bf K}_{\infty},

(9.16)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{X}}W(g)

\displaystyle=0,\quad X\in{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}.

Lemma 9.3
.

The function on
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
,

(9.17)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{Z}:g\longmapsto J(g,i1_{2})^{-l}\,\exp(2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(Z\,g\langle i1_{2}\rangle)),

is, up to a constant, a unique
C^{\infty}
-function that satisfies (
9.14
), (
9.15
) and (
9.16
).

Proof.

This is more or less well-known (
cf
.
[
30
]
).
∎

Thus, there is a constant
C\in{\mathbb{C}}
such that
W_{u}^{Z}(g)=C\,{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{Z}(g)
for
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
. By
\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})=\mathsf{N}({\mathbb{R}})\mathsf{M}({\mathbb{R}}){\bf K}_{\infty}
and by (
9.14
) and (
9.15
), to show the absolute convergence of the integral (
9.13
), it suffices to establish it for points
g=\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}

(h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}),\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times})
. A computation reveals that

(9.18)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{Z}(\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})=\lambda^{l}\,\exp\left(\tfrac{-4\pi\lambda}{\sqrt{|D|}}{\operatorname{tr}}(Z^{\dagger}\,T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1}))\right).

Hence, from
W_{\mathsf{u}}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})=C{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
, we get
C=e^{\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{|D|}}{\operatorname{tr}}(Z^{\dagger}T_{\theta})}\,W_{u}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
. Thus,

(9.19)

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{u}}^{Z}(g)=e^{\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{|D|}}{\operatorname{tr}}(Z^{\dagger}T_{\theta})}\,W_{u}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})\times{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{Z}(g).

When
\lambda^{-1}\det h>0
, we have
\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
and

(9.20)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi\sqrt{|D|}}\,W_{\mathsf{u}}^{\lambda^{-1}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})}(\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{l}e^{\frac{4\pi}{\lambda|D|}\langle T_{\theta},T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1}\rangle}\,W_{\mathsf{u}}^{\lambda^{-1}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})

by comparing (
9.5
) and (
9.13
) and by using (
9.19
). Noting
\mathsf{m}(1_{2},-1)=\iota\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)
and (
3.8
), we easily have
W_{\mathsf{u}}^{-Z}(g)=W_{\mathsf{u}}^{Z}(\mathsf{m}(1_{2},-1)g)
for
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})
. Hence, when
\lambda^{-1}\det h<0
, we have
\mathsf{m}(h,-\lambda^{-1}\det h)b_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{\theta}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{0}
, and thus, by (
9.20
),

(9.21)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{u},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h)=\lambda^{l}e^{\frac{-4\pi}{\lambda|D|}\langle T_{\theta},T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1}\rangle}\,W_{\mathsf{u}}^{-\lambda^{-1}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h^{-1})}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta}).

The computation of the value
W_{\mathsf{u}}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
will be done in §
10.1
, §
10.2
and §
10.3
.

9.3.
Convergence of certain infinite series

We examine the infinite sum

(9.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(\delta h),\quad h\in{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})

with parameters
R_{1},R_{2}>0
, where
{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}:{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{\geqslant 0}
is a function defined by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(h):=\|T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h_{\rm{\bf{f}}})\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-R_{1}}\|T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h_{\infty})\|_{\infty}^{-R_{2}},\qquad h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})

with
\|\cdot\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
being as in §
2.3.1
and
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
being any norm on
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
. Note that
{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}
is left
I_{\theta}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times})
-invariant, and that
I_{\theta}({\mathbb{A}}^{\times}_{E})
contains the center of
{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
.

Lemma 9.4
.

If
R_{1}>2
and
R_{2}>1
, then (
9.22
) converges normally on
{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
.

Proof.

We shall apply the integral test. Choose a complete set of representatives
D\subset{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
of
I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
. Choose a compact neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}
of the identity element
e
in
{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
such that
{\mathcal{V}}{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\cap{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})=\{e\}
. Then,
D{\mathcal{V}}:=\cup_{\delta\in D}\delta{\mathcal{V}}
is a disjoint union and the natural map
{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})\rightarrow I_{\theta}(E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
is injective on
D{\mathcal{V}}
. Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be any compact subset of
{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
then, for some constant
C>1
,

C^{-1}{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(hu)\leqslant{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(h)\leqslant C{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(hy),\qquad h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}),\,y\in{\mathcal{V}},\,u\in{\mathcal{U}}.

From the first inequality, the uniform convergence of (
9.22
) on
{\mathcal{U}}
results from the convergence at the point
h=1_{2}
. By the second inequality,
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathcal{V}})\,{\mathfrak{F}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(1_{2})
is no greater than

\displaystyle C\,\sum_{\delta\in D}\int_{y\in{\mathcal{V}}}{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(\delta y)\,{\rm{d}}y\leqslant C_{1}\,\int_{I_{\theta}(E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})}{\mathfrak{f}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(h)\,{\rm{d}}h=C_{1}\prod_{p\leqslant\infty}I_{p},

where
C_{1}=C\,{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}/E^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times})
and, for a place
p
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
,

I_{p}:=\int_{I_{\theta}(E_{p}^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}\|T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h_{p})\|_{p}^{-R_{p}}\,{\rm{d}}h_{p}

with
R_{p}=R_{1}
if
p<\infty
and
R_{\infty}=R_{2}
. Let
S
be the union of
\{\infty\}
and the set of prime numbers
p
such that
|D|_{p}\not=1
,
|{\mathtt{t}}/2|_{p}\not=1
or
|{\mathtt{N}}|_{p}\not=1
(
cf
. §
2.2
). Then, it suffices to prove
I_{p}<\infty
for all
p
and that the infinite product
\prod_{p\not\in S}I_{p}
converges. By
[
37
, Lemma 2-4]
,

{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}}I_{\theta}(E_{p}^{\times})\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&p^{n-e_{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

where
e_{p}:={\rm ord}_{p}(D)
. Set
{\mathcal{U}}_{n}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&p^{n-e_{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&p^{n-e_{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{-1}\cap I_{\theta}(E_{p}^{\times})
, which is identified with the set of all the matrices
a+\theta b\in E_{p}^{\times}
such that
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
b\in p^{n-e_{p}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\cap p^{-(n-e_{p})}{\mathtt{N}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
and
{\operatorname{N}}_{E_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(a+\theta b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
. We have that
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathcal{U}}_{n}):=\int_{{\mathcal{U}}_{n}}{\rm{d}}\eta_{E_{p}^{\times}}=c_{p}\zeta_{E,p}(1)(1-p^{-1})\,p^{-n+e_{p}}
if
n>e_{p}
. Thus, with respect to the quotient measure on
I_{\theta}(E_{p}^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, the set

Y_{n}:=(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&p^{n-e_{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&p^{n-e_{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\cap{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))

has the measure
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathcal{U}}_{n})^{-1}
if
n>e_{p}
. Suppose
p\not\in S
, then
e_{p}=0
and
{\mathtt{N}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
. Hence,
{\operatorname{vol}}(Y_{0})={\operatorname{vol}}({\mathcal{U}}_{0})^{-1}=1
. Hence, for
p\not\in S
,

\displaystyle I_{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}{\operatorname{vol}}(Y_{n})\,\|T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&p^{n-e_{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right])\|_{p}^{-R_{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\zeta_{E,p}(1)^{-1}(1-p^{-1})^{-1}p^{n}\times\max(p^{n},|{\mathtt{t}}/2|_{p},p^{-n}|{\mathtt{N}}|_{p})^{-R_{1}}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(1+\varepsilon p^{-1})^{-1}p^{-(R_{1}-1)n}=\frac{1+\varepsilon p^{-R_{1}}}{1-p^{-R_{1}+1}}

with
\varepsilon
equal to
1
or
-1
according to
p
being inert in
E
or spliting in
E
. In the same way,
I_{p}<\infty
for any
p\in S
. When
R_{1}>2
, then
\prod_{p<\infty}\frac{1+\varepsilon p^{-R_{1}}}{1-p^{-R_{1}+1}}
converges, so does
\prod_{p<\infty}I_{p}
.
Since
I_{\theta}(E_{\infty}^{\times})
is conjugate in
{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}{\rm SO}(2)
, there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\int_{{\bf PGL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}f(g)\,{\rm{d}}g=C_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}f(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{smallmatrix}\right])\,\sinh(2t)\,{\rm{d}}t

for any
f\in C({\mathbb{R}}^{\times}{\rm SO}(2)\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}))
. Apply this to
f(h)=\|T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)\|_{\infty}^{-R_{2}}
. Then, since
f(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{smallmatrix}\right])=O(e^{-2R_{2}t})
, we have
I_{\infty}<\infty
if
R_{2}>1
. ∎

9.4.
A Dirichlet series from orbits

By (
2.1
) and (
2.2
),

(9.23)

\displaystyle\langle X{\mathsf{s}}(h),Y{\mathsf{s}}(h)\rangle=\langle X,Y\rangle,\quad X,Y\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}),\,h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}).

Recall (
2.12
). By means of the orthogonal decomposition
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})={\mathbb{Q}}T_{\theta}\oplus{\mathsf{V}}^{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{Q}})^{\dagger}
, define
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear maps
c:{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\gamma:{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow E
such that
Z=c(Z)\,T_{\theta}+X_{\gamma(Z)}^{\dagger}
, where
X_{\gamma}^{\dagger}:=(X_{\gamma})^{\dagger}
. A computation reveals

(9.24)

\displaystyle c(Z)=\tfrac{-2}{D}\langle T_{\theta},Z\rangle,\quad{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\gamma(Z))=\tfrac{-1}{D}\langle T_{\theta},Z\rangle^{2}-\det Z.

By (
2.4
), the point
T_{\theta}
is in the set

\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{Q}}):=\left\{Z\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})\mid\det Z=\tfrac{-D}{4}\right\}.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
Z\in\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{Q}})
; then,
|c(Z)|\geqslant 1
with the equality if and only if
Z=\pm T_{\theta}
.

Proof.

By (
9.24
),
we have

\tfrac{-D}{4}(c(Z)^{2}-1)=\tfrac{-D}{4}c(Z)^{2}-\det Z={\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\gamma(Z))\geqslant 0
with the equality if and only of
\gamma(Z)=0
, or equivalently
Z\in\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{Q}})\cap{\mathbb{Q}}T_{\theta}=\{\pm T_{\theta}\}
. ∎

Lemma 9.6
.

The set
\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{Q}})
coincides with the
{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
-orbit of the point
T_{\theta}
. The map
g\mapsto T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(g)
induces a bijection from
I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
onto
\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{Q}})
. The coset
I_{\theta}(E^{\times})
and
I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\sigma
are mapped to
T_{\theta}
and
-T_{\theta}
, respectively.

Proof.

By Witt’s theorem, the set
\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{Q}})
is a single
{\rm SO}({\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}))
-orbit. It turns out that
{\mathsf{s}}({\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}))={\rm SO}({\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}))
by (
9.23
).
∎

For
h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
, set

(9.25)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{q}}(h):=|c(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h))|\,(=\tfrac{2}{|D|}|\langle T_{\theta},T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)\rangle|).

For a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-lattice
{\mathscr{M}}\subset{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}})
, define

(9.26)

\displaystyle\zeta_{D}(s,{\mathscr{M}}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta\in I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})\\
T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\delta)\in{\mathscr{M}}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{q}}(\delta)^{-s},\quad s\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Lemma 9.7
.

The series
\zeta_{D}(s,{\mathscr{M}})
in (
9.26
) converge absolutely for
s>1
.

Proof.

Since
\widehat{\mathscr{M}}:={\mathscr{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}\subset{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
is compact, we have
\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\widehat{\mathscr{M}}}(Z)\ll\|Z\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-3}
for
Z\in{\mathsf{V}}^{\star}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
. Since
\langle T_{\theta},T_{\theta}\rangle=2\det T_{\theta}=\frac{|D|}{2}>0
and
{\rm sgn}{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})=(1+,2-)
, the orthogonal
T_{\theta}^{\bot}
is negative definite; hence, by (
9.24
), we have a bound

\{|c(Z)|^{2}+1\}^{1/2}\asymp\|Z\|_{\infty}
for
Z\in\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{R}})
. Hence, (
9.26
) is majorized by (
9.22
) with
R_{1}=3
and
R_{2}=s
. By Lemma
9.4
, we have the absolute convergence of (
9.26
).
∎

10.
Error term estimates

In this section, we shall prove Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
. To describe the dependence of error terms on the Hecke function
f_{S}\in{\mathscr{H}}_{S}
, we recall
{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})
in (
2.26
) and the
L^{1}
-norm
\|f_{S}\|_{1}
.

10.1.
Estimation of
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{2}({\rm r})}(\Phi)

Recall the set
{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}
from the preamble of §
9
.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
\phi=\otimes_{p<\infty}\phi_{p}\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{2})
and
f=\otimes_{p<\infty}f_{p}\in{\mathscr{H}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}))
be as in §
6.2
. For a compact interval
I\subset(1,\infty)
, the integral (
9.8
) for
\mathsf{u}=w_{2}({\rm r})
has the uniform bound

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{w_{2}({\rm r}),(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})\ll_{M}N^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}\,{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-2}\,\|f_{S}\|_{1},\quad s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}.

Moreover, there exists a lattice
{\mathscr{M}}
depending only on
E
such that the integral for
h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}=\delta I_{\theta}(\tau)

(\delta\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}),\,\tau\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
is zero unless
T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\delta)\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathscr{M}}
.

Proof.

By (
2.34
), (
2.35
) and (
2.41
), we have
|\mathsf{f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}_{\phi}(g^{\#})|\ll_{I}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}(g^{\#})
for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I},\,g^{\#}\in\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}})
. By arguing as in the proof of Lemma
8.3
, the integral in (
9.8
) with
\mathsf{u}=w_{2}
is majorized by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{G}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}{\rm{d}}h\biggl\{\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&-\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]h\right)\,{\rm{d}}\beta\biggr\}\,\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}|f(\iota(h)^{-1}\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\mathsf{m}(h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}},\det h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}))|\,{\rm{d}}a.

Since the
\beta
-integral is evaluated to be
|D|^{1/2}\frac{L({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1})}{L({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1,{\bf 1})}\mathsf{f}_{\widehat{\phi}}^{({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}(h)
when
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1
, and
|\widehat{\phi}|\leqslant\phi_{0}:=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{M^{-1}\widehat{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}\oplus M^{-1}\widehat{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}}
by (
6.4
), we have that

(10.1)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\Phi_{\phi_{0},|f|}^{(-{\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}(\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\mathsf{m}(h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}},\det h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}))\,{\rm{d}}a

is a uniform majorant of the above expression for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
. Suppose that
f_{S}
is the characteristic function of a single
{\bf K}_{S}
-double coset; fix elements
y_{i}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\gamma_{i}&0\\
0&\lambda_{i}({}^{t}\gamma_{i})^{\dagger}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&X_{i}\\
0&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})

(1\leqslant i\leqslant n)
with
y_{i,p}=1_{4}
for
p\notin S\cup S(M)
such that
{\rm supp}(f)=\cup_{i=1}^{n}{\bf K}_{0}(N)y_{i}
.
Then,
|\lambda_{i}|_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{-1}\leqslant{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})
by (
2.30
) and
\|f_{S}\|_{1}=n
. By noting that
f_{p}(k^{\#})\leqslant 1
for any
k^{\#}\in{\bf K}_{p}
for all
p
, the integral (
10.1
) is majorized by

(10.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\int_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}(\beta){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}}(\lambda){\rm{d}}\eta_{{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}}^{\times}}(\tau){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}}(k^{\#}){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}(a)

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(N)y_{i}}(\iota(k^{\#})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(I_{\theta}(\tau),\lambda{\operatorname{N}}(\tau))^{-1}n(X_{\beta})^{-1}\mathsf{n}(aT_{\theta}^{\dagger})\mathsf{m}(h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}},\det h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})\eta)|\lambda|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}

multiplied with a positive constant depending only on
M
. Recall the element
\eta=(\eta_{p})_{p<\infty}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
in §
6.2
(ii). By the inequality
\int_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(N)y_{i}}(\iota(k^{\#})g){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}}(k^{\#})=[{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(N)]^{-1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\iota({\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\#}){\bf K}_{0}(N)}(gy_{i}^{-1})\leqslant\prod_{p|N}(p^{2}+1)^{-1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}(gy_{i}^{-1})\leqslant N^{-2}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}(gy_{i}^{-1})
for
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
and by the change of variables
\tau\mapsto\tau^{-1}
,
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{-1}
,
\beta\mapsto-\beta
, and
X=aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}\mapsto X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\gamma_{i}^{-1})-X_{i}
, the integral (
10.2
) is majorized by

\displaystyle N^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}I_{\theta}(\tau)h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\gamma_{i}^{-1}&\lambda_{i}^{-1}I_{\theta}(\tau)h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\gamma_{i}^{-1}(1_{2}+X)\\
0&\lambda\lambda_{i}^{-1}({}^{t}I_{\theta}(\tau))^{\dagger}({}^{t}h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})^{\dagger}({}^{t}\gamma_{i}^{-1})^{\dagger}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\eta\right)

(10.3)

\displaystyle\times|\lambda|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}}(\lambda){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}}(\tau){\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})}(X).

It is easy to check that the
4\times 4
-matrix inside the bracket is in
{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
if and only if
I_{\theta}(\tau)h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\gamma_{i}^{-1}\in{\bf GL}_{2}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
,
\lambda\in\lambda_{i}N^{-1}\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\times}
, and
X\in-1_{2}+\lambda_{i}N^{-1}{\mathsf{V}}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
. The
\tau
-integral does not affect the evaluation of the inequality because the set of all
\tau\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}
satisfying
I_{\theta}(\tau)h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\gamma_{i}^{-1}\in{\bf GL}_{2}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
is either the empty set or a set of the form
\tau_{0}\widehat{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}^{\times}
(
\tau_{0}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}
), which has volume
1
. Hence, we have that
{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{w_{2}({\rm r}),(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})
is majorized by

\displaystyle N^{-2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}(|\lambda_{i}|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}N)^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}\cdot{\operatorname{vol}}(\tfrac{\lambda_{i}}{N}V(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}))=N^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}\,{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-2}\|f_{S}\|_{1}.

Moreover,
I_{\theta}(\tau)h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\gamma_{i}^{-1}\in{\bf GL}_{2}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
yields
T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}})\in\cup_{i=1}^{n}T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\gamma_{i}{\bf GL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}))
. Suppose
h_{1,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}=\delta I_{\theta}(\tau)
(
\delta\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
,
\tau\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
). By (
2.29
), we have
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathsf{s}}(\gamma_{i})\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
; let
{\mathscr{M}}
be the smallest lattice such that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathsf{s}}({\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
In general,
f_{S}
is of the form
\sum_{j}c_{j}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{X_{j}}
with
X_{j}
being mutually disjoint
{\bf K}_{S}
-double cosets and
c_{j}\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Then,
\|f_{S}\|_{1}=\sum_{j}|c_{j}|\|\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{X_{j}}\|_{1}
. By this, we obtain the desired estimate for
f_{S}
from estimates for
\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{X_{j}}
. ∎

Recall the function
{\mathfrak{q}}(h)
in (
9.25
).

Lemma 10.2
.

For any compact interval
I\subset(1,\infty)
, the integral in (
9.9
) with
\mathsf{u}=w_{2}
is uniformly bounded for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
; moreover, for
h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
,

(10.4)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{w_{2}({\rm r}),(s,\mu)}(1,h)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{5/2}\pi^{s+l}|D|^{(s+l-2)/2}\mu_{\infty}(\det h)}{\Gamma(s+1)\Gamma(l-1)}\,\int_{-1}^{1}(t^{2}-1)^{l-2}\left(t+{\mathfrak{q}}(h)\right)^{s-l+1}\,{\rm{d}}t,

which, as a function in
h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
, is right
I_{\theta}(E_{\infty}^{\times})
-invariant. For
h\not\in{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{R}})
, the integral in (
10.4
) converges absolutely for any
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
. For
q>1
, there exists a unique entire function
\widetilde{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}[q]
in
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
, invariant by
s\rightarrow-s
and such that

\widetilde{{\mathscr{J}}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}[{\mathfrak{q}}(h)]=\left\{\tfrac{|D|}{4}({\mathfrak{q}}(h)^{2}-1)\right\}^{-s/2}\,\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+1)\times{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{w_{2}({\rm r}),(s,\mu)}(1,h).

Proof.

We only need to evaluate
W^{Z}_{w_{2}}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
(see §
9.2
). The value
\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(w_{2}\mathsf{n}(X)b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
is known by (
9.10
) followed by (
9.11
) and (
9.12
); thus, by writing
X=aT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}

(a\in{\mathbb{R}},\beta\in{\mathbb{C}})
, we have that
W^{Z}_{w_{2}}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
equals

\displaystyle|\tfrac{D}{2}|^{1/2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(ZX)}\,\Bigl(1-ia\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\Bigr)^{s-l+1}\Bigl\{\Bigl(1-ia\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\Bigr)^{2}+\beta\bar{\beta}\Bigr\}^{-(s+1)}\,{\rm{d}}a\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta).

Set
Z^{\dagger}=cT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\gamma}
(
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\gamma=u+iv\,u,v\in{\mathbb{R}})
. Then, for
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1
, this becomes

\displaystyle|\tfrac{D}{2}|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}e^{\pi iacD}\Bigl(1-ia\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\Bigr)^{s-l+1}\,{\rm{d}}a

\displaystyle\quad\times\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\Bigl\{\Bigl(1-ia\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\Bigr)^{2}+\beta\bar{\beta}\Bigr\}^{-(s+1)}\,e^{4\pi i{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta\bar{\gamma}}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta),

which, by Lemma
10.3
and by the change of variables
z=1-ia\sqrt{|D}/2
, equals the contour integral

(10.5)

\displaystyle|\tfrac{D}{2}|^{1/2}\tfrac{2\pi(2\pi|\gamma|)^{s}}{\Gamma(s+1)}e^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}}\,\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(z)=1}\exp(-2\pi\sqrt{|D|}cz)z^{1-l}K_{s}(4\pi z|\gamma|)\,\tfrac{-2i}{\sqrt{|D|}}\,{\rm{d}}z.

Due to
K_{s}(z)=K_{-s}(z)
, this yields the functional equation. By the formula
K_{s}(z)=\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}(v+v^{-1})z)v^{s-1}\,{\rm{d}}v
, and by using
[
10
, 3.382.6]
, we have that
W^{Z}_{w_{2}}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
equals

\displaystyle-2^{1/2}\pi i\tfrac{(2\pi|\gamma|)^{s}e^{-2\pi\sqrt{|D|}c}}{\Gamma(s+1)}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(z)=1}z^{1-l}\exp(-2\pi\{\sqrt{|D|}c+(v+v^{-1})|\gamma|\}z)\,{\rm{d}}z\right\}\,v^{s-1}\,{\rm{d}}v

\displaystyle=-2^{1/2}\pi i\tfrac{(2\pi|\gamma|)^{s}e^{-2\pi\sqrt{|D|}c}}{\Gamma(s+1)\Gamma(l-1)}(-2\pi i)(2\pi|\gamma|)^{l-2}\int_{v_{1}(Z)}^{v_{+}(Z)}\{(v-v_{-}(Z))(v-v_{+}(Z))\}^{l-2}v^{s-l+1}\,{\rm{d}}v,

where
v_{\pm}(Z):=|\gamma|^{-1}\left(\tfrac{-\sqrt{|D|}c}{2}\pm\sqrt{\det(Z)}\right)
are the roots of the quadratic equation
|\gamma|v^{2}+\sqrt{|D|}cv+|\gamma|=0
. By
v=2^{-1}(v_{+}(Z)-v_{-}(Z))t+2^{-1}(v_{+}(Z)+v_{-}(Z))
,

\displaystyle e^{2\pi\sqrt{|D|}c}\,W_{w_{2}}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})=-\frac{2^{-1/2+s+l}\pi^{s+l}(\det Z)^{(s+l-2)/2}}{\Gamma(s+1)\Gamma(l-1)}\,\int_{-1}^{1}(t^{2}-1)^{l-2}\left(t+\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}c}{2\sqrt{\det Z}}\right)^{s-l+1}\,{\rm{d}}t.

By (
9.20
) and (
9.21
), we have (
10.4
). The last assertion follows from the espression (
10.5
) of
W^{Z}_{w_{2}}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
together with (
9.20
) and (
9.21
) because the function
{\mathfrak{q}}
is right
{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{R}})
-invariant.
∎

Lemma 10.3
.

For
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1
and
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}(A^{2}+\beta\bar{\beta})^{-(s+1)}\,\exp(4\pi i{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta\bar{\gamma}))\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\beta)=\frac{2\pi(2\pi|\gamma|)^{s}A^{-s}}{\Gamma(s+1)}\,K_{s}(4\pi A|\gamma|).

Proof.

Let
I(\gamma)
denote the integral. Since
I(\gamma)
is a radial function, it suffices to evaluate it for
\gamma>0
. By using
[
10
, 3.715.8]
,
I(\gamma)
equals

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}(A^{2}+r^{2})^{-(s+1)}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\exp(2\pi ir\gamma\sin\theta)\,{\rm{d}}\theta\biggr\}\,r{\rm{d}}r=2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}(A^{2}+r^{2})^{-(s+1)}J_{0}(2\pi\gamma r)r\,{\rm{d}}r.

Then use
[
10
, 6.564.4]
to complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.4
.

For any
t\in(1,l-1)
and
u>1
, we have the uniform bound

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{J}}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}[q]\ll_{t,u}\tfrac{(\pi\sqrt{|D|})^{l-1}}{\Gamma(l-1)}\,\left(1+\tfrac{\Gamma(l-1)\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(l-1/2)}\right)\,q^{-l+1},\quad(s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{[-t,t]},\,l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 3},\,q>u).

Proof.

Fix
q>1
and let
I_{-}
(resp.
I_{+}
) denote the integral of
(1-t^{2})^{l-2}(t+q)^{s-l+1}
over
[-1,0]
(resp.
[0,1]
). Since the function
t\mapsto f(t):=\frac{1-t^{2}}{t+q}
is increasing on
[-1,0]
, we have
f(t)\leqslant f(0)=q^{-1}
for
t\in[-1,0]
. Hence,

\displaystyle|I_{-}|\leqslant\int_{-1}^{0}\left(f(t)\right)^{l-2}(t+q)^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}\,{\rm{d}}t\leqslant q^{-l+2}\,q^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}=q^{l-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1},\quad{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1,

As for
I_{+}
, by using the inequality
(t+q)^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+1}\leqslant q^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+1}
valid for
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)<l-1
,

\displaystyle|I_{+}|\leqslant q^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+1}\,\int_{0}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{l-2}\,{\rm{d}}t=q^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+1}\tfrac{\Gamma(l-1)\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(l-1/2)}.

Thus, we have the inequality

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{J}}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}[q]\leqslant\left\{\tfrac{|D|}{4}(q^{2}-1)\right\}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)/2}\,|\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+1)|\times\left(1+\tfrac{\Gamma(l-1)\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(l-1/2)}\right)\,q^{-l+{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}

for
q>1
,
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-1)}
, whose right-hand side is majorized by
O_{u,t}\left(\Bigl(1+\tfrac{\Gamma(l-1)\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(l-1/2)}\Bigl)q^{-l+1}\right)
uniformly in
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,t)}
and
q>u
. Since
\widetilde{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{(s,\mu)}[q]
is invariant by
s\rightarrow-s
, we conclude the proof by the Phragmen-Lindelöf principle.
∎

Since
\Gamma(l-1)/\Gamma(l-1/2)\sim l^{-1/2}\ll 1
, the majorant in Lemma
10.4
can be replaced by
2{\mathfrak{q}}(\delta)^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+1}
for large
l
. Hence, by (
9.2
) and by Lemmas
10.1
,
10.2
, and
10.4
, we have the absolute convergence of (
9.2
) and the majorization:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{J}}_{l}^{w_{2}({\rm r})}(\phi,f\mid s,\Lambda,\mu)

\displaystyle\ll\tfrac{(\pi|D|^{1/2})^{s+l}}{|\Gamma(s+1)|\Gamma(l-1)}N^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-2}\|f_{S}\|_{1}\,\{\zeta_{D}(\sigma_{l},{\mathscr{M}})-2\}

with
\sigma_{l}:=-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+l-1
uniformly in
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
and
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>l_{0}}
for a fixed compact interval
I\subset(1,l-2)
, where
{\mathscr{M}}:={\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}
, and
l_{0}
is an absolute constant. Note that
\sigma_{l}>1
, i.e.,
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)<l-2
, is necessary to apply Lemma
9.7
. By (
9.26
) and Lemma
9.7
,

\zeta_{D}(\sigma_{l},{\mathscr{M}})-2={\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{\sigma_{l}}\{\zeta_{D}(\sigma_{l},{\mathcal{Q}})-2\}=O({\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{\sigma_{l}}C^{-l})

for some
C>1
. Thus we get Proposition
6.3
.

10.2.
Estimation of
{\mathbb{J}}_{w_{1}}(\Phi)

Recall our choice
(\phi,f)
as well as the definition of
\eta
made in §
6.2
(i) and (ii), and note that
f
depends on a square free integer
N
.

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
\omega(N)
denote the number of prime divisors of
N
. Then,

\Phi^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}_{\phi,f}(k\eta)\ll 2^{\omega(N)}\|f_{S}\|_{1}(N{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S}))^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1},\quad{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1,\,k\in{\bf K}_{\rm{\bf{f}}},

with the implied constant independent of
(s,\Lambda,\mu)
,
f_{S}
and
N
.

Proof.

Suppose that
f
is the characteristic function of
{\bf K}_{0}(N)y_{S}{\bf K}_{0}(N)
for some
y=(y_{p})_{p}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
such that
y_{p}=1_{4}
for
p\not\in S
. Then,

\displaystyle|\Phi^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}_{\phi,f}(k\eta)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Phi_{\phi_{0},f}^{({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}(k\eta)\ll\prod_{p<\infty}I_{p}(N,y_{p})

with
I_{p}(N,y_{p})
being equal to

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}}\int_{E_{p}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\int_{E_{p}}h_{p}(\iota(k^{\#})\mathsf{m}(I_{\theta}(\tau),\lambda{\operatorname{N}}(\tau))^{-1}n(X_{\beta})^{-1}k\eta_{p})|\lambda|_{p}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#}{\rm{d}}\beta{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau

with
h_{p}:=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})y_{p}{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}
.
We have
I_{p}(N,f_{S})\leqslant 1
for
p\nmid N
,
p\not\in S
. If
p\in S
, then we may set
y_{p}=\varpi({\underline{d}})\,({\underline{d}}\in D)
and write
{\bf K}_{p}y_{p}{\bf K}_{p}
as in (
2.27
). Thus, by (
2.30
), the integral becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{E_{p}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\int_{E_{p}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{p}}(\mathsf{m}(I_{\theta}(\tau),\lambda{\operatorname{N}}(\tau))^{-1}n(X_{\beta})^{-1}n(X_{i})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\gamma_{i};\lambda_{i}\det\gamma_{i})^{-1})|\lambda|_{p}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#}{\rm{d}}\beta

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\lambda_{i}|_{p}^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}\ll n\times(p^{2\kappa({\underline{d}})})^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}=\|f_{p}\|_{1}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{p})^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}.

If
p\mid N
, since
\eta_{p}
normalizes
{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and
\iota({\bf K}_{p}^{\#}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\subset{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, for any
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}(\iota(k^{\#})g\eta_{p})\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}(\eta_{p}\iota(k^{\#})g)\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#}
