\displaystyle=[{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]^{-1}\sum_{\ell\in{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\backslash{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}(\eta_{p}\iota(\ell)g).

In the last sum, the summand is written as
\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}(\iota(\ell)g\eta_{p})
, which is non-zero for at most
1
coset
\ell
and is no greater than
\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{p}}(g\eta_{p})
. Thus,

\displaystyle I_{p}(N,y_{p})\leqslant p^{-2}\int_{E_{p}^{\times}}\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\int_{E_{p}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{p}\eta_{p}{\bf K}_{p}}(\mathsf{n}(X_{\beta})\mathsf{m}(I_{\theta}(\tau),\lambda{\operatorname{N}}(\tau)))|\lambda|_{p}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}\,{\rm{d}}\beta{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau.

Since the integrand is non-zero only if
(\tau,\lambda,\beta)
belongs to
p^{i}\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}^{\times}\times p^{1-2i}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\times p^{-1}\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}
with
i=0,1
, we get a bound
I_{p}(N,y_{p})\leqslant p^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}(1+p^{-2({\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1)})\leqslant 2p^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}
.
∎

For
g\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
, set
y(g):=|a|_{\mathbb{A}}
with
a,z\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
,
b\in{\mathbb{A}}
such that
g\in\left[\begin{smallmatrix}az&b\\
0&z\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\bf GL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})
.

Lemma 10.6
.

For any compact interval
I\subset(-1,\infty)
, the integral (
9.8
) with
\mathsf{u}=w_{1}
is uniformly bounded in
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
; moreover, (
9.8
) with
\mathsf{u}=w_{1}
is zero unless
\lambda\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N{\mathbb{Z}}-\{0\}
. We have the bound

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{w_{1},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h)

\displaystyle\ll y(h)^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}\,2^{\omega(N)}N^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+3},

\displaystyle h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}),\,\lambda\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N{\mathbb{Z}}-\{0\},\,s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}

with the implied constant independent of
N
and
f_{S}
.

Proof.

Set
g=w_{1}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)
with
X=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}&x_{2}\\
x_{2}&x_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right]
,
h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
and
\lambda\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
. A direct computation
shows the formulas

(10.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\,[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}(-{\mathtt{N}}+{\mathtt{t}}+\det X)\,\xi_{1}-[\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}&x_{2}-{\mathtt{t}}/2\\
x_{2}-{\mathtt{t}}/2&x_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})]+\lambda\,\xi_{5},

(10.7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}x_{3}\,\xi_{1}-[\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})],

where
\mathsf{r}
as in §
2.3
. By Lemma
3.3
, for (
9.8
) to be non zero,
g=\iota\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&\bar{\tau}a\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)k\eta
with
\tau\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E,{\rm{\bf{f}}}}^{\times}
,
a\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{\times}
and
\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{E}
and
k=(k_{p})_{p<\infty}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
, where
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}=\prod_{p<\infty}{\mathcal{K}}_{p}
with
{\mathcal{K}}_{p}={\bf K}_{0}(N{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
for
p\not\in S\cup S(M)
and
{\mathcal{K}}_{p}={\rm supp}(f_{p})
for
p\in S\cup S(M)
.
Thus,

(10.8)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]=\mathsf{r}(k\eta)^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}],\quad\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}=a^{-1}\mathsf{r}(k\eta)^{-1}\xi_{1}.

By Lemma
2.3
,
\mathsf{r}({\mathcal{K}}_{p}){\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\subset{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
for
p\nmid N
. Note that
[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]\in{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}})
; then, by (
10.6
) and the first formula in (
10.8
), we have
\lambda\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
for
p\nmid N
, and

(10.9)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{smallmatrix}x_{1}&x_{2}-{\mathtt{t}}/2\\
x_{2}-{\mathtt{t}}/2&x_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathsf{V}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}).

If
q\mid N
, then
\eta_{q}=\iota\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\mathsf{m}(-\sigma,q)
; hence, by Lemma
2.1
and (
2.14
), we have
\mathsf{r}(\eta_{q})^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]=[-T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]
. Since
\eta_{q}
normalizes
{\bf K}_{0}(q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
, the element
k_{q}^{\prime}:=\eta_{q}^{-1}k_{q}\eta_{q}
is in
{\bf K}_{0}(q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
; thus
k^{\prime}_{q}=\mathsf{m}(A,c)\mathsf{n}(B)\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&0\\
NC&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]
with some
A\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
,
B\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
and
C\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
. Using (
2.14
), we easily have
\mathsf{r}(k_{q}\eta_{q})^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]=\mathsf{r}(k_{q}^{\prime})^{-1}[-T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})+N{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\xi_{5}
. This, combined with the first formula in (
10.8
) and (

), yields
\lambda\in N{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
for all

. Recall §
2.3.1
. We have
\|\mathsf{r}(k\eta)^{-1}\xi_{1}\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\asymp|N{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-1}=N{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})
for
k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}
uniformly in

. By this, due to (

) and the second formula in (

),

\displaystyle N{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})|a|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-1}\gg\|\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}\|_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}\geqslant\|\xi\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})\right)\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}=y(h)^{-1}.

By this, combined with (
3.11
) and Lemma
10.5
,
|\Phi_{f,\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(g)|=|a|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}|\Phi_{f,\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(k\eta)|
is bounded by the product of

2^{\omega(N)}\|f_{S}\|_{1}(N{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S}))^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}y(h)^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}
and the characteristic function of the set of
(\lambda,X)
with

and (
10.9
). Since

preserves the Haar measure on

, the

-integral yields
{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{3}
.∎

Recall the vectors
\varepsilon_{i}

(i=1,2,3)
; see (
2.3
). We need the following lemma; for the
J
-Bessel function
J_{\nu}(z)

(\nu>0)
, we refer to
[
25
, Chap.III]
.

Lemma 10.7
.

For
q>0
,
A>0
,
B<0
,

\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(\zeta)=1}\zeta^{-q}\exp(-A\zeta^{-1}-B\zeta)\,{\rm{d}}\zeta=-2\pi i(A|B|^{-1})^{(1-q)/2}J_{q-1}(2\sqrt{|AB|}).

Proof.

Expand
e^{-A\zeta^{-1}}
to the power series in
\zeta^{-1}
and evaluate the integral termwise by
[
10
, 3.382]
. Then use the series expression of
J_{q-1}
. ∎

Lemma 10.8
.

For any compact interval
I\subset(1,l-3)
, the integral (
9.9
) is bounded by
\exp(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s))
uniformly in
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
; moreover,
{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{w_{1},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h)
is zero unless
\lambda\,{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)^{-1}\varepsilon_{1})>0
, in which case it equals

\displaystyle-i^{-l}\mu_{\infty}(\lambda^{-1}\det h)

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-3s+2l-3}(2\pi)^{-s+l}}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)}\,\frac{|{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)^{-1}\varepsilon_{1})|^{-s-1}}{|\lambda|^{-s+l-5/2}}

\displaystyle\times J_{l-\frac{3}{2}}(\pi^{1/2}|D||\lambda|^{-1})\,\exp(-2\pi i\lambda{\mathtt{t}}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)^{-1}\,\varepsilon_{2})).

Proof.

By (
9.10
) followed by (
9.11
) and (
9.12
),

the value
W_{w_{1}}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
is
(\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}})^{-l}\,(\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{8})^{s-l+1}\exp(-2\pi i{\mathtt{t}}{\operatorname{tr}}(Z\varepsilon_{2}))
times the integral

(10.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})}\Bigl(1-\tfrac{2}{\sqrt{|D|}}ix_{3}\Bigr)^{-(s+1)}\Bigl(1+\tfrac{|D|}{4}-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle X,X\rangle-\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D}}\langle T_{\theta}^{\dagger},X\rangle\Bigr)^{s-l+1}\,e^{-2\pi i{\operatorname{tr}}(ZX))}\,{\rm{d}}X.

Let

\eta^{+}:=(\tfrac{2}{|D|})^{1/2}\,T_{\theta}^{\dagger},\quad\eta^{-}:=(\tfrac{2}{|D|})^{1/2}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger}-\tfrac{|D|}{2}\varepsilon_{1}).

Then,
\langle\eta^{\pm},\eta^{\pm}\rangle=\pm 1
and
\langle\eta^{+},\eta^{-}\rangle=0
; choose a vector
\eta_{0}\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
so that
\langle\eta_{0},\eta_{0}\rangle=-1
and that
\{\eta^{+},\eta^{-},\eta_{0}\}
is an orthogonal basis of
{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
. Write

X=y_{+}\eta^{+}+y_{-}\eta^{-}+y_{0}\eta_{0},\quad Z^{\dagger}=c_{+}\eta^{+}+c_{-}\eta^{-}+c_{0}\eta_{0}.

Then, the integral in (
10.10
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}

\displaystyle\left(1-\tfrac{y_{+}+y_{-}}{\sqrt{2}}i\right)^{-(s+1)}\Bigl\{-\left(\tfrac{y_{+}}{\sqrt{2}}+i\right)^{2}+\left(\tfrac{y_{-}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^{2}+\Bigl(\tfrac{y_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\Bigr)^{2}+\left(\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\right)^{2}\Bigr\}^{s-l+1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp(-2\pi i(c_{+}y_{+}-c_{-}y_{-}-c_{0}y_{0}))\,{\rm{d}}y_{+}{\rm{d}}y_{-}{\rm{d}}y_{0}.

By setting
\zeta=1-\frac{y_{+}+y_{-}}{\sqrt{2}}i
, this equals

(10.11)

\displaystyle\tfrac{-i}{\sqrt{2}}\exp(-2\sqrt{2}\pi c_{+})\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(\zeta)=1}\exp(2\sqrt{2}\pi\zeta c_{+})\zeta^{-(s+1)}\ d\zeta\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\exp(2\pi ic_{0}y_{0})\,{\rm{d}}y_{0}.

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Bigl\{\zeta^{2}+\sqrt{2}iy_{-}\zeta+\left(\tfrac{y_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\right)^{2}\Bigr\}^{s+1-l}\exp(2\pi iy_{-}(c_{+}+c_{-})){\rm{d}}y_{-}.

By
[
10
, 3.382.6]
, the
y_{-}
-integral is zero unless
c_{+}+c_{-}>0
, in which case it equals

\displaystyle\frac{-2\pi|2\pi(c_{+}+c_{-})|^{-s+l-2}}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)}\exp\left(-\tfrac{2\pi(c_{+}+c_{-})}{\sqrt{2}\zeta}(x_{0}^{2}+\zeta^{2}+\tfrac{|D|}{4})\right).

In the rest of the proof, we assume
c_{+}+c_{-}>0
. Thus, (
10.11
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{i\exp(-2\sqrt{2}\pi c_{+})}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)}(2\pi)^{-s+l-1}|c_{+}+c_{-}|^{-s+l-2}\,2^{(s-l+1)/2}

\displaystyle\times\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(\zeta)=1}\zeta^{-l}\exp\left(2\sqrt{2}\pi c_{+}\zeta-\sqrt{2}\pi(c_{+}+c_{-})(\zeta+\tfrac{|D|}{4\zeta})\right)\,{\rm{d}}\zeta

\displaystyle\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\exp(-2\sqrt{2}\pi ic_{0}y_{0})\,\exp(-\sqrt{2}\pi\zeta^{-1}(c_{+}+c_{-})y_{0}^{2})\,{\rm{d}}y_{0}.

The
y_{0}
-integral is computed to be
-(\tfrac{\zeta}{\sqrt{2}|c_{+}+c_{-}|})^{1/2}\exp\left(\tfrac{\sqrt{2}\pi c_{0}^{2}\zeta}{|c_{+}+c_{-}|}\right)
. Putting this back to the last displayed formula, after a simple computation, we obtain the following:

\displaystyle\frac{-i2^{\frac{s-l+1}{2}+\frac{3}{4}}(2\pi)^{-s+l-1}}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)}\exp(-2\sqrt{2}\pi c_{+})\,|c_{+}+c_{-}|^{-s+l-5/2}

\displaystyle\times\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(\zeta)=1}\zeta^{-l+1/2}\exp\left(-\tfrac{\sqrt{2}\pi\langle Z,Z\rangle}{|c_{+}+c_{-}|}\zeta-\sqrt{2}\pi\tfrac{|D|(c_{+}+c_{-})}{4\zeta}\right)\,{\rm{d}}\zeta.

By Lemma
10.7
, after a direct computation, we get the following as the value
W_{w_{1}}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
:

\displaystyle(\tfrac{2i}{\sqrt{|D|}})^{-l}\,(\tfrac{\sqrt{|D|}}{8})^{s-l+1}\exp(-2\pi i{\mathtt{t}}{\operatorname{tr}}(Z\varepsilon_{1}))\cdot(-1)2^{(s+1)/2}(2\pi)^{-s+l}\exp(-2\sqrt{2}\pi c_{+})\tfrac{|c_{+}+c_{-}|^{-s-1}}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\tfrac{\langle Z,Z\rangle}{|D|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}(l-\frac{3}{2})}J_{l-\frac{3}{2}}((2\pi|D|\langle Z,Z\rangle)^{1/2}).

Note that
c_{+}=\langle Z^{\dagger},\eta_{+}\rangle=(\tfrac{2}{|D|})^{1/2}{\operatorname{tr}}(ZT_{\theta}^{\dagger})
and
c_{+}+c_{-}=(\frac{|D|}{2})^{1/2}{\operatorname{tr}}(Z\varepsilon_{2})
. By (
9.20
) and (
9.21
), we obtain the desired formula after a simple computation.
∎

Let
I\subset(1,l-3)
be any compact interval. Then, from Lemmas
10.8
, applying the uniform estimate
J_{q}(x)\ll(x/2)^{q}\Gamma(q+1)^{-1}
(
x>0
,
q>1
) (proved by
[
10
, 8.411 8]
) and Lemma
10.9
, we get the estimate

(10.12)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{w_{1},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h)\ll_{I}\tfrac{(4\pi|D|)^{l}}{|\Gamma(-s+l+1)|\,\Gamma(l-1/2)}|\lambda|^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-2l+4}\,y(h)^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1},\quad h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}),\,s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I},

which is uniform in
l
. This bound, combined with Lemma
10.6
, yields the following majorization, which yields (
9.7
) for
\mathsf{u}=w_{1}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{{\delta\in{\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}}\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}|{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f,l}^{w_{1},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,\delta I_{\theta}(\tau))|

\displaystyle\ll_{I}C2^{\omega(N)}N^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+3}\Bigl(\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N{\mathbb{Z}}-\{0\}}|\lambda|^{-2l+{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+4}\Bigr)\,\Bigl(\sum_{\delta}y(\delta I_{\theta}(\tau))^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1}\Bigr)

for
\tau\in{\mathcal{N}}_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
,
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
and
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{>1}
, where
C:=\frac{(4\pi|D|)^{l}}{|\Gamma(-s+l+1)|\,\Gamma(l-1/2)}\ll_{l}\exp(\frac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|)
and
\delta
is summed over
{\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
. Note that, when
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-3)}
, the first series in the majorant is convergent and amounts to
O(({\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N)^{-2l+{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+4})
. The second one is the Eisenstein series on
{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
, which is convergent for
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>0
.

Lemma 10.9
.

There exists a constant
C>1
such that

C^{-1}y(h)^{-1}\leqslant|{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)^{-1}\,\varepsilon_{1})|\leqslant Cy(h)^{-1},\qquad h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}).

Proof.

The function
{{\mathfrak{y}}}(h):=y(h)|\langle(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)^{-1})^{\dagger},\varepsilon_{1}\rangle|
is continuous on the compact manifold
{\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
. Moreover,
{\mathfrak{y}}(h)>0
since the orthogonal of
(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(h)^{-1})^{\dagger}
is negative definite and
\varepsilon_{1}
is isotropic. Hence, the image
{\mathfrak{y}}({\mathsf{B}}({\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
) is in a compact set of
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.
∎

10.3.
Estimation of
{\mathbb{J}}_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}(\Phi)

Lemma 10.10
.

For any compact interval
I\subset(-1,\infty)
, the integral (
9.8
) with
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
is uniformly bounded in
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
; moreover, (
9.8
) with
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
is zero unless
\lambda\in\frac{2}{D}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N{\mathbb{Z}}-\{0\}
. We have the bound

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{\phi,f}^{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2},(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\lambda,h)\ll\|f_{S}\|_{1}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{3{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+4}2^{\omega(N)}N^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}\Bigl(\|T_{\theta}^{\dagger}{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h)^{-1}\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}|\lambda|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\Bigr)^{-({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1)}

for
h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
and
\lambda\in\tfrac{2}{D}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N{\mathbb{Z}}-\{0\}
uniformly in
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
with the implied constant independent of
N
and
f_{S}
.

Proof.

Set
g=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}\mathsf{n}(X)\mathsf{m}(h,\lambda^{-1}\det h)
with
X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
,
\lambda\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
, and
h\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
.
By Lemma
3.3
,
\Phi_{f,\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(g)\not=0
implies
g\in\iota({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}))\,{\mathcal{K}}\eta
with

{\mathcal{K}}:=\prod_{p<\infty}{\mathcal{K}}_{p}\subset\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
,
{\mathcal{K}}_{p}:={\rm supp}(f_{p})
. Since
\mathsf{r}(\iota({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})))
fixes the vector
[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]\in{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Q}})
(Lemma
2.1
), the set
{\mathcal{K}}
yields a compact set
{\mathcal{U}}
of
{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{A}})
such that
\Phi_{f,\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(g)\not=0
implies
\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]\in{\mathcal{U}}
. Enlarging
{\mathcal{U}}
if necessary, we may suppose
{\mathcal{U}}=\prod_{p<\infty}{\mathcal{U}}_{p}
with
{\mathcal{U}}_{p}={\mathfrak{a}}(f_{p})^{-1}{\mathsf{X}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
for
p\in S\cup S(M)
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{p}={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\xi_{1}+{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})+N{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\xi_{5}
for
p\not\in S\cup S(M)
. By formulas (
2.14
) and (
2.15
),

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{2}\det X-\langle X,T_{\theta}^{\dagger}\rangle\right)\,\xi_{1}+[(-\tfrac{D}{2}X+T_{\theta}^{\dagger}){\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})]+\tfrac{D}{2}\lambda\,\xi_{5},

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\det(X)\,\xi_{1}-[X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})]+\lambda\,\xi_{5}.

Hence,
\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}[T_{\theta}^{\dagger}]
and
\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}\in{\mathcal{U}}
yield

(10.13)

\displaystyle\left(\tfrac{-D}{2}X+T_{\theta}^{\dagger}\right){\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}{\mathsf{X}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}),\,\qquad\tfrac{D}{2}\lambda\in{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}-\{0\}.

The second condition yields
|\lambda|_{p}^{-1}\geqslant|\tfrac{D}{2}|_{p}|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})|_{p}
; using this on the way, We get

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{r}(g_{p})^{-1}\xi_{1}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\geqslant\|-[X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1}]+\lambda\xi_{5}\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\max(\|X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p},|\lambda|_{p})

\displaystyle=|\lambda|_{p}\max(|\lambda|_{p}^{-1}\|X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p},1)\geqslant|\lambda|_{p}\,\min(|\tfrac{D}{2}|_{p}|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})|_{p},1)\,\max(\|X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p},1),

and

\displaystyle\|T_{\theta}^{\dagger}{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leqslant\max(\|\tfrac{D}{2}X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p},\|\left(\tfrac{-D}{2}X+T_{\theta}^{\dagger}\right){\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})\|_{p})

\displaystyle\leqslant\max(\|\tfrac{D}{2}X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p},|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}|_{p})\leqslant\max(|\tfrac{D}{2}|_{p},|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}|_{p})\,\max(\|X{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h_{p}^{-1})\|_{p},1).

Set
C_{p}^{\prime}:=\max(|\frac{D}{2}|_{p},|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}|_{p})^{-1}\min(|\frac{D}{2}|_{p}|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S}),1)=|{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})|_{p}^{-2}\min(|\frac{D}{2}|_{p},|\frac{D}{2}|_{p}^{-1})
, because
S
contains no ramified primes. Then,
C^{\prime}_{p}
is a positive constant being equal to
1
for almost all
p
such that
C^{\prime}:=\prod_{p<\infty}C_{p}^{\prime}\,(\geqslant\frac{4}{|D|}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-2})
satisfies

(10.14)

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\geqslant C^{\prime}\|T_{\theta}^{\dagger}{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\,|\lambda|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}

for all
g=(g_{p})_{p}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})
with
\Phi_{f,\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(g)\not=0
. If we write
g=\iota\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&\beta\\
0&a\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)k\eta\in\iota({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}})){\mathcal{K}}\eta
, then by (
2.9
), (
2.10
) and (
2.14
),

\|\mathsf{r}(g)^{-1}\xi_{1}\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\leqslant\|\mathsf{r}(\eta^{-1}\iota(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&\beta\\
0&a\bar{\tau}\end{smallmatrix}\right])^{-1})\xi_{1}\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}=|a|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\|\mathsf{r}(\eta)^{-1}\xi_{1}\|_{{\rm{\bf{f}}}}=|a|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\,N

By this, combined with (
10.14
) and Lemma
10.5
,
|\Phi_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(g)|\leqslant|a|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1)}\,|\Phi_{\phi_{0},f}^{({\operatorname{Re}}(s),{\bf 1},{\bf 1})}(k\eta)|
is bounded by the product of
(C^{\prime}\,N^{-1}\|T_{\theta}^{\dagger}{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}h^{-1})\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}\,|\lambda|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}))^{-({\operatorname{Re}}(s)+1)}\times 2^{\omega(N)}\|f_{S}\|_{1}(N{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S}))^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}
and the characteristic function of the set of
(\lambda,X)
satisfying (
10.13
) if
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>-1
. The
X
-integral yields the factor
(\frac{|D|}{2}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S}))^{3}
. ∎

Lemma 10.11
.

For any interval
I\subset(0,l-2)
, the integral (
9.9
) with
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
is bounded by
\exp(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|)
for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
; we have
{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h)
equals

\displaystyle-i^{l-s}\mu_{\infty}(\lambda^{-1}\det h)

\displaystyle 2^{\frac{l}{2}+1}\pi^{l+\frac{3}{2}}|D|^{\frac{1}{2}(s+l-3)}|\lambda|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma(s+1)^{-1}\Gamma(l-s-1)^{-1}

(10.15)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{0}^{1}(1-x)^{s}x^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}J_{l-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\tfrac{\pi x}{|\lambda|}\right)\,\exp\left({-\pi i}{\mathfrak{q}}(h))\right)\,{\rm{d}}x.

Proof.

Let us evaluate the integral
W_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}^{Z}(\mathsf{m}(1_{2},\lambda^{-1})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
for large
\lambda>0
. Let
X\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
and write it as
X=xT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\beta}
with
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\beta\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Set
z:=-{ix}+\frac{2\lambda}{\sqrt{|D|}}
and
\beta_{1}=\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2}\beta
; then, by (
9.10
) followed by (
9.11
) and (
9.12
),
we get

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}^{(s,\mu_{\infty})}(\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}\,\mathsf{n}(X)\,\mathsf{m}(1_{2},\lambda^{-1})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})

\displaystyle=(2^{-1}\sqrt{|D|})^{s-3l+1}\lambda^{l}\left(z^{2}+|\beta_{1}|^{2}\right)^{-l}\left\{(-i)\left(1-\tfrac{{4}{|D|^{-1}}i\,z}{z^{2}+|\beta_{1}|^{2}}\right)\right\}^{s-l+1}.

Set
Z=cT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\gamma}
(
c\in{\mathbb{R}},\,\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}})
. Then,

(10.16)

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2})}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})=(2^{-1}\sqrt{|D|})^{s-3l+1}\lambda^{l}\times ie^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}\lambda}\,\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(z)=\frac{2\lambda}{\sqrt{|D|}}}I(z)e^{\pi iDcz}\,{\rm{d}}z

with

\displaystyle I(z):=\tfrac{|D|}{4}\,\int_{{\mathbb{C}}}\left(z^{2}+|\beta_{1}|^{2}\right)^{-l}\left\{(-i)\left(1-\tfrac{{4}{|D|^{-1}}i\,z}{z^{2}+|\beta_{1}|^{2}}\right)\right\}^{s-l+1}e^{-2\pi i\sqrt{|D|}{\operatorname{Re}}(\bar{\beta}_{1}\gamma))}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta_{1}).

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. It is easy to see that
w:=\frac{{-4}{|D|^{-1}}i\,z}{z^{2}+|\beta_{1}|^{2}}
is in the disc
|w|<\varepsilon
if
{\operatorname{Re}}(z)=\frac{2\lambda}{\sqrt{|D|}}>\frac{4}{\varepsilon|D|}
, or equivalently
\lambda>\frac{2}{\varepsilon\sqrt{|D|}}
. Then,
((-i)(1+w))^{s-l+1}=(-i)^{s-l+1}(1+w)^{s-l+1}
by (
3.2
), and we can expand
(1+w)^{s-l+1}
to the Taylor series in
w
to see that
I(z)
equals

\displaystyle\tfrac{|D|}{4}\,(-i)^{s-l+1}\,\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\tbinom{s-l+1}{n}({-4}{|D|^{-1}}i\,z)^{n}\int_{{\mathbb{C}}}(z^{2}+|\beta_{1}|^{2})^{-(n+l)}e^{-2\pi i\sqrt{|D|}{\operatorname{Re}}(\bar{\beta}_{1}\gamma))}\,{\rm{d}}\eta_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\beta_{1}).

Suppose
\gamma\not=0
. Then, by Lemma
10.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle I(z)=\tfrac{|D|}{4}\,(-i)^{s-l+1}\,2\pi^{l}(\sqrt{|D|}|\gamma|)^{l-1}z^{1-l}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\tbinom{s-l+1}{n}\tfrac{(-2\pi i|D|^{-1/2}|\gamma|)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+n)}K_{n+l-1}(2\pi|D|^{1/2}|\gamma|z).

By this and (
10.16
),
W_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2})}^{Z}(\mathsf{m}(1_{1},\lambda^{-1})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
with large
\lambda>0
equals

\displaystyle(2^{-1}\sqrt{|D|})^{s-3l+1}\lambda^{l}\times ie^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}\lambda}\times\tfrac{|D|}{4}\,(-i)^{s-l+1}\,2\pi^{l}(\sqrt{|D|}|\gamma|)^{l-1}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\tbinom{s-l+1}{n}\tfrac{(-2\pi i|D|^{-1/2}|\gamma|)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+n)}\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(z)=\frac{2\lambda}{\sqrt{|D|}}}z^{1-l}K_{n+l-1}(2\pi|D|^{1/2}|\gamma|z)\,e^{\pi Dcz}\,{\rm{d}}z.

Note the the contour integral is independent of
\lambda
. Now, we use

\displaystyle\int_{{\operatorname{Re}}(z)=\frac{2\lambda}{\sqrt{|D|}}}z^{1-l}K_{s}(2\pi|D|^{1/2}|\gamma|z)\,e^{\pi Dcz}\,{\rm{d}}z

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-\pi i}{\Gamma(l-1)}(\pi|D|^{1/2})^{l-2}|\gamma|^{-s}(\det Z)^{(s+l-2)/2}\,\int_{-1}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{l-2}\left(t+\tfrac{-\sqrt{|D|}\,c}{2\sqrt{\det Z}}\right)^{s-l+1}\,{\rm{d}}t,

which is proved in the proof of Lemma
10.2
. Thus,
W^{Z}_{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
is the product of

\displaystyle(2^{-1}\sqrt{|D|})^{s-3l+1}\lambda^{l}\times ie^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}\lambda}

(10.17)

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{|D|}{4}\,(-i)^{s-l+1}\,2\pi^{l}(\sqrt{|D|}|\gamma|)^{l-1}\times\tfrac{-\pi i}{\Gamma(l-1)}(\pi|D|^{1/2})^{l-2}|\gamma|^{-(l-1)}(\det Z)^{l-3/2}

and

(10.18)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\tbinom{s-l+1}{n}\tfrac{(-2\pi i|D|^{-1/2}|\gamma|)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+n)}(|\gamma|^{-1}\sqrt{\det Z}|)^{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{l-2}\left(t+\tfrac{-\sqrt{|D|}\,c}{2\sqrt{\det Z}}\right)^{n}\,{\rm{d}}t.

The expression in (
10.17
) is easily simplified as

\displaystyle\Gamma(l-1)^{-1}\lambda^{l}e^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}\lambda}\,2^{-s+3l-2}\,\pi^{2l-1}\,|D|^{(s-l)/2}\,i^{l-s}\,(\det Z)^{l-3/2}.

By using the hypergeometric series
{}_{1}F_{1}(a,b;z)
, the expression in (
10.18
) is written as

\displaystyle-{\Gamma(l)}^{-1}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{l-2}{}_{1}F_{1}\left(l-s+1,l;\,{2\pi i}\sqrt{\tfrac{{\det Z}}{|D|}}\left\{t+\tfrac{-c}{2}\sqrt{\tfrac{|D|}{\det Z}}\right\}\right)\,{\rm{d}}t,

which in turn is computed by Lemma
10.12
.
Thus, we end up with the formula

(10.19)

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{m}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}^{Z}(\mathsf{m}(1_{2},\lambda^{-1})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})

\displaystyle=-2^{-s+3l-2}\,\pi^{l+\frac{3}{2}}\,|D|^{\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{3}{2})}\,i^{l-s}\,(\det Z)^{\frac{1}{2}(l-\frac{3}{2})}\,\lambda^{l}e^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}\lambda}\,

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{1}{\Gamma(s+1)\,\Gamma(l-s+1)}\int_{0}^{1}(1-x)^{s}x^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}J_{l-\frac{3}{2}}\left(2\pi\sqrt{\tfrac{\det Z}{|D|}}x\right)\,e^{-\pi icx}\,{\rm{d}}x.

for large
\lambda>0
and for
Z=cT_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\gamma}
such that
\gamma\not=0
,
\det Z>0
. Since the both sides of (
10.19
) are continuous in
Z
, the equality is extended to all
Z\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{R}})
such that
\det Z>0
. By (
9.18
), the factor
\lambda^{l}e^{-2\pi c\sqrt{|D|}\lambda}
equals
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{Z}(\mathsf{m}(1_{2},\lambda^{-1})b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
. Hence by (
9.19
), we obtain the formula of
W_{\mathsf{m}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}}^{Z}(b_{\mathbb{R}}^{\theta})
as on the right-hand side of (
10.19
) with
\lambda=1
being substituted. Applying (
9.20
) and (
9.21
), we are done.
∎

Lemma 10.12
.

For
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>1}
,
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(-1,l-1)}
and
a\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
,
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(10.20)

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{l-2}{}_{1}F_{1}(l-s+1,l;2\pi i(at+b))\,{\rm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pi\Gamma(l)\Gamma(l-1)}{\Gamma(s+1)\Gamma(l-s+1)}(\pi|a|)^{\frac{3}{2}-l}\int_{0}^{1}(1-x)^{s}x^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}J_{l-\frac{3}{2}}(2\pi|a|x)\,e^{2\pi ibx}\,{\rm{d}}x.

Proof.

By the integral representation

\displaystyle{}_{1}F_{1}\left(-s+l-1,l;\,{2\pi i}(at+b)\right)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\Gamma(l)}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)\,\Gamma(s+1)}\,\int_{0}^{1}\exp\left({2\pi i}(at+b)\,x\right)\,x^{-s+l-2}(1-x)^{s}\,{\rm{d}}x,

valid on the region
{\mathcal{T}}_{(-1,l-1)}
(
[
25
, p.274]
), the left-hand side of (
10.20
) equals

\displaystyle\tfrac{\Gamma(l)}{\Gamma(-s+l-1)\,\Gamma(s+1)}\times\int_{0}^{1}\left\{\int_{-1}^{+1}(1-t^{2})^{l-2}\,\exp\left({2\pi i}a\,t\,x\right){\rm{d}}t\right\}\,e^{2\pi ibx}\,x^{-s+l-2}(1-x)^{s}\,{\rm{d}}x.

The
t
-integral, which is absolutely convergent for
l>1
, is evaluated by
[
10
, 3.384.1]
as

\displaystyle 2^{2(l-1)-1}\,B(l-1,l-1)\,e^{{2\pi i}|a|x}\,{}_{1}F_{1}\left(l-1,2l-2;\,{4\pi i}|a|x\right),

where
B(l-1,l-1)=\Gamma(l-1)^{2}\Gamma(2l-2)^{-1}
is the beta function. The hypergeometric function in the formula equals
\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma(l-\rho+1/2)\,\left({\pi|a|x}\right)^{-l+3/2}\,J_{l-3/2}\left(2{\pi x}|a|\right)
by the fifth displayed formula on
[
25
, p.283]
. ∎

By (
10.15
) and Lemma
10.15
, noting
|i^{-s}|=O(\exp(\frac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|)
, we get the bound

(10.21)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{J}}_{l}^{\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2},(s,\mu)}(\lambda,h)\ll\tfrac{\exp(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|)}{|\Gamma(s+1)\Gamma(l-s-1)|}\tfrac{(8\pi\sqrt{|D|})^{l}}{|\Gamma(l-s-1)|}\,|\lambda|^{-l+5}{\mathfrak{q}}(h)^{-2}

for
(\lambda,h)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\times{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
uniform in
l\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 3}
and
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-3)}
. Combining (
10.21
) with Lemma
10.10
, we obtain the following quantity as a uniform majorant of the series in (
9.7
) with
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{n}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger})w_{2}
for
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(1,l-3)}
and
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant l_{1}}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\rm{\bf{f}}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\exp\left(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|\right)(1+|s|)^{2}}{|\Gamma(s+1)|}\tfrac{l^{2}\,(8\pi\sqrt{|D|})^{l}}{|\Gamma(l-s-1)|\Gamma(l-1/2)}\|f_{S}\|_{1}2^{\omega(N)}N^{2{\operatorname{Re}}(s)}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{3{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+4}

\displaystyle\times\Bigl(\sum_{\lambda\in\frac{2}{D}{\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}}\lambda^{{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-l+6}\Bigr)\times\Bigl\{\sum_{\delta\in I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}\|T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\delta^{-1})\|_{\rm{\bf{f}}}^{-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1}{\mathfrak{q}}(\delta_{\infty})^{-2}\Bigr\}.

Note that
\tfrac{\exp\left(\tfrac{\pi}{2}|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|\right)}{|\Gamma(s+1)|}\ll 1
by Stirling’s formula. The
\lambda
-series is convergent and is bounded by
O(({\mathfrak{a}}(f_{S})^{-1}N)^{-l+{\operatorname{Re}}(s)+6})
if
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)<l-7
. The
\delta
-sum is convergent by Lemma
9.4
if
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>1
, because
\|Z\|_{\infty}\asymp\{|c(Z)|^{2}+1\}^{1/2}
for
Z\in\Omega_{D}({\mathbb{R}})
by (
9.24
).

10.4.
An integral involving Bessel function

For
(l,a,b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\times{\mathbb{R}}
, set

{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(s)}(a,b):=\int_{0}^{1}(1-x)^{s}x^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}J_{l-\frac{3}{2}}(2\pi ax)\exp(2\pi ibx)\,{\rm{d}}x,\quad{\operatorname{Re}}(s)>0.

Lemma 10.13
.

Let
{\operatorname{Re}}(s)<l-2
and
b\not=0
. For any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
such that
q\leqslant l-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-1
and
q-1\leqslant{\operatorname{Re}}(s)
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(s)}(a,b)

\displaystyle=(2\pi ib)^{-q}\,\int_{0}^{1}\tfrac{{\rm{d}}^{q}}{{\rm{d}}x^{q}}\bigl\{x^{-(s+1/2)}(1-x)^{s}\,J_{l-3/2}\left(2\pi ax\right)\bigr\}\,\exp\left(2\pi ibx\right)\,{\rm{d}}x.

Proof.

Note
J_{\nu}(x)\sim(x/2)^{\nu}
as
x\rightarrow+0
. By using the formula

(10.22)

\displaystyle J^{\prime}_{\nu}(x)=2^{-1}(J_{\nu-1}(x)-J_{\nu+1}(x))

(
[
25
, §3.1.1(p.67)]
), it is not hard to confirm that for
q\leqslant l-{\operatorname{Re}}(s)-2
and
q\leqslant{\operatorname{Re}}(s)
all the derivatives
(\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}x})^{j}x^{-(s+1/2)}(1-x)^{s}J_{l-3/2}(2\pi ax)
for
j<q
vanish at
x=0
. Thus, a successive application of integration by parts finishes the proof. ∎

By the formula in
[
10
, 8.411 8]
, we have
the uniform bound

(10.23)

\displaystyle|J_{\nu}(x)|\leqslant 2\Gamma(\nu+1)^{-1}(x/2)^{\nu}\quad(x>0,\,\nu>1).

Lemma 10.14
.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\tfrac{{\rm{d}}^{q}}{{\rm{d}}x^{q}}\bigl\{x^{-(s+1/2)}(1-x)^{s}\,J_{l-3/2}(2\pi ax)\bigr\}\right|\ll_{q}(1+|s|)^{q}\tfrac{l^{q}\,a^{l-3/2}(a^{2q}+a^{-q})}{\Gamma(l-1/2)}

holds uniformly for
x\in(0,1)
,
a>0
,
l>2q
,
s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(q-1,l-q-1)}
and
a>0
.

Proof.

By a successive application of the formula (
10.22
), using (
10.23
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\tfrac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}x}\right)^{k}J_{l-3/2}(2\pi ax)\right|

\displaystyle\ll_{k}\max(1,a^{k})\sum_{u=-k}^{k}{a^{l-3/2+u}}\Gamma(l-1/2+u)

\displaystyle\ll_{k}a^{l-3/2}(a^{2q}+a^{-q})\,l^{q}\,\Gamma(l-1/2)^{-1}.

Estimating all the non-negative powers
x
and
1-x
on
(0,1)
bounded trivially by
1
, we get the desired bound by Leibnitz’ rule.
∎

From Lemmas
10.13
and
10.14
, we obtain the following uniform bound of
{{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{(s)}(a,b)
.

Lemma 10.15
.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then,

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{(s)}(a,b)|

\displaystyle\ll_{q}{(1+|{\operatorname{Im}}(s)|)^{q}}\Gamma(l-1/2)^{-1}\,\left|b\right|^{-q}{l^{q}a^{l-3/2}(a^{2q}+a^{-q})}

holds for
l>2q,\,s\in{\mathcal{T}}_{(q-1,l-q-1)},\,a\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}_{+}
and
b\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
.

11.
The
p
-adic local orbital integrals for the main terms

In this section, we abbreviate superscripts in notation for most of the local objects, e.g., write
E
,
\mathfrak{o}
,
\psi
,
\Lambda
,
{\bf K}^{\#}
,
{\bf K}
in place of
E_{p}
,
\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}
,
\psi_{p}
,
\Lambda_{p}
,
{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
,
{\bf K}_{p}
. Let
\mu:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a character with conductor
p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, and
W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu)
its root number (
cf
.
[
20
, §6.3]
). Recall the integral
{\mathscr{W}}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda)}(f;g)
defined by (
6.9
) for
f\in{\mathscr{H}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}))
and
g\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
Let
\delta\in{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
By (
2.7
), the set
I_{\theta}(E^{\times})\backslash{\bf GO}_{T_{\theta}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/I_{\theta}(E^{\times})
is represented by
1_{2}
and
\sigma=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&{\mathtt{t}}\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}\right]
.
For
\delta\in\{1_{2},\sigma\}
and
(\phi,f)\in{\mathcal{S}}(E^{2})\times{\mathscr{H}}(\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}))
, define

(11.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta):=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\int_{{\bf K}^{\#}}

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}(\lambda)|\lambda|_{p}^{-s+1}\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(k^{\#}){{\mathscr{W}}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda^{\delta})}}\left(f;\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)^{-1}\iota(k^{\#})\right)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#},

where
\Lambda^{\delta}:=\Lambda^{-1}
if
\delta=1_{2}
and
\Lambda^{\delta}:=\Lambda
if
\delta=\sigma
. Here is the main result of this section:

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be an unramified extension,
\mu
a ramified character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
of conductor
p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, and

\phi(x,y)=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{p^{e}\mathfrak{o}}(x)\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{1+p^{e}\mathfrak{o}}(y),\quad f=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{b_{p}{\bf K}},\quad\Lambda={\bf 1}\quad\text{with $b_{p}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{e}1_{2}&T_{\theta}^{\dagger}\\
0&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})$}.

Then,

(11.2)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)

\displaystyle=p^{(s-11/2)e}(1+p^{-2})^{-1}\mu\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)\,\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(1)\zeta_{E}(1)W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu),

(11.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\widehat{\phi},f}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta)

\displaystyle=p^{e(-3s-11/2)}(1+p^{-2})^{-1}\mu^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)\,\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(1)\zeta_{E}(1)\,W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu^{-1})^{3}.

Proposition 11.2
.

Let
E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be an unramified extension and

\phi=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\mathfrak{o}\oplus\mathfrak{o}},\quad f=R(\eta)\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})},\quad\Lambda={\bf 1},\quad\mu|{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}={\bf 1}\quad\text{with $\eta:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}{}&{}&{}&{-1}\\
{}&{}&{1}&{}\\
{}&{p}&{}&{}\\
{-p}&{}&{}&{}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})$}.

Then,
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)=\mu(p)\,p^{s-1}.

11.1.
Proof of Proposition
11.1

We prove formula (
11.2
). Note that
p\,(>2)
, is a prime element of
E
, and
e>0
. Set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

\displaystyle:={\bf K}\cap(1_{4}+p^{e}{\bf M}_{4}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})),\quad{\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}):={\bf K}^{\#}\cap(1_{2}+p^{e}{\bf M}_{2}(\mathfrak{o})).

Then we easily have the following.

Lemma 11.3
.

We have
\iota_{\theta}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\subset{\mathcal{U}}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
b_{p}^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})b_{p}\subset{\bf K}
, and

(11.4)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).

Lemma 11.4
.

Set

F(k^{\#}):=\mu(\det k^{\#})\,\,\mu(d\bar{d})^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\mu^{-1}(\lambda)|\lambda|_{p}^{-s+1}\,{\mathscr{W}}^{T_{\theta},\Lambda}(f;\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\iota(k^{\#}))\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda,

viewed as a function in
k^{\#}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}
.

(i)

F(k^{\#})
is constant on
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

(ii)

The value
F(1_{2})
equals

\mu(p^{-e})\,p^{e(s-1)}\,\mu\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\mu(u)\psi(p^{-e}u)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}u.

Proof.

(i) Write
k^{\#}=x^{\#}u^{\#}
with
u^{\#}\in{\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and
x^{\#}\in{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
; then,
F(k^{\#})
is the product of
\mu(\det k^{\#})\,\,\mu(d\bar{d})^{-1}
and the integral

(11.5)

\displaystyle\int\mu(\lambda)^{-1}|\lambda|_{p}^{-s+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}}(b_{p}^{-1}\iota(u^{\#})^{-1}\iota(x^{\#})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X))\,\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))\,{\rm{d}}X\,\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda

with integration domain being
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\times E^{\times}\times{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. By Lemma
11.3
, this is independent of
u^{\#}
. Write
(x^{\#})^{-1}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\tau&0\\
0&\bar{\tau}v\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&\beta\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]
with
\tau\in\mathfrak{o}^{\times}
,
\beta\in\mathfrak{o}
and
v\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
. Since

\displaystyle\iota(x^{\#})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau_{1}\tau)\mathsf{n}(X)^{-1}=\mathsf{m}(\delta,(v^{-1}\lambda)^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X+X_{\beta_{1}})^{-1}

with some
\tau_{1}\in\mathfrak{o}^{\times}
and
\beta_{1}\in\mathfrak{o}
, by the change of variables
X\rightarrow X-X_{\beta^{\prime}}
,
\tau\rightarrow\tau_{1}^{-1}\tau
and
\lambda\rightarrow v\lambda
, the integral (
11.5
) as a function in
x^{\#}
equals its value at
x^{\#}=1_{2}
multiplied with
\mu(v)^{-1}
, because
{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X_{\beta_{1}})=0
. Since
\det k^{\#}\in(1+p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})(\tau\bar{\tau}v)^{-1}
and
d\bar{d}\in(1+p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})(\tau\bar{\tau}v^{2})^{-1}
, we have
\mu(\det k^{\#})\,\,\mu(d\bar{d})^{-1}=\mu(v)
.

(ii) The condition
b_{p}^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X)\in{\bf K}
is equivalent to

\tau\in p^{e}\mathfrak{o}^{\times},\quad\lambda^{-1}\in p^{-e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},\,X-\lambda^{-1}T_{\theta}^{\dagger}{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}\delta^{-1})\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).

Thus,
\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))=\psi(-\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\delta^{-1})\,T_{\theta}^{\dagger}))
, which is equal to
\psi(\lambda^{-1}D/2)
if
\delta=1_{2}
and to
\psi(-\lambda^{-1}D/2)
if
\delta=\sigma
due to
T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(\sigma)=-T_{\theta}
. Thus,
F(1_{2})
equals

\displaystyle\int_{p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\mu^{-1}(\lambda)|\lambda|_{p}^{-s+1}\,\psi(\det\delta\,\lambda^{-1}D/2)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda=p^{e(s-1)}\,\mu(p^{-e})\mu(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2})\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\mu(u)\,\psi\left(\tfrac{u}{p^{e}}\right)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}u.

∎

Proposition 11.5
.

We have
[{\bf K}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]=p^{2e}(1+p^{-2})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)=[{\bf K}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]^{-1}\,\mu\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)\,p^{(s-7/2)e}\,\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(1)\zeta_{E}(1)W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu).

Proof.

By (
6.3
), (
2.34
), and (
11.4
), for
k^{\#}
as above,

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{\phi}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(k^{\#})={\operatorname{vol}}(1+p^{e}\mathfrak{o})\,\mu(\det k^{\#})\,\mu(d\bar{d})\,\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e})}(k^{\#}).

Hence, by Lemma
11.4
, we have
{\mathcal{I}}_{\phi,f}^{(s,\Lambda,\mu)}(\delta)={\operatorname{vol}}(1+p^{e}\mathfrak{o})\,{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\,F(1_{2})
. Note that
{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))=[{\bf K}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e})]^{-1}
and
{\operatorname{vol}}(1+p^{e}\mathfrak{o})=\zeta_{E}(1)p^{-2e}
. Moreover, we use
[
20
, Lemma 6.1]
. ∎

Next, we move to the proof of (
11.3
). Recall
w_{0}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]
.

Lemma 11.6
.

The value
\mathsf{f}_{\widehat{\phi}}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(k^{\#}w_{0})
for
k^{\#}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf K}^{\#}_{p}
equals

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}(D)\mu(p^{2e})p^{2e(-s-1)}\,\mu^{-1}(-\det k^{\#})\,\mu(d\bar{d})\,\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}(d)\int_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}\psi_{E}(p^{-e}u))\,\mu^{-1}(u\bar{u})\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}u.

Proof.

The value
\widehat{\phi}((x,y)w_{0})
is known by (
6.4
). Since
\Lambda
is the trivial character, by (
2.34
), the value
\mathsf{f}_{\widehat{\phi}}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(k^{\#}w_{0})
equals

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}(-\det k^{\#})p^{-4e}\int_{E^{\times}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{p^{-e}\mathfrak{o}}(c\bar{\tau})\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{p^{-e}\mathfrak{o}}(d\bar{\tau})\,\psi_{E}({d\bar{\tau}}{\sqrt{D}}^{-1})\mu_{E}^{-1}(\tau)|\tau\bar{\tau}|_{p}^{-s+1}{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau.

Since
c
and
d
are relatively prime in
\mathfrak{o}
, the condition
c\bar{t}\in p^{-e}\mathfrak{o}_{E,p}
and
d\bar{\tau}\in p^{-e}\mathfrak{o}
is equivalent to
\tau\in p^{-e}\mathfrak{o}
. Set
d=p^{k}d_{0}
with
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}
and
d_{0}\in\mathfrak{o}^{\times}
. Then, since the conductor of
\mu_{E}
is
p^{e}\mathfrak{o}

(e={\rm ord}_{p}(M)>0)
,

\displaystyle\int_{p^{-e}\mathfrak{o}}\psi_{E}({d\bar{\tau}}{\sqrt{D}}^{-1})\mu_{E}^{-1}(\tau)|\tau\bar{\tau}|_{p}^{-s+1}{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\geqslant-e}\mu_{E}^{-1}(-\sqrt{D}\,p^{l})p^{-2l(-s+1)}\int_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}\psi_{E}\left(p^{k+l}d_{0}\bar{u}\right)\mu_{E}^{-1}(u)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}u

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}(D)\sum_{l\geqslant-e}\mu^{-1}(p^{2l})p^{-2l(-s+1)}\times\delta_{-l-k,e}\,\mu_{E}^{-1}(\bar{d}_{0}^{-1})\int_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}\psi_{E}(p^{-e}u)\mu_{E}^{-1}(\bar{u})\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}u

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}(D)\mu(p^{2e})p^{2e(-s+1)}\mu(d\bar{d})\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}(d)\,\int_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}\psi_{E}(p^{-e}u)\mu^{-1}(u\bar{u})\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}u.\qed

Define

F^{*}(k^{\#}):=\mu^{-1}(\det k^{\#})\,\,\mu(d\bar{d})\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\mu(\lambda)|\lambda|_{p}^{s+1}\,{\mathscr{W}}^{T_{\theta},\Lambda^{-1}}(f;\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)^{-1}\iota(k^{\#}w_{0}))\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda,

Then, by Lemma
11.6
,
{\mathcal{I}}_{\widehat{\phi},f}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta)
equals

(11.6)

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}(-D)\,p^{2e(-s-3/2)}\,\zeta_{E}(1)\,W_{E}(\mu_{E}^{-1})

times the integral

(11.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\#}\in{\bf K}^{\#}\\
d\in\mathfrak{o}^{\times}\end{subarray}}F^{*}(k^{\#})\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#}.

The function
F^{*}(k^{\#})
is left
{\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
-invariant and right
{\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
-invariant (
cf
. the proof of Lemma
11.4
). Since
{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\backslash{\bf K}^{\#}={\mathsf{B}}^{\#}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\backslash{\bf K}_{p}^{\#}/{\mathcal{U}}^{\#}(p^{\varepsilon}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is represented by the elements
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]

(\xi\in\mathfrak{o}/p^{e}\mathfrak{o})
and
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1\\
1&p^{j}\eta\end{smallmatrix}\right]

(i\in[1,e],\,\eta\in(\mathfrak{o}/p^{e-j}\mathfrak{o})^{\times})
, the integral in (
11.7
) becomes the sum
[{\bf K}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]^{-1}\sum_{\xi\in\mathfrak{o}/p\mathfrak{o}}F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)
.

Lemma 11.7
.

If
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
, then

F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)=\mu(p^{e})p^{-e(s+1)}\,\mu^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\right)\,\mu\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)\,\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\mu^{-1}(v)\psi(p^{-e}v)\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}v.

If
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, then
F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)=0
.

Proof.

By definition,
F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)
equals

\displaystyle\int\mu(\lambda)|\lambda|_{p}^{s+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}}(b_{p}^{-1}\iota(k^{\#}w_{0})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X))\,\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X)\,{\rm{d}}X\,\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda.

If
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathsf{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
satisfies
g_{2}=g_{1}k\,(\exists k\in{\bf K})
, we write
g_{1}\sim_{{\bf K}}g_{2}
. Then,

(11.8)

\displaystyle\iota\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)\,b_{p}\sim_{{\bf K}_{p}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&0\\
T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger}&p^{e}1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right].

Note that
\det(T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger})=-(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi))
.
If
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
, then
A:=(T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger})^{-1}\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and
B:=-p^{e}A\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
; hence,

\iota(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0})b_{p}\sim_{{\bf K}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&0\\
T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger}&p^{e}1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&B\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1_{2}\\
-1_{2}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&0\\
0&{}^{t}A^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{e}1_{2}&(T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger})^{-1}\\
0&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right].

By this,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}}(b_{p}^{-1}\iota(k^{\#}w_{0})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}}(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{e}1_{2}&(T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger})^{-1}\\
0&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{-1}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\delta I(\tau)&0\\
0&\lambda^{-1}\det\delta{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tau){}^{t}\delta^{-1}{}^{t}I(\tau)^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&X\\
0&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right])

is non-zero if and only if

\displaystyle\tau\in p^{e}\mathfrak{o}^{\times},\quad\lambda\in p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},\quad X+\lambda^{-1}\{(\tfrac{D}{4}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi\frac{\bar{\tau}^{\prime}}{\tau^{\prime}}}){\mathsf{s}}(\delta)\}^{-1}\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

with
\tau^{\prime}
denoting
\tau
or its conjugate for
\delta=1_{2}
or for
\sigma
, respectively. For
X
as above,

\displaystyle\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))

\displaystyle=\psi(-\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}\{(\tfrac{D}{4}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi\frac{\bar{\tau}^{\prime}}{\tau^{\prime}}}){\mathsf{s}}(\delta)\}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\psi(-\det\delta\cdot\lambda^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\right)^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}(T_{\theta}^{\dagger}+X_{\xi\bar{\tau}^{\prime}/\tau^{\prime}})))

\displaystyle=\psi(-\det\delta\cdot\lambda^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\right)^{-1}\tfrac{-D}{2}).

Hence,
F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)
equals

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda\in p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\int_{\tau\in p^{e}\mathfrak{o}}\mu(\lambda)|\lambda|_{p}^{s+1}\psi(\lambda^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\right)^{-1}\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2})\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\lambda.

By a simple change of variable, we obtain the desired expression.

Suppose that
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We can write
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)=p^{m}v
with
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
and
v\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
. Then,
{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tfrac{2}{\sqrt{D}}\xi)\in 1+4p^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
, which yields
\tfrac{2}{\sqrt{D}}\xi\in E^{1}(1+4p^{m}\mathfrak{o})-E^{1}(1+4p^{m+1}\mathfrak{o})
because
E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is unramified. Since
F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)
as a function in
\xi
is
E^{1}
-invariant, we may assume
\frac{2}{\sqrt{D}}\xi=1+4p^{m}y
with
2\sqrt{D}\,y=y_{2}+\theta y_{3}\,(y_{2},y_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Then,

\displaystyle T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]Y,\quad Y:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}y_{3}&y_{2}\\
p^{m}y_{2}&-D/2+p^{m}y_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\quad(y_{1}=-{\mathtt{t}}y_{2}-{\mathtt{N}}y_{3}).

Since
\det(T_{\theta}+X_{\xi}^{\dagger})=-(\frac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi))\in p^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
, we have
\det Y\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
and
Y\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, i.e.,
Y\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. The last element in (
11.8
) equals

\displaystyle\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p{-m}&{}&{}&{}\\
{}&1&{}&{}\\
{}&{}&{p^{m}}&{}\\
{}&{}&{}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m}&{0}&{0}&{0}\\
{0}&{1}&{0}&{0}\\
y_{3}&y_{2}&-p^{e-m}&0\\
p^{m}y_{2}&-D/2+p^{m}y_{1}&0&-p^{m}\end{smallmatrix}\right],

which turns out to be
{\bf K}
-equivalent to
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{e}(T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi})^{-1}&1_{2}\\
0&T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi}\end{smallmatrix}\right].
Thus,

(11.9)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}}(b_{p}^{-1}\iota(k^{\#}w_{0})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)\mathsf{m}_{\theta}(\tau)\mathsf{n}(X))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}}(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{e}(T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi})^{-1}&1_{2}\\
0&T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{-1}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\delta I(\tau)&0\\
0&\lambda^{-1}\det\delta{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tau){}^{t}\delta^{-1}{}^{t}I(\tau)^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1_{2}&X\\
0&1_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right])

and this value is zero unless
p^{-e}(T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}_{\xi})\delta I(\tau)\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Since
T_{\theta}+X^{\dagger}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m}&1\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]Y
and
Y\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, the last condition implies
p^{-e}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m}&1\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]I(\tau^{\prime})=k
for some
k=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}k_{11}&k_{12}\\
k_{21}&k_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Set
p^{-e}I(\tau^{\prime})=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&-b{\mathtt{N}}\\
b&a+{\mathtt{t}}b\end{smallmatrix}\right]
with
a,b\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Then,

\displaystyle a=p^{-m}k_{11},\quad b=k_{21},\quad b{\mathtt{N}}=-p^{-m}k_{12},\quad a+{\mathtt{t}}b=k_{22},

which together with
(k_{11},k_{12})_{\mathfrak{o}}=\mathfrak{o}
and
(k_{21},k_{22})_{\mathfrak{o}}=\mathfrak{o}
yields

(a,b)_{\mathfrak{o}}=(k_{21},k_{22})_{\mathfrak{o}}=\mathfrak{o}\subset p^{-m}\mathfrak{o}=(p^{-m}k_{11},p^{-m}k_{12})_{\mathfrak{o}}=(a,b{\mathtt{N}})_{\mathfrak{o}}\subset(a,b)_{\mathfrak{o}},

a contradiction for
m>0
. Hence, the value (
11.9
) is zero, which in turn yields the vanishing of
F^{*}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]w_{0}\right)
.
∎

Lemma 11.8
.

(
cf.
[
20
, Lemmas 6.2 and 6.4]
)

We have

W_{E}(\mu^{-1}_{E})=(-1)^{e}W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu^{-1})^{2}
,
W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu^{-1})W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu)=\mu(-1)
, and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathfrak{o}/p\mathfrak{o}\\
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\end{subarray}}\mu^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\right)=(-1)^{e}\mu^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}\right)\,p^{e}.

Proposition 11.9
.

We have that
{\mathcal{I}}_{\widehat{\phi}}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta)
equals

\displaystyle[{\bf K}^{\#}:{\bf K}^{\#}_{0}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]^{-1}\mu^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)\,\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(1)\zeta_{E}(1)p^{e(-3s-7/2)}W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu^{-1})^{3}.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.7
, the integral (
11.7
) becomes

\displaystyle[{\bf K}^{\#}:{\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p^{e}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})]^{-1}\,p^{-e(s+3/2)}\,\mu\left(\tfrac{D\det\delta}{2}\right)\,\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(1)\,W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\mu^{-1})\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathfrak{o}/p\mathfrak{o}\\
\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\end{subarray}}\mu^{-1}\left(\tfrac{D}{4}+{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\xi)\right).

As a product of this expression and (
11.6
), we obtain an evaluation of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\widehat{\phi}}^{(-s,\Lambda^{-1},\mu^{-1})}(\delta)
. Then, we use Lemma
11.8
to get the desired formula.
∎

11.2.
The Proof of Proposition
11.2

The value

(11.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf K}^{\#}}{\mathscr{W}}^{(T_{\theta},\Lambda)}(f;\mathsf{m}(\delta,\lambda^{-1}\det\delta)^{-1}\iota(k^{\#}))\,{\rm{d}}k^{\#}

is given by the integral of

\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\left(\iota(k^{\#})^{-1}\mathsf{m}(\lambda\delta I_{\theta}(\tau),\det\delta{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tau))\,\mathsf{n}(X)\eta\right)\,\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))

over
(\tau,X)\in E^{\times}\times{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Since
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]
and
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
-\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\,(\xi\in\mathfrak{o}/q\mathfrak{o})
yield a complete set of representatives of
{\bf K}^{\#}/{\bf K}^{\#}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, noting that
{\operatorname{vol}}({\bf K}_{0}^{\#}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))=\frac{1}{p^{2}+1}
, we have that (
11.10
) becomes the sum of the following terms:

(11.11)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{p^{2}+1}\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}{\rm{d}}X\int_{E^{\times}}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\,\sum_{\xi\in\mathfrak{o}/p\mathfrak{o}}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\left(\iota(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right])\mathsf{m}(\lambda\delta I_{\theta}(\tau),\det\delta{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tau))\mathsf{n}(X)\eta\right)\,\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X)),

(11.12)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{p^{2}+1}\int_{{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}{\rm{d}}X\int_{E^{\times}}\,{\rm{d}}^{\times}\tau\,\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\left(\iota(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right])\mathsf{m}(\lambda I_{\theta}(\tau),\det\delta{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tau))\mathsf{n}(X)\eta\right)\,\psi(-{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X)).

We have

\displaystyle\iota(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
\bar{\xi}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right])\mathsf{m}(\lambda\delta I_{\theta}(\tau),\det\delta{\operatorname{N}}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\tau))\mathsf{n}(X)\eta

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{matrix}\lambda h&\lambda hX\\
-\lambda X_{\xi}^{\dagger}h&-\lambda X_{\xi}^{\dagger}hX+{}^{t}h^{-1}\det h\end{matrix}\right]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}{}&{}&{}&{-1}\\
{}&{}&{1}&{}\\
{}&{p}&{}&{}\\
{-p}&{}&{}&{}\end{smallmatrix}\right]

with
h=\delta I_{\theta}(\tau)
belongs to
{\bf K}_{0}(p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
if and only if

(11.13)

\displaystyle\lambda hX\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&p\\
-p&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

(11.14)

\displaystyle\lambda h\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

(11.15)

\displaystyle(-\lambda X^{\dagger}_{\xi}hX+{}^{t}h\det h)\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&p\\
-p&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in p\,{\bf M}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

(11.16)

\displaystyle\lambda X_{\xi}^{\dagger}h\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

(11.17)

\displaystyle\lambda p\det h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}.

By taking the determinant of (
11.16
), we have
\lambda^{2}\det h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
, which together with (
11.17
) yields
\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
; then, by (
11.16
),
h\in p^{-1}{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, which is equivalent to
I_{\theta}(p\tau)\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, and (
11.14
) is satisfied. From
I_{\theta}(p\tau)\in{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, we get
p\tau\in\mathfrak{o}^{\times}
. Condition (
11.13
) is equivalent to
X\in p^{-1}{\bf GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Condition (
11.15
) is equivalent to
X-\lambda^{-1}X_{\xi}{\mathsf{s}}({}^{t}I_{\theta}(\tau))\in{\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
which yields
\psi({\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X))=\psi(\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}{\mathsf{s}}(I_{\theta}(\tau))\,X_{\xi})=\psi(\lambda^{-1}{\operatorname{tr}}(T_{\theta}X_{\xi}))=1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\eqref{LocalSingIdq-f00}=\tfrac{1}{p^{2}+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\,{\operatorname{vol}}_{E^{\times}}(p^{-1}\mathfrak{o}^{\times})\,{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\,\cdot\#(\mathfrak{o}/p\mathfrak{o})=\tfrac{p^{2}}{p^{2}+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}.

In the same manner, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{LocalSingIdq-f1}=\tfrac{1}{p^{2}+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\,{\operatorname{vol}}(p^{-1}\mathfrak{o}^{\times})\,{\operatorname{vol}}({\mathsf{V}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))=\tfrac{1}{p^{2}+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}.

Thus, (
11.10
) is equal to

\tfrac{p^{2}}{p^{2}+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}+\tfrac{1}{p^{2}+1}\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}=\operatorname{\mbox{{\rm 1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{{\rm l}}}_{\lambda\in p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}
. ∎
